# CODEMAP

Map of the code as it stands. Written for engineers and agents who extend it; update it in the same change as the code. The requirements are in `SQL Playground Build Spec.md`, which is kept next to the working copy on purpose and is not part of the repository (so it is not deployed either); `README.md` is for the instructor, the marker and a developer picking the repository up.

The site is a static, browser-only SQL playground based on SQLime (nalgeon/sqlime): SQLite runs as WebAssembly, none of its code is built at deploy (the two parts that are built, `vendor/editor.js` and `bundle/`, are committed), and the repository root is the site. Upstream's execution core is kept; everything added lives in `js/lab/` (and `css/`), and `js/index.js`, which is rewritten (see below), reaches it through the named seams of section 2.

**What differs from upstream.** `js/sqlite/` differs from upstream only by the removed gist code, the one `runSql` call in `fromSql` and one line in `create()` (both in `manager.js`) that puts the engine's memory on the wrapper as `database.wasm`; nothing else in it was touched. That line is the one exception to "do not edit `js/sqlite/`" (section 15): the table export reads each text as its bytes (9.7), and the engine's own object is private to `manager.js`, so no other file can reach its memory; only `manager.js` can hand it on. `js/index.js` is substantially rewritten, not lightly modified: 919 lines added and 213 removed against upstream's file on Nov 18, 2026 (`git diff --numstat upstream/main -- js/index.js`, working tree; the remote `upstream` is https://github.com/nalgeon/sqlime.git, fetched first). Its `execute()` goes through `runSql` (`js/lab/runner.js`) instead of `SQLite.execute`, its load flow (load tickets, `openCurrentUrl`, `startFromDataset`; the demo database and gist saving are gone) is new, it carries the seams of section 2, it wires the app shell of the UI plan's Phase 2 (top bar, sidebar tabs, menus, pane sizes, status bar), and since Phase 4 it drives the results pane through a load (its loading state, a failure that keeps the view) and lets a run wait one frame (4.2). Merging upstream changes to it will conflict and has to be done by hand. Other upstream JavaScript files changed too (added/removed lines): `js/components/sqlime-editor.js` 227/11 (it draws CodeMirror inside itself since the UI plan's Phase 3, section 4.5); `js/db-path.js` 28/15; `js/storage.js` 33/3; `js/printer.js` 14/8 (upstream's printer with escaping added (`escapeHtml`) and BLOB cells shown as `x'<hex>'`, section 8). Upstream's `<command-bar>`, `<tool-bar>`, `<db-name>` and `<copy-on-click>` components (`js/components/command-bar.js`, `toolbar.js`, `db-name.js`, `copy-link.js`) are deleted: the app shell replaced them. The UI plan's Phase 4 deleted `<sqlime-status>` (`sqlime-status.js`, with `css/status.css`) and `action-button.js` too: the results pane (`js/lab/results.js`) and the toasts (`js/lab/toast.js`) replaced the status line, and the empty-database welcome's About button is a plain link. `js/components/` keeps `sqlime-editor.js` and `sqlime-result.js`. `js/controllers/` is untouched. Outside `js/`, upstream's `index.html` (rewritten for the shell: 272 lines added and 52 removed, `git diff --numstat upstream/main -- index.html`), `about.html`, `README.md`, `Makefile`, `.gitignore`, most of `css/` and the icons in `img/` are changed as well, so merging those can conflict too. Removed from upstream: `js/github.js`, `js/settings.js`, `js/sqlite/dumper.js` and the gist code in `js/sqlite/`, `css/layout.css`, `header.css`, `toolbar.css` and `commandbar.css`, its test page, its deploy workflow, `CNAME`, the demo files, `settings.html` and `img/cover.png`.

References are `file` plus function name, since line numbers drift. The Playwright tests in `tests/` pin most behaviour described here (section 11); a statement with a test name after it is checked there.

## 1. Layout

| Path | What it is |
|---|---|
| `index.html` | The playground, static markup only (the components and modules fill it). A skip link (`.skip-link`), then `div.app#app`, a grid of three rows as high as the window. `header.topbar`: the Sidebar button (`#sidebar-button`, shown under 1100 px: it opens the sidebar's drawer), the brand (`h1 > a#reset`, the link to `./`, with an inline SVG mark), the database switcher (`#database-button`, its menu `#database-menu`), the hidden `#open-file-input`, and on the right `.topbar-actions`: Copy link (`#copy-link`, `data-action="copyLink"`), the Export menu (`#export-button`, `#export-menu`), the Theme button (`js/lab/theme.js` mounts it into `.topbar-theme`) and Help (`#help-button`, `#help-popover`); then More (`#more-button`, its menu `#more-menu`, shown under 700 px, where it holds the actions). `div.workbench`: `nav#sidebar` (`.sidebar-head`: the tablist `.tabs` with `#tab-schema`, `#tab-exercises`, `#tab-history`, and the drawer's Close button `#sidebar-close`; the tabs' panels `#panel-schema` holding `#schema`, `#panel-exercises` holding `.exercise-set`, `#panel-history` holding `#run-history-list`), the vertical splitter `#sidebar-splitter`, and `main#main` (`data-view`, "editor" or "result": the view a phone shows): the view buttons `#view-switch` (`#view-editor`, `#view-result`, under 700 px), the exercise prompt `#exercise-panel` (hidden until a set opens: `#exercise-prompt`, then `.exercise-controls` with `#exercise-hint-button` and the group `#exercise-nav` of `#exercise-previous` and `#exercise-next`, then `#exercise-hints` and the live region `#exercise-announce`), `#editor-toolbar` (Run `#execute`, disabled in the markup until a database opens, as the old command bar was; Check answer is added beside it in exercise mode), `#editor-pane` (`#editor`, `#editor-hint`), the horizontal splitter `#editor-splitter`, and `#results-pane` (`#exercise-feedback`, the feedback banner of a Check, not a live region, `#results-summary` (hidden but for a result) holding `#status` (the summary line's text, a plain `<p>`) and the Download CSV button, `#result`, `#result-scroll-note`, the long-cell popover `#cell-popover` and its hint `#cell-popover-hint`; `js/lab/results.js` adds the live region `#results-announce`), and last the text size, `.text-size` (`#text-smaller`, `#text-larger` and the live region `#text-size-said`: drawn at the right end of the toolbar's row, but after the result in the Tab order, so the editor's way in and out by Shift+Tab and Tab stays as it was). `footer.statusbar` (`#status-database`, `#status-run`, `#status-version`, the credit link to About). After `.app`, the toast region `#toast-region` (a polite live region, there from the start). Copy link's fallback popover `#copy-link-popover` sits in the top bar after `#copy-link`. The shell is described in 4.4, the results pane in section 8. The page links one stylesheet, `bundle/app.css`, and runs one module, `bundle/app.js`, in its `<head>`: its stylesheets of `css/` and its modules of `js/`, one file each (`bundle/`, below); its classic scripts and the order everything runs in are in section 2. |
| `about.html` | The student-facing About page: what the site offers, what is remembered, shortcuts, credit. Styled by `css/article.css` (the app's look since the UI plan's Phase 6: a brand bar, one column of text, the credit in a bar at the foot); scripts are `js/lab/brand.js` and `js/lab/static-page.js` (the way back, the platform's keys). |
| `verify.html` | The marker's page (F11): paste, drop or choose an evidence file, read VALID or EDITED. Styled by `css/article.css` + `css/verify.css`; scripts are `js/lab/brand.js` and `js/lab/verify-page.js` only (no engine, no lz-string, no storage, no request after load). Nothing in the app links to it on purpose; the marker bookmarks `<site>/verify.html`. |
| `js/` | Application code, vanilla ES modules: the source. `index.html` runs the ones it uses as one file, `bundle/app.js`, built from here by `npm run build:app`; `about.html` and `verify.html` load theirs from here directly. A test reaches a module of the app's page through `bundle/app.js`, which exports the ones the tests use (`bundle/`, below). `js/index.js` the page controller (upstream's file, substantially rewritten: see "What differs from upstream" above); `js/components/` custom elements (upstream's `sqlime-*` with small edits, and `action-button.js`); `js/controllers/` click and shortcut delegation (upstream's, untouched); `js/sqlite/` the engine front door (upstream's; differs only by the removed gist code, the one `runSql` call in `fromSql` and the line in `create()` that gives the wrapper `database.wasm`); `js/lab/` everything added. Loose files: `db-path.js`, `storage.js`, `printer.js` (upstream's, edited). |
| `css/` | One stylesheet per region. `index.html` loads its sixteen joined in one file, `bundle/app.css`, in the order of the cascade: `tokens.css`, `fonts.css`, `core.css`, `buttons.css`, `shell.css` and `menu.css`, which apply to the whole page, then each region's, from the top of the page down: `share.css` (Copy link's popover), `schema.css`, `exercises.css`, `run-log.css`, `editor.css`, `result.css` (the results pane, section 8), `evidence.css` (the three dialogs), `import.css` (the import's dialog and report, sections 9.6 and 9.8), `export-tables.css` (the table export's dialog, section 9.7) and `toast.css`. `about.html` and `verify.html` link `tokens.css`, `fonts.css` and `article.css` (and `verify.css`, the latter) directly. `core.css` holds the element reset, the focus ring (`:focus-visible`), `.icon`, `.hidden`, `.visually-hidden`; `buttons.css` the `.button` family (`--primary`, `--quiet`, `--small`); `shell.css` the grid, top bar, Help popover, sidebar, tabs, splitters, the main area, the status bar, the text size's place, the drawer under 1100 px and the phone's layout under 700 px (4.4); `menu.css` every menu (`js/lab/menu.js`), including the two-line items of the database switcher and Export, and the `menu-fade` animation the popovers and toasts share; `evidence.css` the three dialogs (the database export, the evidence export and Open from URL, all `.app-dialog`), placed 12% of the window's height down (32 to 128 px) rather than centred, so a refusal shown inline makes a dialog taller downward and the field being typed in stays where it is. `article.css` serves the static pages (About and the evidence check page share it). **`tokens.css` is the only place a colour is written** (the UI plan's tokens, section 3 of `docs/design/SQL Lab UI and UX Plan.md`): the light values are on `:root`, the dark ones in two identical blocks, one for a dark device without `data-theme="light"` and one for `data-theme="dark"` (plain values, not `light-dark()`, so every browser with custom properties keeps its colours; a test compares the two blocks). The tokens are the plan's colours (`--desk`, `--paper`, `--sunken`, `--rule`, `--rule-strong`, `--ink`, `--graphite`, `--accent` and its hover, subtle and on-accent forms, `--success`, `--danger`, `--warning` and their subtle forms, the `--syntax-*` colours, `--shadow-float`, `--scrim`) and its type, space and shape steps (`--font-ui`, `--font-mono`, `--text-xs` to `--text-lg`, `--space-1` to `--space-6`, `--radius-control`, `--radius-float`, `--control-height`, `--focus-ring`, `--motion-fade`); the shell and the redesigned components are drawn with them. Weights are 400 and 600 only. `tests/ui1-tokens.spec.js` fails on a raw colour (hex, `rgb()`, `hsl()` and the like, or a colour name in CSS) anywhere else in the site's CSS or JS, checks every pairing the interface uses for 4.5:1 (text) or 3:1 (outlines) in both themes, and fails on a `var()` that nothing defines (`--sidebar-width` and `--editor-height`, which `js/lab/layout.js` sets from script, are allowed). The only `url()`s are the vendored typefaces in `fonts.css` (relative; no external fonts or images). |
| `vendor/` | `sqlite3.js` + `sqlite3.wasm` (official SQLite WASM 3.54.1, committed as plain files; `make sqlite` refreshes them after editing `SQLITE_YEAR` / `SQLITE_VERSION` in the `Makefile`), `lz-string.min.js` + `lz-string.LICENSE.txt` (lz-string 1.5.0, MIT; defines the global `LZString`, used only by `js/lab/share.js`), `papaparse.min.js` + `papaparse.LICENSE.txt` (Papa Parse 5.7.1, MIT: the publisher's own minified file, unchanged, as the npm package holds it; it defines the global `Papa`; not named by `index.html` and not in the bundle: `js/lab/import.js` asks `loadVendor` for it the first time a CSV file is previewed, section 9.6), `fflate.min.js` + `fflate.LICENSE.txt` (fflate 0.8.3, MIT, Arjun Barrett: the publisher's own `umd/index.js`, unchanged, 33,044 bytes; it defines the global `fflate`; not named by `index.html` and not in the bundle: `js/lab/export-tables.js` asks `loadVendor` for it when Export is pressed with two tables or more, section 9.7), `xlsx.full.min.js` + `xlsx.LICENSE.txt` (SheetJS 0.20.3, Apache-2.0, SheetJS LLC: the publisher's own full build from its own distribution, not the npm registry's copy, unchanged, 951,904 bytes; it defines the global `XLSX`; not named by `index.html` and not in the bundle: `js/lab/import.js` hands the dialog a `loadVendor` for it, and it is fetched when the reading of a workbook begins, section 9.8), `fonts/` (Atkinson Hyperlegible Next for the interface and Atkinson Hyperlegible Mono for SQL and data, variable weight, latin and latin-ext, upright and italic: eight `woff2` files, 169,880 bytes, SIL Open Font License, from `@fontsource-variable` 5.3.0 by `node tools/vendor-fonts.mjs`, which also writes `css/fonts.css`), `lucide.LICENSE.txt` (the icons, ISC), and `editor.js` + `codemirror.LICENSE.txt` (CodeMirror 6 for the SQL editor, about 390 KB minified, 130 KB compressed: the library only, an ES module that exports the CodeMirror pieces `js/lab/code-editor.js` uses; built by `npm run build:editor` (`tools/build-editor.mjs`, esbuild) from the exact versions `package.json` pins, section 4.5; the licence file holds the MIT licences of the fourteen bundled packages). |
| `bundle/` | `app.js` and `app.css`: the modules and the stylesheets `index.html` uses, one file each, so that a first visit asks for 18 files with three exercise sets (20 in Firefox; section 11 has the count and what it is made of) instead of 85 (87) and more: students opening the site at once from one school network are one burst from one address, which GitHub Pages can answer with "429 Rate limit exceeded". Built by `npm run build:app` (`tools/build-app.mjs`: esbuild for the script, ESM and not minified, from the four module scripts the page ran (`js/lab/brand.js`, `js/components/sqlime-editor.js`, `js/components/sqlime-result.js`, `js/index.js`, in that order) and what they import; the stylesheets joined as they are, each under a comment with its name) and committed; `node tools/build-app.mjs --check` fails while they differ from a fresh build (`tests/app-bundle.spec.js`). `app.js` also exports, by name, the twenty-one modules the tests reach in the page (`EXPOSE` in the tool: `checker`, `datasets`, `dbPath`, `events`, `exercises`, `flags`, `import`, `importCells`, `importDb`, `importReport`, `importWorkbook`, `importZip`, `manager`, `progress`, `results`, `router`, `runLog`, `runner`, `share`, `storage`, `vendorLoader`), so `import("./bundle/app.js")` in a test returns the very instances the page runs, state included, as importing a module of `js/` did before the bundle; that import would now load a second, separate copy, and a test refuses it. Each exported module must be one the page's scripts import statically (the build checks), so the exports bring in no module and change no order. The one import it leaves out is `vendor/editor.js`, which stays a file of its own (the plain editor takes over when it cannot load). The build also fails on a stylesheet of `css/` that no page uses (`index.html` through `STYLES`, any other root page by linking it), an `@import` or `@charset`, a `url()` whose address does not start with `../` (the same from `bundle/` as from `css/`), `data:` or `#`, or an `image-set()` or `src()` (whose addresses it does not check), each in any letter case and outside comments; an import from `vendor/` other than the editor; or an argument other than `--check`. It reads the stylesheets (dropping a byte order mark), and compares the committed files, with `\n` line breaks, so a checkout with `\r\n` (Git on Windows) builds and checks the same. The deploy publishes `bundle/` as committed and checks nothing (section 10). Never edit by hand. |
| `img/` | The favicon set, all written by `tools/build-favicons.mjs` from the brand mark and the tokens: `favicon.svg` (the mark; it follows the colour scheme, and is the logo of About and the evidence check page; the top bar draws its mark as an inline SVG), `favicon.ico` (16, 32 and 48 px), `favicon-64x64.png`, `apple-touch-icon.png` (180 px), `icon-192.png`, `icon-512.png` and `icon-maskable-512.png` (the web manifest's), and `social.png` (1200×630, the picture of a shared link's preview, with the app's name drawn in it). |
| `datasets/` | `manifest.json`, `northlight_retail.db`, `home_store.db`, `shopping.db`, `generate_db.py` (the owner's files; do not edit). Section 6.3. |
| `exercises/` | One JSON file per exercise set (`home-store-basics.json`, `retail-basics.json`, `shopping-basics.json`, the owner's). Section 7. |
| `tests/` | Playwright acceptance tests (dev only). Section 11. |
| `tools/` | Dev scripts, not deployed: `vendor-fonts.mjs` (the typefaces into `vendor/fonts/` and `css/fonts.css`), `build-icons.mjs` (the icon sprite in `index.html`), `ui-matrix.mjs` (the UI plan's screenshot matrix; `--ui shell` takes the recipes of the app shell, the default `legacy` the interface before the redesign; full-page shots, the phone's taken as a touch screen with its pointer and hover media rules pinned first, as Chromium leaves mobile emulation to draw past the window), `build-editor.mjs` and `editor-entry.js` (the CodeMirror bundle, `vendor/editor.js`: one of the site's two build steps, section 4.5), `build-app.mjs` (the other: `bundle/app.js` and `bundle/app.css` from `js/` and `css/`; run it after changing them), `build-favicons.mjs` (the favicon set and the preview picture in `img/`, drawn by Playwright's Chromium; run after changing the mark or `APP_NAME`), and `site-meta.mjs` (the one tool the deploy runs: the link preview's tags and the web manifest, written into the staged copy, section 10). |
| `docs/` | Not deployed. `docs/design/` is the owner's UI plan and mockups (the mockups load Google Fonts, which is why the folder must never ship); `docs/ui/` holds the screenshot matrices (`before/`, `after/phase-N/`) and `UI_INVENTORY.md` (every interactive element of the interface before the redesign, its handler, the tests that pin it, and its home in the new design). |
| `.github/workflows/pages.yml`, `.nojekyll`, `Makefile`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `playwright.config.js`, `.gitignore` | Deploy and tooling, section 10. |
| `README.md`, `CODEMAP.md`, `LICENSE` | Docs and licence (MIT, upstream copyright kept). All are deployed with the site. |

## 2. Start-up and the page controller

`index.html` runs, in order: the classic `js/lab/theme-boot.js` (first in `<head>`, on every page: it puts the stored theme on `<html>` before the first paint); then, at the end of `<body>`, the **classic** scripts `js/lab/storage-guard.js`, `js/lab/engine-guard.js`, `vendor/sqlite3.js` (defines `sqlite3InitModule`, does not start the engine) and `vendor/lz-string.min.js`; then, once the page is parsed, its one module, `bundle/app.js` (in the `<head>`, so that it is fetched early; a module waits for the parse wherever it sits), which runs the page's modules in the order the page ran them as four scripts before they were bundled: `js/lab/brand.js` (writes `APP_NAME` into the title and every `[data-app-name]`), then `components/sqlime-editor.js` and `sqlime-result.js` (each defines its element, which upgrades; the rest of the page is static markup; the editor element starts loading `vendor/editor.js` at once, which a `<link rel="modulepreload">` in the `<head>` has already asked for, section 4.5); and last `js/index.js`, which builds the `ui` handles (`buttons.{reset, execute}`, `topbar`, `toolbar` = `#editor-toolbar`, `fileInput` = `#open-file-input`, `editor`, `status` = `#status` (the summary line's text), `summary` = `#results-summary`, `pane` = `#results-pane`, `result`) and the `actions` (`executeCurrent`, `copyLink`, `exportDatabase`) / `shortcuts` (`o`, `u`) maps, exposes `window.app = {actions, ui, get database, whenIdle()}` (`whenIdle()` resolves once no run is waiting or running: a Run starts one frame after it is asked for, section 4.2; tests wait on it), mounts the lab modules, registers listeners and ends with `startFromCurrentUrl()`. Order dependencies: `index.js` uses `ui.editor` at once, so the element modules must have run (module order); a mount that needs another's result comes after it (`mountRunLog` before `mountEvidence`, `mountResults` and `mountEvidence` before `mountExportMenu`; `mountTableExport` before both menus, which are given its entry; the state of the long jobs, `runningJob`, `jobMessage` and `addressAtJob`, before the import and the export are mounted, since each one's `onBusy` writes it and a `let` read by a closure before its declaration throws, section 9.7); `mountExercises` comes before `mountResults` but calls it only later, through `showIdle` and `modeChanged`; and `mountTabs` calls its `onShow` once while mounting, before `runLog` exists, which is why `index.js` reads `runLog?.refresh()`. Being one module, the four also fail as one: an exception at the top level of any of them, or of a module they import statically, stops everything after it, `js/index.js` and so the whole page included, where as four scripts a failing element module (`sqlime-editor.js`, `sqlime-result.js`) stopped only itself (`brand.js` stopped `js/index.js` too, which imports it). `js/lab/code-editor.js`, which the editor element imports with `import()`, still fails alone: the editor stays the plain box. None throws at its top level today (`js/index.js` would, on an element missing from the markup); keep it so.

`js/index.js` holds the current database (`let database`, module-private) and owns load, execute, rename (from the export dialog) and rendering of the status. It reaches `js/lab/` only at these seams:

| Seam | Module | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `startFromCurrentUrl`, `openCurrentUrl` | `router.js` | `resolveRoute(window.location)` decides what to open (section 6.1). |
| `execute` | `runner.js` | `runSql(run, sql)` is the one way user SQL runs (section 5). |
| `showResult`, `showError`, `errorText`, `results.*` | `results.js` | The results pane (section 8): `mountResults({ui, pane, summary, getDatabase, getSamples, isExercise})` -> `{showResult, showReport, showError, showProblem, showEmpty, refreshEmpty, showLoading, loadFailed, loadCancelled, startRun, say, canDownload, download}`; `showResult` and `showError` are one-line delegates, and the load flow calls `showLoading`, `showEmpty`, `loadFailed` and `showProblem` directly. `getSamples` is `samplesFor(openDataset, database)` (`samples.js`), `isExercise` is `lab.isOpen()`. |
| `mountDatabaseSwitcher` | `database-switcher.js` | The top bar's database button and menu (4.4); `index.js` names the controls it returns `picker` (`{setManifest, sync, rename}`). Its `onSelect` is `startFromDataset`, `onOpenFile` clicks `ui.fileInput`, `onOpenUrl` opens the Open from URL dialog. |
| `mountOpenUrlDialog` | `open-url-dialog.js` | "Open from URL…" (and Ctrl/Cmd+U): `mountOpenUrlDialog({onOpen})` -> `{open(returnFocus), isOpen()}`; `onOpen` is `startFromUrl`, and `isOpen` is what `openUrl` asks first: Ctrl/Cmd+U in the dialog itself goes to its field, and under any other dialog the key does nothing. |
| `mountTableExport` | `export-tables.js` | Export of tables as CSV, behind the flag `export` (on by default; 9.7): `mountTableExport({returnFocus: exportReturn, getDatabase, isLoading, otherJobMessage, runLog, onBusy})` -> `{enabled, open()}`; `open` is the menu entry's `onSelect`, and `index.js` builds the entry (`EXPORT_TABLES_MENU_TEXT`, `EXPORT_TABLES_MENU_DESCRIPTION`) only when `enabled`. Its `onBusy` and its `otherJobMessage` come from `longJob("export", EXPORT_TABLES_BUSY_MESSAGE)` (`index.js`, 9.7): the state of the long jobs is `index.js`'s own (`runningJob`, `jobMessage`, `addressAtJob`, `jobRunning()`, `refusedWhileBusy()`, `jobInTheWay()`), shared with the import's `onBusy`. |
| `toast` | `toast.js` | `toast(message, {tone})`: the answer of an action (section 8). `index.js` uses it for a database the student opened (`openedMessage`), a Run refused while loading, and the export dialog's refusals and the busy jobs' (`refusedWhileBusy`); `copy-link.js`, `export.js`, `export-tables.js`, `evidence-dialog.js`, `results.js` (the CSV) and `run-log.js` (a restored query) import it. |
| `mountThemeMenu`, `mountHelp` | `theme.js`, `help.js` | The Theme button and its menu go into `.topbar-theme` (`buttonClass: "button button--quiet"`); the Help button and its popover (4.4). |
| `mountTabs` | `tabs.js` | The sidebar's tabs; returns `{show(id), current()}`. `onShow` redraws the History tab. |
| `mountExercises`, `createSetActions` | `exercise-panel.js`, `set-actions.js` | Exercise mode: `mountExercises({pickerSlot, ui, execute, isLoading, openSet, closeSet, showTab, showIdle, modeChanged, say})` (section 7.4; `execute` is `execute(sql, {quiet: true})`: a Check's run is shown but not said, the verdict is, through `say`, which is `results.say`; `showIdle` is `results.showEmpty`, `modeChanged` `results.refreshEmpty`); the set picker's decisions live in `set-actions.js`, which gets only primitives (`hasDatabase`, `isLoading`, `cancelLoad`, `loadDataset`, `pushUrl`, `showError` = `results.loadFailed`, `showInvite` = `results.loadCancelled`). `start()` takes `exercise` ({set, exerciseId}) and asks `lab.editorSql(exercise, sql)` for the editor text. |
| `mountSchema`, `mountResultScroll` | `schema.js`, `result-scroll.js` | `mountSchema({container: #schema, editor})`, the Schema tab (4.3); the "scroll sideways" note. |
| `mountRunLog`, `mountEvidence` | `run-log.js`, `evidence-dialog.js` | `mountRunLog({ui, list: #run-history-list, isShown})`, the History tab, and F11 `mountEvidence({runLog, returnFocus: #export-button})` (section 9). |
| `mountExportDialog`, `mountExportMenu` | `export-dialog.js`, `export-menu.js` | The Export menu (4.4); while the flag `export` is on (it is by default; `?preview=-export` switches it off for a visit) it is also given `tables`, the entry of the table export (below). The dialog's `onExport(name)` is `index.js`: `changeName(name)` when the name changed, then `actions.exportDatabase()`; `onNoDatabase(message)` is a problem toast (`NO_DATABASE_MESSAGE`, `EXPORT_EMPTY_MESSAGE`). |
| `mountStatusBar`, `mountLayout`, `showPlatformKeys` | `status-bar.js`, `layout.js`, `keys.js` | The status bar (`rename(name, {builtIn})` is its one control); the pane sizes, kept under `sqllab.layout` (4.4, section 12); the platform's modifier key on every `[data-mod]` element (`⌘` on Apple devices). |
| `mountPlainText`, `guardFileDrops` | `plain-text.js` | Paste and drop into `ui.editor` take `text/plain` only; a file dropped anywhere else is refused, not opened (section 3.2). |
| `mountStorageNotice` | `storage-notice.js` | `mountStorageNotice({ui, before: ui.summary})`: the notice band, put just before `#results-summary`, while the log or the progress cannot be saved (9.1). |
| `createCopyLinkAction`, `createExportAction`, `fetchDataset` / `keepPristine` / `clearPristine`, `samplesFor`, `setShareContext`, `titleFor`, `caretToEnd`, `runSql` / `splitStatements` / `stripLeadingNoise`, message constants | `copy-link.js`, `export.js`, `datasets.js`, `samples.js`, `share.js`, `brand.js`, `caret.js`, `runner.js`, `config.js` | Plain helpers (`createCopyLinkAction({editor, button: #copy-link, popover: #copy-link-popover})`, `createExportAction(getDatabase)`; `splitStatements` and `stripLeadingNoise` only tell whether a run with no result set wrote rows, section 4.2). The skip link's click handler (it focuses the editor: following `#editor` would change the address, which the router would take for a database) and the `popstate`, `change` (file input) and `execute` listeners are in `index.js` itself. |

**Preview flags and the loader.** The flags are `PREVIEW_FLAGS` in `config-workflow.js`: `import`, `export` and `excel-import` are `true` since Nov 20, 2026, so the page ships with the three features on, and `excel-export` is `false` (nothing reads it). `true` is on for everyone and `false` is off for everyone; setting one to `false` and rebuilding the bundle switches that feature off again. An address can change a flag for one visit: a name in `preview=` switches it on (`?preview=excel-export`), and a name with a hyphen-minus in front of it switches it off (`?preview=-import`). A name given both ways is off, whatever the order and whatever the parameter it is in. A flag that needs another is off while that one is off, so `-import` also switches off `excel-import`, and `?preview=-export,-excel-import` is the page of the import of text files alone. The minus is what keeps the page without a feature reachable, and tested, now that the feature is on for everyone: with it a test or the owner sees the page as it will be if a switch is set back to `false`. `js/index.js` imports `flagOn` from `flags.js` among its first imports, and that place matters: the module reads `location.search` when it is first run, which is while the bundle's modules are evaluated: after `engine-guard.js` (a classic script, which removes only `sqlite3.*` parameters and so leaves `preview=` as it was) and before the body of `index.js`, where the first `history.pushState` or `replaceState` can happen. The answer is frozen in the module. Opening a dataset, a file, a set or an exercise, and closing a set, all rewrite the address without the parameter, and a flag the address had switched on or off keeps that state for the rest of the visit; Open from URL keeps the parameter; a share link and an evidence link are built without the query string. `flagOn("import")` is read in three places, `js/lab/import.js` (`mountImport`), `index.js` (the description of the "Open a file…" entry) and `js/lab/storage-notice.js` (`mountStorageNotice`, for the sentence about events); `flagOn("export")` in two, `js/lab/export-tables.js` (`mountTableExport`; `index.js` asks the mounted object's `enabled`) and `storage-notice.js`; `flagOn("excel-import")` in two, `import.js` (`mountImport`, once, after it has found `import` on) and `index.js` (the description of the same entry); the one flag that nothing reads yet is `excel-export`. The loader (`vendor-loader.js`) has three callers, the import (Papa Parse, section 9.6), the table export (fflate, section 9.7) and the import of workbooks (SheetJS, section 9.8).

**Page events.** Lab modules follow the page through three events dispatched on `document` by `announce()` in `index.js` (documented in the comment above it). They are the only coupling between the controller and the features; do not monkeypatch upstream objects.

- `lab:database-loaded` `detail {database, dataset, exercise}`: a database is open (page load, the switcher, upload, open-from-URL, the empty fallback). `dataset` is the manifest entry or null; `exercise` is `{set, exerciseId}` when a set opened with it, else null (the exercise panel opens or closes on it).
- `lab:run-finished` `detail {database, sql, ok, rows, error, elapsed, resultSet, changes, preview}`: after every run that had SQL, failed ones too (statements before the error stay applied). `rows` is the full row count (not the 500 shown; 0 for a run with no result set), `error` the engine's message alone on one line (`errorText`), `elapsed` the run's time in ms, `resultSet` false when no statement returned a result set (the status bar then has the summary line's words: "Done in 2 ms", or "Done. 3 rows changed in 2 ms"), `changes` the rows the run changed for such a run (null otherwise, or when it ran no INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE; section 4.2) (those three for successful runs only). `preview` is always false since the UI plan's Phase 4, which removed the table preview the app used to run on open; loggers still skip a run with `preview` true. An empty Run is not announced.
- `lab:database-changed` `detail {database}`: the open database gained tables without a run, which is an import into it (section 9.6). The Schema tab and the editor's names follow it exactly as they follow a run (they read again when `schema_version` or `total_changes()` has moved); nothing else listens, and on purpose it is not `lab:run-finished`: the run log, the History tab, the status bar and the exercise panel take every run for a query the student ran.

Custom events of the elements, for completeness: `ui.editor` fires `execute` (detail = SQL; does not bubble) and `start` (first input; nothing listens to it any more); `ui.fileInput` fires the native `change`, which `index.js` turns into a load (`FileReader`; a `.sql` name is read as text). Neither the elements' old `open-file` and `open-url` events nor the name's `change` exist any more.

## 3. Modules

### 3.1 Upstream code (js/)

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `db-path.js` | `DatabasePath`: classifies a location as `empty`, `remote` (`http(s)://`), `local` (the browser's URL parser resolves it to this origin), `binary` (an `ArrayBuffer`), `sql` (passed explicitly for a `.sql` upload) or `unsupported` (everything else: `scheme:id`, `//host`, `\\host`, `data:`, `javascript:`, `file:`). `extractName`, `toString` (raw text: escape before showing), `toHash` (unused). |
| `storage.js` | `get`/`set`/`remove` for the last SQL per database name, key `sqlime.query.<name>`. Every access is in try/catch; SQL that could not be written is kept in a module-private `Map` for the page's life. |
| `printer.js` | `{columns, values}` or a table list to an HTML `<table>` string. Escapes every column name, value and table name; a cell is `cellText(value)` first, so a BLOB reads `x'00ff10'` as in the CSV (upstream showed its bytes as `0,255,16`). Since the UI plan's Phase 4 nothing calls it: the result table is built by `js/lab/result-table.js`. `printTables` (a table list whose buttons carry `data-action="showTable"`) has no caller either and nothing handles that action. |
| `sqlite/manager.js` | `init(name, path)` boots the WASM once (`sqlite3InitModule` is single-use; the result is cached in `loading`) and returns an `SQLite` for any path type: remote/local -> `fetchFile` then `fromSql` (name ends `.sql`) or `fromBytes`; binary -> `fromBytes`; sql -> `fromSql`; empty -> `create` (`new sqlite3.oo1.DB()`, `:memory:`). `fromSql` creates an empty database and runs the script with `runSql` (`js/lab/runner.js`), so an uploaded or fetched `.sql` is under the same guard as a Run (section 5); a refused or failing statement throws and `start()` shows "Failed to load database from sql script: ...", and `database.query` is reset to `""` afterwards. `fromBytes` flips header bytes 18/19 from 2 to 1 (no WAL in memory), `sqlite3_deserialize`s into `main` and gathers tables. Exports only `{init}`; the API object is private. Every database is made by `create`, which also puts the engine's memory and its bare functions on the wrapper as `database.wasm` (`sqlite3.wasm`): the table export reads a text as its bytes through it (9.7). |
| `sqlite/db.js` | `SQLite`: `name`, `path`, `capi`, `db` (the `oo1.DB`), `wasm` (set by `manager.js` after the constructor), `query`, `tables`, `execute` (first result set, object rows; nothing calls it any more, the tables view that used it is gone), `gatherTables`, `getTableInfo` (splices the name raw; unused, do not call), `meaningfulName`. Also `DEFAULT_NAME` (`new.db`), `QUERIES`, `MESSAGES` (`executing`, `invite`, `loading`). |
| `components/` | `sqlime-editor.js` (`<sqlime-editor>`, section 4.5: a plain contenteditable box until CodeMirror, loaded at once, is drawn inside it, and for good if CodeMirror does not load; the box's own paste handler inserts text/plain and its drops are handled by `js/lab/plain-text.js`; `index.html` gives the tag `autocorrect="off"` and `autocapitalize="off"` next to the `spellcheck` the component sets, so a phone keyboard leaves the SQL alone, and CodeMirror sets the same on its content); `sqlime-result.js` (`#result`, the results pane's content box: `clear`; its `print` and `printTables` go through `printer.js` and `innerHTML`, and nothing calls them since Phase 4: `js/lab/results.js` fills the box with `replaceChildren`). Upstream's `command-bar`, `copy-link`, `db-name` and `toolbar` components are gone (the shell's top bar, the Export menu and the switcher replace them), and so are `sqlime-status` (the status line; the results pane and the toasts replace it) and `action-button.js` (Phase 4). |
| `controllers/` | `ActionController` (click delegation: finds the `<button>`, calls `handlers[btn.dataset.action]`, disables it until the returned **Promise** settles; `index.js` listens on `.topbar` and `#editor-toolbar`); `ShortcutController` (on `keydown` with Ctrl/Meta calls `handlers[event.key]` and `preventDefault`s; `index.js` maps `o` and `u`). |

### 3.2 Added code (js/lab/)

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `config.js` | **Every constant and user-visible message.** `APP_NAME` (the only place the app name is written; keep `APP_NAME = "..."` on one line, a test rewrites it), `STUDENT_ID_PATTERN`, limits (`ROW_LIMIT`, `RUN_LOG_MAX_ENTRIES`, the `SHARE_MAX_*` sizes, `DIFF_MAX_ROWS`, ...), URL parameters (`DATASET_PARAM`, `SET_PARAM`, `EXERCISE_PARAM`, `SHARE_PARAM`), paths (`MANIFEST_URL`, `EXERCISES_DIR`, `exerciseSetUrl`), storage prefixes (`PROGRESS_PREFIX`, `RUN_LOG_PREFIX`) and keys (`LAYOUT_STORAGE_KEY`, `THEME_STORAGE_KEY`, `TEXT_SIZE_STORAGE_KEY`), the breakpoints (`DRAWER_QUERY`, `PHONE_QUERY`), the text sizes (`TEXT_SIZES`, `TEXT_SIZE_DEFAULT`), `SITE_URL` (the site's public address for the link preview, empty until the owner sets one; read as text by `tools/site-meta.mjs` at deploy, never by the page) and all texts (several are functions of a name or count). Put new constants here, except what the assessment workflow plan adds: those go in `config-workflow.js` (below), because the verify page loads this file whole. |
| `brand.js` | Writes `APP_NAME` into `document.title` and `[data-app-name]`, and `APP_NAME` followed by the attribute's text into the `aria-label` of `[data-app-label]` (the brand link: "SQL Lab, home: open the default database"); `titleFor(part)`. A static page sets its part with `<title data-page-title="About">`; the text inside `<title>` is only what a no-JS view sees, so it is the page part or `Loading`, never the app name. |
| `html.js`, `sql.js`, `caret.js` | `escapeHtml` (`& < > " '`; null/undefined give `""`). `quoteIdent`, `quoteLiteral`, `tableInfoSql` (unused), `SQL_KEYWORDS`, `needsQuoting`, `identifierForSql`, `SCHEMA_OBJECTS_SQL`, `countRowsSql`, `pragmaTableInfoSql`, `pragmaForeignKeyListSql`: SQL around a name, built without splicing it raw. `caretToEnd(editor)` (CodeMirror's own selection once it draws the editor). |
| `storage-guard.js` | Classic script before the engine: reads `localStorage` and `sessionStorage` and, where that throws (site data blocked), redefines them as `undefined`, because the engine reads both while initialising without a try/catch. Where storage works it does nothing. |
| `engine-guard.js` | Classic script directly before `vendor/sqlite3.js`. The engine reads its own settings from the page's query string (`sqlite3.dir` and `sqlite3.wasm` choose where the `.wasm` is fetched from, `sqlite3.debugModule` turns on logging), so `?sqlite3.dir=https://host/` would load and run the engine from another host. This script removes every query parameter whose name, decoded the way `URLSearchParams` decodes it, starts with `sqlite3.` (so `sqlite%33.dir` too) with `history.replaceState` (no new history entry), built from the whole current URL with only its query replaced (never from the bare path: a path that starts with `//`, on a root-hosted site whose server maps it to the index, makes `//?x` an address on another host, `replaceState` throws, and the engine would read the parameter). If the address still cannot be rewritten the script calls `window.stop()`, so the engine never loads (fail closed). The other parameters (`db`, `set`, `ex`, ...), the path and the fragment are kept byte for byte; with no such parameter it does not touch the history. It also sets `globalThis.sqlite3ApiConfig.warn` (the engine merges and removes it): the engine's `sqlite3_step() rc= ... SQL = <statement>` warning for a statement that fails to run loses its SQL and keeps the result code, so a failing model query never reaches the console that way. `vendor/` stays as downloaded. `constraints.spec.js`. |
| `plain-text.js` | `mountPlainText(element)`, mounted by `index.js` for `ui.editor` (it matters for the plain box; CodeMirror pastes and drops text only itself, and a paste or drop it has dealt with is cancelled, which this module skips): only `text/plain` may enter a contenteditable area. Paste is cancelled and the text inserted with `execCommand("insertText")` (so undo and the `input` event work as for typing); the editor's own paste handler runs first and the module skips a paste that is already cancelled. A drop is cancelled and the text goes in at the drop point (`caretPositionFromPoint`, else `caretRangeFromPoint`; the end of the element when the point is outside it); a drop with files inserts nothing and is cancelled so the tab does not open the file (with the import on, `js/index.js` takes a file dropped anywhere as a chosen one; with every flag off there is no drag-to-open feature and only `verify.html` takes dropped files; `guardFileDrops` does the same for a file dropped outside the editor); a drag that starts inside the same element (`dragstart`, cleared by `dragend`, any `drop`, and by the first `pointerdown`, `pointermove` or `keydown` anywhere, which a browser does not send during a drag, so a drag that ended without a `dragend` does not leave the next, unrelated drop unprotected) is left to the browser's own move. `guardFileDrops(target = document)`, also called by `index.js`, refuses (`preventDefault`) every `dragover` and `drop` that carries files, anywhere on the page, so a dropped file is never opened in place of the page (a file is opened from the database menu, or taken from a drop when the import is on); drops of text are left to the browser (the export dialog's file name takes them natively). Never `contenteditable="plaintext-only"`: the editor relies on `execCommand`. `constraints.spec.js`. |
| `share.js` | F3 state <-> URL fragment, no DOM: `encodeShare`, `decodeShare` (never throws; section 6.2), `isShareFragment`, `shareFragment`, `shareUrl(sql, pageUrl)`, `setShareContext` / `getShareContext` (dataset, set and exercise ids carried by Copy link; validated at the setter). |
| `copy-link.js` | `createCopyLinkAction({editor, button, popover, altAnchor})`: the handler of Copy link (`#copy-link` in the top bar, and the More menu's entry, whose popover is placed under `altAnchor`, the More button, while `#copy-link` is not shown); builds the link from `editor.value` and puts it on the clipboard (`navigator.clipboard.writeText`, else, or when that is refused, `execCommand("copy")` on an off-screen field, the focus put back). It worked: the toast "Link copied" (with `NOT_IN_LINK_MESSAGE` after it when the open database is not a course one); the old way puts the focus back on Copy link, after the action has answered (`ActionController` holds the button disabled until then, and a disabled button takes no focus). It did not: the popover `#copy-link-popover` (a non-modal dialog under the button, like Help) shows the link in the read-only `#share-link-field`, focused and selected, so it costs one Ctrl+C; too long: a problem toast. It returns a resolved promise at once so the button is not held disabled by a permission prompt. |
| `router.js` | `resolveRoute(location)` -> what to open (section 6.1). |
| `datasets.js` | `validateManifest` (with the optional `samples`, 6.3), `loadManifest`, `getManifest` (memoised while it succeeds), `findDataset`, `defaultDataset`, `datasetFileName`, `fetchDataset(entry, {onProgress})` (status and 16-byte header checked; with `onProgress(loaded, total)` the body is read as a stream and counted, `total` from Content-Length, 0 when there is none or the body is compressed; the bytes are the same), and the pristine record: `keepPristine`, `clearPristine`, `getPristineBytes` (a fresh `Uint8Array` copy of the open built-in dataset exactly as fetched, or null), `getPristineId`. |
| `database-switcher.js` | `mountDatabaseSwitcher` (4.4; `openFileDescription` is the line under "Open a file…", which `index.js` changes while the import is on, and again while `excel-import` is on too). Follows `lab:database-loaded`: a course database is shown by its manifest name, anything else (an upload, a URL, the empty database) by its file name; "No database open" before any. |
| `export-menu.js`, `export-dialog.js` | `mountExportMenu({button, menu, onDatabase, results, evidence, tables})`, `mountExportDialog` (4.4). `tables` is optional, `{text, description, onSelect}`: one more entry, `#export-tables` with the sheet glyph, right after "Database (.db)…"; `index.js` gives it, and its words, only when the flag `export` is on (9.7), so that the plan's Phase 8 renames the item in one place; left out, the menu is what it was. |
| `help.js`, `keys.js` | `mountHelp({button, popover, altAnchor})` -> `{open()}` (the More menu's Help opens it under `altAnchor`); `IS_APPLE`, `MOD_KEY`, `showPlatformKeys` (4.4). |
| `more-menu.js` | `mountMoreMenu({button, menu, copyLink, onDatabase, results, evidence, help, tables})`: the More menu of a phone (4.4): Copy link, the three exports (four with the optional `tables` entry, `#more-export-tables`, as in the Export menu), the three themes, Help. |
| `text-size.js` | `readTextSize()`, `mountTextSize({smaller, larger, said})` -> `{size()}`: A− and A+ (4.4). |
| `static-page.js` | About's script: `a[data-back]` goes back in the history when the page was opened from this site (else its href, `./`), and `showPlatformKeys()`. |
| `layout.js` | `mountLayout`: the sidebar's width and the editor's height, the two splitters, the exercise prompt's cap, `sqllab.layout`, and the breakpoints: the drawer under 1100 px and the two views of a phone (4.4). |
| `status-bar.js` | `mountStatusBar` (4.4). |
| `tabs.js` | `mountTabs` (4.4). |
| `export.js` | `safeBaseName`, `exportFileName`, `exportProblem`, `serializeDatabase` (`sqlite3_js_db_export`; an empty schema gives a message), `saveFile(data, filename, type)` (Blob + hidden `<a download>`; used by the database export, the CSV, the evidence file and the table export, which hands it a Blob; `safeBaseName` also names the table export's zip), `createExportAction(getDatabase)` (run by the export dialog's `onExport`; there is no button of its own; its outcome is a toast, "Exported <file>" or the problem). |
| `schema.js`, `insert.js` | F4 Schema tab (4.3); `trackEditorSelection(editor)` -> `{insert, forget}` (DOM ranges in the plain box, CodeMirror's selection once it is drawn). |
| `result-scroll.js` | `mountResultScroll`: shows `#result-scroll-note` while `#result` is wider than its box (`MutationObserver` + `ResizeObserver`). |
| `result-table.js` | `buildResultTable({columns, values}, {popover})` (the ruled table, section 8) and `mountCellPopover(popover)` (the whole value of a long cell). |
| `samples.js` | `samplesFor(dataset, database)`: the sample queries of "no result yet": the manifest's (at most `MAX_SAMPLES`), else the first table's first rows, else none. |
| `toast.js` | `toast(message, {tone})` (section 8). |
| `open-url-dialog.js` | `mountOpenUrlDialog({onOpen})` -> `{open(returnFocus), isOpen()}`: the Open from URL dialog (4.4). |
| `editor-keys.js` | `leavesEditor`, `cancelLeave`: the Escape-then-Tab way out of the editor, called by `sqlime-editor.js` (the plain box) and `code-editor.js` (CodeMirror). |
| `code-editor.js`, `editor-schema.js`, `sqlite-words.js` | The SQL editor drawn by CodeMirror (4.5): `createCodeEditor(cm, host, {doc, anchor, head, schema})`; `mountEditorSchema({editor})` (the names to complete, read on `lab:database-loaded` and after a run, or an import into the open database (`lab:database-changed`), that moved `PRAGMA schema_version`) and `readSchema(database)`; `SQLITE_KEYWORDS`, `SQLITE_FUNCTIONS` (SQLite's own words, for the highlighting and the completion). |
| `runner.js` | `splitStatements`, `runSql`, `engineMessage`, `engineLine`, `isVacuumInto`, `stripLeadingNoise` (section 5; `engineLine` is `engineMessage` on one line, each run of white space one space: the table export puts the engine's words in a row of its dialog and in its alert line). |
| `results.js` | F5 and the UI plan's results pane: `mountResults`, `errorText`, `toCsv`, `csvBytes`, `csvFileName` (section 8). `view.showReport(node, {summary})` shows a report of the page's own in place of a result (the import's): the pane's state is then `report`, the summary line is shown and said, and the next run replaces it. |
| `cell-text.js` | `cellText(value)`: the text of one result cell (NULL `""`, BLOB `x'<hex>'`, else `String`), shared by the CSV (`results.js`) and the result table (`js/printer.js`). Not escaped; the HTML side escapes. The table export (9.7) writes a BLOB the same way with a table of its own, so that its rule cannot change by accident; `aw3-export-rules` holds the two equal. |
| `exercises.js` | Sets: `validateSet`, `loadSet`, `listSets`, `idsInListing`, `badNamesInListing` (section 7.1). |
| `checker.js` | `normalizeValue`, `normalizeRow`, `serializeRow`, `displayValue`, `compareResults`, `createChecker()` (section 7.2). |
| `progress.js` | `isPassed`, `markPassed`, `passedCount`, `getDraft`, `saveDraft`, `resetSet` (section 7.3). |
| `storage-notice.js` | `reportStorage(what, ok)` (the `lab:storage-state` document event, sent by `run-log.js`, `events.js` and `progress.js` after each write) and `mountStorageNotice({ui, before})` (called from `index.js`: `#storage-notice`, a `role=status` warning band inserted before `before` = `#results-summary`, shown while the log, the events of the evidence log or the progress could not be saved; section 9.1). The sentence about the events is made when the band is mounted, from the preview flags (`storageNoticeEventsText({imports, exports})` of `config-workflow.js`): with the import alone it names "the files you brought in", with the export alone "the tables you exported", with both "the files you brought in and the tables you exported"; with neither flag it is the first (nothing writes an event then, so it is never shown). The page ships with both flags on, so a visit meets the third sentence unless its address switches one off. |
| `exercise-panel.js`, `set-actions.js` | `mountExercises`, `chooseExercise`; `createSetActions` (section 7.4). |
| `run-log.js` | F6: `logKeyFor`, `isDatasetKey`, `readLog`, `appendLog`, `mountRunLog` (the History tab, section 9.1). |
| `evidence.js` | F11, pure: `utcTimestamp`, `sanitizeLine`, `escapeSql`, `formatEvidence`, `sha256Hex`, `hasCrypto`, `buildEvidence`, `verifyEvidence` (section 9.2; version 2, a file with events, in 9.5). |
| `evidence-dialog.js` | F11: `mountEvidence`, `linkFor` (section 9.3). |
| `verify-page.js` | The whole script of `verify.html` (section 9.4). |
| `config-workflow.js` | The settings of what the assessment workflow plan adds (`docs/`, section 9.5): the preview flags (`PREVIEW_PARAM`, `PREVIEW_FLAGS`, `PREVIEW_FLAG_NEEDS`), the events store (`EVENTS_PREFIX`, `EVENTS_VERSION`, `EVENTS_MAX`, `EVENTS_MAX_BYTES`, `EVENT_MAX_LINES`, `EVENT_MAX_LINE_CHARS`, `evidenceSavedWithEventsMessage`, `storageNoticeEventsText`) and the loader (`VENDOR_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS`). Constants and message builders only, like `config.js`, and apart from it because `verify.html` and `about.html` load `config.js` whole and the verify page is held to 100,000 bytes (`m5-verify-page`): nothing the verify page loads may import this file. From the plan's Phase 2 on, the texts and limits of the import and export dialogs go here too: Phase 2's are the section "Import of CSV files" (Phase 5's constants sit in it beside their neighbours, each group with a comment: the limits and refusals of a workbook after the text limits, the sheets' words after the dialog's, the report's two notes after the report's, and the interval of the memory watch `WORKBOOK_WATCH_MS`), Phase 3's the last one, "Export of tables as CSV" (the piece sizes `EXPORT_PIECE_CHARS` and `EXPORT_PIECE_MS`, the file and zip names, the cut of a table's file name `EXPORT_NAME_MAX_CHARS`, types, library, level and limits, the menu entry's words, every text of the dialog and the event's two line builders `exportEventFileLine` and `exportEventTableLine`). `EVENT_KINDS` and the version 2 words of the evidence file stay in `config.js`, because `evidence.js` needs them on the verify page. |
| `flags.js` | The plan's preview flags: `readFlags(search, defaults?, needs?)` (the rule, pure: the defaults are `PREVIEW_FLAGS`, then the names asked for on, then the names asked for off with a hyphen-minus, so that off wins, then the needs), `flagOn(name)`, `flags()` (a copy). The address is read once, when the module is first run (`index.js` imports it), and never again (section 2). |
| `vendor-loader.js` | `loadVendor(file, globalName)` -> a promise of the library: adds `vendor/<file>` to the page as a classic `<script>` the first time it is asked for, once however many callers wait; a failure (no answer in `VENDOR_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS`, an error, a file that does not define the global) removes the script and rejects every waiting caller with an `Error`, and the next call tries again; so does a page that cannot add the script at all. Only a plain file name of `vendor/` and a library name that is text are taken, and both are looked at first, before a library already on the page is handed back (anything else is a rejection with no request). The library must be a build that attaches itself to the page as a global, under a name nothing else on the page has (the page's own `name` or `top` would be handed back as the library): the page has no `define`, `module` or `exports`. Removing the script does not stop the browser, so a file that arrives after the wait is over still runs, and the next call then finds the library on the page. A browser can join a new request to one for the same address that is still open, so after a request that never answered the next try may wait for that one to end. Three callers: the import, for Papa Parse (the plan's Phase 2, section 9.6), the table export, for fflate (Phase 3, section 9.7), and the import of workbooks, for SheetJS (Phase 5, section 9.8). |
| `events.js` | The events of the evidence log: `recordEvent(key, {kind, lines}, now?)`, `readEvents(key)`, `timeline(queries, events)` (section 9.5). |
| `import.js` | The import of CSV files, behind the flag `import` (on by default; section 9.6), and, with `excel-import` on as well (it is by default), of Excel workbooks (section 9.8): `mountImport(page)` -> `{enabled, take(files), busy()}` and, when on, `run(job, {progress, cancelled})` (what the dialog calls; the tests call it with a job made by hand); `page.otherJobMessage()` (optional, `() => null`) gives the busy message of a table export that runs, and `take()` and `run()` refuse with it. Takes the files a student chooses or drops, runs the import (a new database built apart, or a savepoint in the open one; a text file through a staging table, a sheet of a workbook straight into a typed table), records its events and shows its report. |
| `import-dialog.js` | `mountImportDialog({returnFocus, getDestination, loadReader, excel, onImport})` -> `{open(files)}`: one tab per file with the first rows as read, the settings, the table's name, the destination, and the progress of the import with Cancel; with `excel` given (the flag `excel-import`: `{loadLibrary, inspectWorkbook, readWorkbook}`), a workbook's tab holds a strip of sheets, each a unit with its own name, row of names, preview and tick (section 9.8). Follows `lab:database-loaded`: a database that opens under the idle dialog closes it. Calls `swallowHeldEnter()` (`held-enter.js`) once for each closing, the first time it hears of it. |
| `import-report.js` | `buildImportReport(tables)` -> the report's element: per table and per column what the import made, and the names a query must write in double quotes (9.6); for a sheet's table also the paragraph of formula cells that came out empty and, in a column's note, what Excel showed for a number whose zeros are not in the table (9.8). |
| `import-db.js` | The database side: `takenNames`, `beginImport` / `endImport` / `undoImport` (a savepoint, whose name `SAVEPOINT` is exported for a test to ask the engine about), `newStage` (the TEMP staging table: `add`, `copyInto`, `drop`), `newTypedTable(database, name, columns)` (a sheet's table: made at once with its declared types, one prepared insert, `add(row)`, `finish()`, `abandon()`; a number for a REAL column is bound as a double, section 9.8), `namesNeedingQuotes` (which of a table's names the engine does not take bare), `isOutOfMemory` and `isEngineError` (what a failure was: the engine or its allocator had no memory left; the engine or its allocator threw it at all, and not the page's own code; the table export asks both, 9.7). |
| `import-csv.js` | Reading delimited text with the library handed in: `readStart` (the preview), `lineEnding` (the line ending of a text, with the quotes read as the parser reads them: not Papa's guess, 9.6), `readPieces` (a whole file, a piece at a time), `newTableReader` (rows to a table's columns), `ImportRefused`, `DELIMITERS`, `MAX_COLUMNS` (defined in `config-workflow.js` since Phase 5, which the sheets of a workbook are held to as well, and handed on from here). Pure: runs in Node. |
| `import-text.js` | Bytes to text: `kindByStart` (what a file is, by its first bytes), `inspectText`, `guessEncoding`, `fitsEncoding`, `startOfText`, `textPieces`; and `markOf` / `sameStart` / `TextChanged` (a mark of the preview's text, and the check that the import reads the same text). Pure. |
| `import-rules.js` | The faithful import rules: `isWhole`, `isDecimal`, the tally that types a column from all its values (`tallyValue` for a text, `tallyNumber` for a numeric cell of a workbook), and names (`sanitiseName`, `uniqueName`, `columnNames`, `tableNameFor`, `sameNameKey`, `FALLBACK_TABLE_NAME`). Pure; the import of workbooks (9.8) goes through it too, and so does the table export for the names of its files (`sanitiseName`, `sameNameKey`, `FALLBACK_TABLE_NAME`, and the rule of `uniqueName`, which `csvFileNames` follows with a count of its own; not `tableNameFor`, which cuts at a dot, section 9.7). |
| `import-pace.js` | `newPace()` -> `{rest()}`: the page's turn between two pieces of a long job, with a drawn frame every `PAINT_EVERY_MS`, and `nextFrame()`: a promise that resolves when the browser has drawn a frame, or after `FRAME_MAX_MS` in a tab that draws none. It serves both long jobs: the import for its slices and the steps of its copy (and, for a workbook, the zip check, the frame before the library's call, the sheets and the steps of a sheet's rows), the table export for its counts and its pieces, and for the frame that its dialog waits for before the first count and its controller before the first piece (both import `nextFrame` from here). |
| `import-workbook.js` | Reading one workbook, behind the flag `excel-import` (on by default; section 9.8). Pure: no DOM; what needs the page (the library, a rest, a frame, whether the dialog is still the one that began) is handed in. `inspectWorkbook(file, kind)` says `{refused: text}`, `{ask: {megabytes, unpackedMegabytes, heavy}, directory}` or `{go: true, directory}` (the limits, before the file is read; it never throws); `readWorkbook(file, kind, {directory, loadLibrary, rest, frame, current})` gives `{sheets, charts}`, `{refused: text}` or `{moved: true}` (the library's one blocking call, the sheets listed); `watchCall`, `watchStep`, `roomAgain` and `forWantOfMemory` (how a step that the browser has no memory left for is told of); `letStringsGo` and `formatsHeld` (what the library keeps) are inside. |
| `import-zip.js` | A zip's own check, made before the library sees an .xlsx (section 9.8). Pure, no library: `zipDirectory(file)` -> `{entries, unpacked}`, `{damaged: true}`, or `null` for a Zip64 file, `checkZip(bytes, entries, {rest})` (every part unpacked by the browser's `DecompressionStream` and compared with its stored size and CRC-32), `canUnpack`, `crc32`. |
| `import-cells.js` | The rules for the cells of a workbook, as pure functions over the library's cell objects (section 9.8): `readSheet` (a sheet: its rectangle, its state), `cellValue`, `serialText` (a date, a time, a duration, on the app's own calendar), `numberInFull`, the tests of a number format (`isDateFormat`, `isDurationFormat`, `paddingZeros`), and the only ways to a sheet's cells: `previewRows`, `tableOf`, `storedRow`, `sheetTableName`. The library is handed in (`ssf`), never imported. |
| `import-events.js` | `importEvents(tables)` -> `[{lines}]`: one evidence event for each file of an import, a line for each of its tables, each ending with its sheet when it came from one (section 9.8). Pure. |
| `held-enter.js` | `swallowHeldEnter()`: from now on a repeated Enter (`event.repeat`) does nothing, wherever the focus is, until Enter is let go. Both long jobs' dialogs (`import-dialog.js`, `export-tables-dialog.js`) call it in `endSession()`, which ends a dialog's session once for each closing, the first time the page hears of it: at `close()`, at the end of a job, or at Escape, which the browser tells of (the dialog's `cancel` event) before it closes the dialog, so that no repeat of the key slips in between. The `close` event, a moment after the fact, ends a session only for a closing that nothing else told of. Only the dialog's own `cancel` event is Escape: one that comes up from inside it (a file chooser that is dismissed sends one) ends nothing. A key that is held down repeats; a dialog that closes under it (a small table is exported before the key is let go, and Cancel and Close close at the first press) would hand the repeats to what gets the focus back: the Export button, whose menu then opens, the next repeat pressing its first entry, "Database (.db)…", whose dialog then saves the database file again and again, or the editor, which gets empty lines. One `keydown` and one `keyup` listener on `window`, in the capture phase, so that it hears a key before the editor, a menu or the page's shortcuts; it takes both away when Enter is let go, and at a press of Enter that is not a repeat, which acts as it always does, so a dialog closed with the mouse changes nothing, and nothing puts it back on for that closing, however late the `close` event comes. Another key does not end it, pressed or let go: the keyboard may go on repeating Enter after that (Tab let go a moment after Enter went down, Shift pressed while it is down), and no other key is touched meanwhile. Not covered: Enter held on the Export button itself, with no dialog of a long job in between, still opens the menu and goes on into "Database (.db)…" (older than the plan, and left as it was so that the page with every flag off is the page it was). |
| `export-csv.js` | The table export's files, pure (loads in Node): `fieldText`, `realText`, `valueText(kind, value)`, `rowText(kinds, values)` -> `{text, blobs}`, `headerText`, `CSV_BOM`, `VALUE_KINDS` (defined in `export-read.js`, handed on from here), `csvFileNames(tableNames)` (the name of each table's file: `sanitiseName`, cut at 100 characters, `.csv` added, and made unique as `uniqueName` makes names unique, with the count for a name going on where it stopped), `tablesZipName(databaseName)`, `planOutput({format, databaseName, tables})` (what is saved: a file or a zip, its name and type, the library it needs, its entries, each with `renamed`) and `zipProblem(entryCount, bytes, limits?)` (section 9.7). |
| `export-read.js` | The table export's engine side, values and no text: `VALUE_KINDS`, `listObjects(database)`, `countRows(database, name)`, `openRows(database, name)` -> `{columns, next({chars, ms}), close()}`, rows as `{kinds, values}` with the engine's own storage class for each value. Reads every text, names included, as its bytes through `database.wasm`, so that a leading U+FEFF is kept. Knows nothing of CSV (section 9.7). |
| `export-tables.js` | The table export, behind the flag `export` (on by default; section 9.7): `mountTableExport(page)` -> `{enabled, open()}` (it has no `busy()`: nothing asked for one, and the page is told by `onBusy`). Runs one export (the job the dialog hands it: the library, the pieces and the pace, the sinks, Cancel, the event, the toast, the notes). Also exports the sinks, `fileSink(type)`, `zipSink(fflate, type, entries, limits?)` and `sinkFor(plan, zipTool, limits?)` (the sink a plan asks for, which is the only way `run` makes one, and which throws for a kind of output it has no sink for); the module loads in Node, so a spec can give a zip small limits. |
| `export-tables-dialog.js` | `mountTableExportDialog({returnFocus, getDatabase, list, count, plan, onExport})` -> `{open()}`: the list of tables and views with their counts and tick boxes, the line beside Export, the progress and Cancel, the error line and the note at the end. Holds no SQL and no CSV rule: the engine side and the plan come in as functions. Its frame and its pace come from `import-pace.js`, and it calls `swallowHeldEnter()` (`held-enter.js`) once for each closing, the first time it hears of it. `css/export-tables.css` is its stylesheet (after `import.css` in `STYLES`; the tokens and the shared `.app-dialog-*` and `results-*` classes only, none of the `.import-*` ones). |
| `theme-boot.js`, `theme.js` | The theme: light, dark or match the device (the default). `theme-boot.js` is a classic script at the top of every page's `<head>` that sets `data-theme` on `<html>` from storage before the first paint (it repeats the key, because a module would run too late; a test checks it equals `THEME_STORAGE_KEY`). It applies the stored text size too (`--code-size` on `<html>`, a size of `TEXT_SIZES` only; `tests/ui6-responsive.spec.js` checks the key and the sizes against `config.js`). `theme.js`: `themeChoice`, `resolvedTheme`, `setThemeChoice` (stores `"light"`/`"dark"`, removes the key for `"device"`; a choice that cannot be stored holds for the page; it sends `lab:theme-chosen`, so the Theme menu and the More menu tick the same entry), `mountThemeMenu(container, {before, buttonClass})`. A pinned theme also sets both `<meta name="theme-color">` to the page's drawn background, read with `getComputedStyle` (no colour in JS); Match device puts the page's own values back. The button shows a sun or a moon for the scheme in use (both icons exist, one is hidden: the sprite builder finds icons by literal name). |
| `menu.js` | `mountMenu(button, menu, {onSelect, onOpen, opensOn})`: the WAI-ARIA menu button on top of the `popover` attribute (top layer, light dismiss). The button stays the popover's invoker (`popoverTargetElement`), so a press on it while open is not a light dismiss; its click is handled here (the browser's own toggle is cancelled) so the focus moves into the menu in the same task: Enter, Space and ArrowDown open on the checked item (else the first; with `opensOn: "first"`, as the More menu, on the first), ArrowUp on the last; in the menu the arrows wrap, Home and End, a letter jumps, Escape closes back to the button, Tab closes and moves on; `aria-expanded` is set in the synchronous `beforetoggle`. An item with `aria-disabled="true"` does nothing. `placePopover(popover, anchor)` puts any popover under (or over) its anchor, aligned to the anchor's nearer edge, cut to the window. |
| `icons.js` | `icon(name, {filled})`: a decorative `<svg>` (`aria-hidden`) that uses `#icon-<name>` from the sprite `tools/build-icons.mjs` writes into `index.html` (Lucide, from `lucide-static` 1.61.0). The sprite holds exactly the icons named literally in `icon("...")` calls and `#icon-...` references (a test compares). `.icon` in `core.css` draws 16 px with a 1.5 px stroke in `currentColor`. |

## 4. Flow: open a database, execute, render

### 4.1 Open

```
startFromCurrentUrl          ticket = beginLoad(); results.showLoading() ("Loading the database"); route = await resolveRoute(window.location)
 -> picker.setManifest(route.manifest)       (the switcher's list, set before the stale check: the list is true whichever load wins)
 -> the loading state is named (the dataset's manifest name, else the file name)
 -> route.dataset?  fetchDataset(dataset, {onProgress}) (relative URL, bytes validated, counted into the progress bar)
      fails, a database open (back/forward step) -> kept, results.loadFailed: the view from before the load back, the band "The previous database is still open" above it, switcher synced
      fails, none open (first load)              -> dataset = null, problem = "could not be loaded, so an empty database is open", empty database
      ok -> path = DatabasePath(copy of the bytes, "binary"), name = file basename; the fetched buffer is kept for keepPristine
 -> else DatabasePath(route.database)         a plain fragment path, or "" (empty database)
 -> start(name, path, {sql, dataset, pristine, ticket, exercise})
      unsupported path -> no loader call, "Failed to load database from <address>" (results.loadFailed: over the view kept, or the band alone)
      manager.init(name, path), then the stale check (ticket !== latestLoad: close the new database, show nothing)
      database = loaded, the previous connection is closed; pristine bytes kept (dataset) or cleared (anything else); openDataset = dataset
      setShareContext({dataset: dataset?.id})
      database.query = exercise ? lab.editorSql(exercise, sql) : sql ?? (database.query || storage.get(database.name))
      title, editor value, focus the editor (unless keepFocus), announce("lab:database-loaded") (the switcher, the schema, the status bar, the run log and the exercise panel follow that event)
 -> a dataset the engine cannot open, none open yet -> start() again on an empty database with the "could not be loaded" message
 -> showStarted({shared, notice, problem})
      results.showEmpty({variant, notice, problem}): "no result yet", nothing run (section 8)
        variant "shared" (the editor holds a link's SQL), "restored" (SQL from storage, or an exercise's draft) or "fresh" (an empty editor)
        problem (a damaged link, a manifest or dataset problem): the error band above it
      a load the student asked for (anything after the page's first) -> the toast "Opened <name>"
```

Other ways in:

- `startFromDataset(dataset, exercise)` (the database switcher, and the set picker when the set's dataset is not open): ticket, the loading state with the dataset's name, `fetchDataset` with its progress, `start(..., {keepFocus: true})`, then `history.pushState` of `?db=<id>` (or `?set=<id>` with an exercise; relative, no fragment). On failure the previous database stays open, `picker.sync()` puts the switcher's label back, and `results.loadFailed` brings back what the pane showed before the pick with the band above it. Focus is not moved to the editor on purpose: a menu returns it to its button, so a keyboard user can keep choosing.
- `openSet(set)` in `set-actions.js` (the Exercises tab's set menu): when the open database is the set's dataset (`getPristineId() === set.dataset`, nothing loading) it is kept, because reloading would discard the student's work (`lab.open(set)`, `pushState("?set=<id>")`); otherwise `startFromDataset` with the exercise. `closeSet()` cancels a load still on its way, `lab.close()`, `pushState("?db=<id>")` (or `./` for a non-dataset database); a cancelled load leaves "Loading database..." behind, so the invitation replaces it.
- `startFromUrl` ("Open from URL…" in the switcher / Ctrl+U: the dialog of `open-url-dialog.js`, which refuses an empty or unsupported address inline; the loading state names the file, with no size; pushes `#<url>`), `startFromFile` ("Open a file…" / Ctrl+O: `FileReader`, `.sql` as text, else an `ArrayBuffer`; the loading state names the file with its size, already all read; pushes `./`), and `popstate` (back/forward, hash edits, a pasted link in an open tab), which goes through `startFromCurrentUrl`. The fragment stays in the address bar after opening a link and `?db=` after a pick, so a reload restores the same state.

**Stale-load guard.** Every way of opening a database takes a ticket from `beginLoad()` (`++latestLoad`, `loading = true`) before any fetch and passes it to `start`; a load whose ticket is old after an `await` (after `resolveRoute`, after the dataset fetch, after `manager.init`, and in `start`'s `catch`) is dropped without touching the page, and its failure is silent. The last request always wins. `endLoad(ticket, opened)` runs in a `finally`, only the latest counts: it clears `loading` and, when nothing opened, syncs the switcher and the set picker (they show what is open again). While `loading` is true `execute` refuses (`LOADING_BUSY_MESSAGE`, nothing run or saved).

**Behaviours worth knowing.**

- **A database built apart.** `start()` takes `built`, a database that is open already, and then calls no loader: `openBuilt(database)` uses it for the import's new database (section 9.6), which opens as a file does (`pushState("./")`) but with no "no result yet" and no "Opened" toast, because the import shows its report. `openFile(file)` is the old body of the file input's handler, shared with the drop (9.6).
- **Nothing runs on load.** Restored SQL (storage, a share link, a draft, a history entry) is put in the editor and never run; only an explicit Run or Check executes SQL. A runaway query saved earlier, or sent in a link, must not hang the tab on load. Anything new that restores SQL keeps this rule. Since the UI plan's Phase 4 the app does not run the first table's preview on open either (inventory row 82): a first visit has an empty editor and "no result yet" with the sample queries, and the preview's query is the fallback sample for a database without manifest samples. `app.openEmpty()` gives an empty start.
- A failed load shows the error band `Failed to load database from <path>` (plus `: <exception>`; `loadFailedMessage`), as text. `database` is assigned only after a successful load, so a failure leaves what was open, and the results pane shows what it showed before the load (the student's result is still true of it) with the band above it (`loadFailed`, section 8). With none open, Run shows the band "No database is loaded." (`NO_DATABASE_MESSAGE`) and "Database (.db)…" a toast with it, instead of throwing.
- With no usable manifest or dataset the app opens an empty in-memory `new.db` with a specific message; upload and open-from-URL work; the next start retries the manifest.
- Choosing the same file twice loads it twice (`index.js` clears the file input after reading).
- The logo links to `./` (a full navigation: the default dataset).

### 4.2 Execute

```
Ctrl/Cmd+Enter in the editor -> CustomEvent "execute" {detail: editor.query} -> execute(detail).catch(ignoreRejection)
Run button -> ActionController -> actions.executeCurrent -> execute(ui.editor.query)

execute(sql, {quiet})                              index.js
   loading -> a note toast (LOADING_BUSY_MESSAGE), refused; no database -> the band "No database is loaded."
   sql = sql.trim(); storage.set(database.name, sql)              (saves what was run, i.e. the selection if any)
   empty -> "no result yet", its heading said, return (no lab:run-finished)
   run = database; endRun = results.startRun()     ("Running…" armed, invisible for 300 ms); pendingRuns++
   await afterPaint()                              one frame drawn (rAF, then a task; at most 250 ms in a tab that draws none)
   database replaced meanwhile, or a load begun -> dropped (rejected with RUN_DROPPED, {dropped: true}; nothing run, shown or announced; a Check whose run was dropped checks nothing)
   before = run.db.changes(true)                   (sqlite3_total_changes)
   result = runSql(run, sql)                       js/lab/runner.js
   changes = result ? null : changedRows(run, before, sql)
   showResult(result, elapsed, {changes, quiet})   -> results.showResult (quiet: shown, not said: a Check's run)
   catch -> showError(exc) -> results.showError; returns a rejected promise
   finally -> announce("lab:run-finished", {database, sql, preview: false, ok, rows, elapsed, resultSet, changes | error}); endRun(); pendingRuns--
```

- **Run answers at once.** `executeCurrent` (the Run button's action) starts `execute` and returns a resolved promise: `ActionController` holds a button disabled until its action's promise settles, and a disabled button loses the keyboard focus, so a Run that waited its frame took the focus off Run (to `<body>`) after every press. Run is never disabled during a run now (the page is blocked during it anyway); two quick presses run twice, as they always did.
- **The frame before a run.** A run blocks the page until it ends (the engine is synchronous on the main thread; a Web Worker is held back by the plan), so "Running…" after 300 ms (the plan's section 5) can only be drawn by the compositor: `results.startRun()` puts the overlay in the page with a CSS animation that waits 300 ms and fades it in, and `execute` lets one frame be drawn (`afterPaint`) before `runSql`, so the animation is already on the compositor when the run starts. A run that ends sooner removes it before it ever shows. Without the frame nothing appears during the run (checked by recording a 1.5 s run in Chromium and Firefox; `tests/ui4-results.spec.js` checks the order: overlay, a frame, then the run). The frame costs every run about 16 ms and makes `execute` finish after the key press or click that asked for it: `window.app.whenIdle()` resolves once no run is waiting or running, and the test helpers (`app.run`, `app.settle`) wait on it. Check (`runCheck`) awaits `execute` as before.
- **Rows changed.** For a run with no result set, `changes` is the growth of `sqlite3_total_changes` over the run (every row an INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE touched, triggers and cascades included, summed over its statements); when it did not grow, 0 if a statement of the run begins (after comments) with INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE or REPLACE (an UPDATE that matched nothing: "Done. 0 rows changed"), else null ("Done" alone: CREATE TABLE and the like). A DML statement inside a CTE (`WITH ... INSERT`) that changes nothing reads as "Done". Reading the counter changes nothing about the run.

- `ui.editor.query` is the selected text if the whole selection lies inside the editor, else the full text (so text selected in a prompt or a result cell is never what Run executes).
