- A multi-statement run shows **the last statement that has result columns** (zero rows included): `select 1 as a; select 2 as b, 3 as c` shows `b, c`; trailing DDL/DML does not replace it; a final `select ... where 0` shows "0 rows". **Duplicate column names are kept** and the order is the SELECT's. The status line and table are one per run, not per statement; time covers the whole run.
- Statements before a failing one stay applied: nothing wraps a run in a transaction (no `BEGIN` in `js/`), so each auto-commits; the failing one and the rest never run. The failed run's rows are lost (the error path clears the table).
- `execute` returns a rejected promise on error (and when it refuses or drops a run); the editor listener swallows it (`ignoreRejection`), `ActionController` swallows it for buttons, and `runCheck` catches it.
- The editor keeps Tab for indentation (two spaces); Escape then Tab / Shift+Tab leaves it (Help says so, and `#editor-hint`, a visually hidden sentence, is the editor's `aria-describedby`); any other key or a blur cancels the Escape. Section 4.5 has the rest of the editor's keys.
- Fidelity: Copy link carries `ui.editor.value`. In CodeMirror that is the text exactly as typed. In the plain box it is `innerText`: in Chromium typed text makes `<div>` lines, so a blank line reads back with one extra newline (two Enters give three newlines; it matters only for a string literal or comment that spans a blank line); the recipient sees exactly what the sender's `value` read (upstream behaviour, not changed).

### 4.3 The Schema tab (F4)

`index.js` has one line for it: `mountSchema({container: #schema, editor: ui.editor})`. Code: `js/lab/schema.js`, `js/lab/insert.js`, `css/schema.css`.

- **Where.** `#schema` is the content of `#panel-schema`, the first tab panel of `nav#sidebar` (4.4), which scrolls on its own. Another tab, or a hidden sidebar, hides it; the panel has no toggle of its own and the selected tab is not stored (Schema is selected on every load; opening an exercise set switches to Exercises).
- **Content.** `.schema-dataset`: the dataset's description, clamped to two lines, with "Show more" / "Show less" (`aria-expanded`) only when two lines do not hold it (measured again by a `ResizeObserver` whenever the description's box changes: a database loaded while another tab was shown is laid out only when the Schema tab is); hidden for an upload; the dataset's name is in a visually hidden line (the switcher and the status bar show it). A search box ("Filter tables and views", case-insensitive substring) appears once there are more than `SCHEMA_FILTER_FROM` = 8 objects. Then two groups, "Tables" and "Views" (`section.schema-group`, `h2` + `ul`: the page's h1 is the brand), each hidden when empty, listing `SCHEMA_OBJECTS_SQL` (tables then views, by name; `sqlite_*` and TEMP objects are not listed). Each `li.schema-item` has an expander (`aria-expanded`, name "Columns of table orders"), an insert button ("Insert table name orders into the query") and the row count (thousands separated; a failed count shows an en dash). A filter with no match says so.
- **Columns.** Read from `PRAGMA table_info("<quoted>")` when a table is first expanded (a failed lookup shows an en dash). Each `li.schema-column` has an insert button with the column's name, a key icon named "Primary key" when `pk` is set, a link icon named "Foreign key to <table>" when `PRAGMA foreign_key_list` (`pragmaForeignKeyListSql`; tables only; a failure just gives no marks) lists the column, and the declared type (none for a view's expression column).
- **Refresh.** `lab:database-loaded` renders from scratch (expanded names and the filter are forgotten). `lab:run-finished`, and `lab:database-changed` after an import into the open database, computes `schema_version + ":" + total_changes()` and re-renders only if it changed (DDL moves the first, INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE and triggers the second, SELECT neither). A render keeps the expanded names, the filter, the scroll position (of the tab panel `#panel-schema`, the real scroller; a new database starts it at the top) and the focused button (`data-focus-key`). Every row and column name is a 32 px target (44 on a touch screen); "Show more" draws a 32 px area around its small text.
- **Insert.** A click calls `tracker.insert(identifierForSql(name))`, for a table, a view or a column. Once CodeMirror draws the editor, its own selection (kept while the focus is elsewhere and moved with every change) is where the name goes, one transaction and so one undo step, then the focus returns with the caret after the name; the rule below about the app's own text is the same. In the plain box, `insert.js` records the editor's selection on `selectionchange` and on `blur` (Chromium delivers `selectionchange` late) as a cloned `Range` plus the editor text, trusts it only if the text is unchanged, focuses with `HTMLElement.prototype.focus`, restores the range (or `caretToEnd`) and uses `execCommand("insertText")` (one undo step in Chromium and Firefox). Spaces are added around the name unless the neighbouring character is whitespace or punctuation. Trap: after the app sets the editor text itself the browser parks the caret at the start, so `lab:database-loaded` calls `tracker.forget()` and nothing is remembered until the student acts in the editor. Insert only edits the editor; it never runs anything.

### 4.4 The app shell

Code: `js/lab/layout.js`, `tabs.js`, `database-switcher.js`, `export-menu.js`, `export-dialog.js`, `help.js`, `more-menu.js`, `text-size.js`, `status-bar.js`, `keys.js`, `css/shell.css`; the markup is in `index.html`.

- **Frame.** `.app` is a grid of three rows (top bar, `.workbench`, status bar), `100dvh` high with `overflow: hidden`: the page does not scroll at desktop sizes, the panes do (`.tab-panel`, `.editor-pane`, the exercise prompt and the result). `.app` and every scrolling pane are `position: relative`, so absolutely positioned content (`.visually-hidden` text) is clipped by its pane instead of lengthening the page. `.workbench` is the navigation landmark `nav.sidebar-region` (a grid of `var(--sidebar-width, 280px)` for `#sidebar` and the sidebar's splitter, so the splitter is inside a landmark and stays usable while `#sidebar` is hidden and inert) and `main#main` ("Query and result"). `.main` is a grid of five rows, prompt, toolbar (`#editor-toolbar`, `role="group"` "Query"), editor (`var(--editor-height, 40%)`), splitter and results (the rest). The results pane is a flex column: the verdict, the storage notice, the summary line and the scroll note keep their size and `#result` takes the rest as its own scroller, both ways (at least 6rem; the pane scrolls only when the rest leaves it less), so the summary and Download CSV stay in view and a wide table never widens the page; what the pane shows in each state is section 8. The sidebar comes before the main area in the markup, so the skip link (the first Tab stop; it focuses `#editor`) is how a keyboard user gets past it. The top bar is 48 px (7 px padding, 32 px controls, a 1 px rule) and keeps one row down to 700 px; the status bar is one 28 px row from 700 px up (`flex-wrap: nowrap`; only `#status-database` shrinks, with an ellipsis and its `title` whole).
- **The breakpoints** (the UI plan's section 6 and Phase 6). From 1100 px, the three panes above. **From 700 to 1099 px** (`DRAWER_QUERY`) the sidebar is a drawer over the page: `layout.js` gives `#sidebar` the `popover="auto"` attribute (and takes it away from 1100 px up) and makes the top bar's Sidebar button (`#sidebar-button`, icon and "Sidebar") its invoker, so the browser draws it in the top layer, over a scrim (`::backdrop`, `--scrim`), and closes it on Escape and on a press beside it. It is fixed at the left, the window's height, `min(320px, 100vw - 56px)` wide, edged by a 1 px rule (a pane: no shadow); its Close button (`#sidebar-close`) closes it too, and so does the focus arriving anywhere but in it or on its button (a `focusin` on the document: Tab past its end, Shift+Tab past its button, a name inserted from the Schema tab, a History entry; an exercise chosen: `index.js` calls `closeDrawer()`). Up to 900 px the Sidebar button and the brand are their icons alone (their names stay for a screen reader), so the database switcher keeps room for a name. As it opens the focus goes to its selected tab; as it closes with the focus inside, or with the focus nowhere after a press beside it, the focus goes back to the Sidebar button. It never opens by itself (a set that opens leaves it shut: `showSidebar()` changes nothing under 1100 px), and the wide window's choice to hide the sidebar is kept for later. The sidebar's splitter is not shown; the editor and the result keep their split. **Under 700 px**, and on a touch screen less than 500 px tall, a phone held sideways (`PHONE_QUERY`, `(max-width: 699.98px), (max-height: 499.98px) and (pointer: coarse)`; `DRAWER_QUERY` takes the same second clause, and the rules for 700 px up exclude it; `docs/design/mockup/phone.html`): one column of 56 px top bar (the Sidebar button as its icon, the brand's mark in a 44 px target, the switcher across the rest, 44 px high, its name at the left and its arrow at the right end, and More), the view buttons (`#view-editor`, `#view-result`: 48 px, the shown one marked by `aria-current` and an accent underline), then the view: `data-view` on `.main` shows the exercise prompt (at most 45 % of the view, scrolling) and the editor, or the results pane. A run shows the result (`lab:run-finished`; `index.js` calls `showView("result")` for a Check's verdict, which can come without a run, and for a Run that answers without running: nothing written, no database), and the editor shows itself whenever something puts text or the focus in it (`sqlime-editor.js` sends "reveal" from `focus()` and `fill()`, and `insert.js` before it inserts a name, besides "input" and "replace"). When the focus was in the view that goes, it goes to the button of the one that comes. The top bar's actions are in the More menu (`more-menu.js`); `.topbar-actions` stays in the page, 0 px wide (its menus and popovers live there), with its buttons not shown. A menu whose button stops being shown while it is open (the window crossed 700 px) closes (`menu.js`). The editor toolbar is the bar at the foot (fixed, 64 px, 12 px in from each side, Run and Check answer 44 px high and sharing its width; no shortcut hints, no text size), the status bar keeps only the credit ("Made by ..." alone, on one line from 360 px; the app's name stays for a screen reader), the view buttons draw their focus ring inside them, and the toast sits above both.
- **Sizes (`mountLayout`).** The sidebar is 220 to 420 px wide (`SIDEBAR_WIDTH`, 280 initially), or hidden (`collapsed`: the column is 0, the sidebar `visibility: hidden` and `inert`, and the splitter stays as a 12 px handle with a grip); `showSidebar()` brings it back, and `index.js` calls it when a set opens (its list lives there; under 1100 px it changes nothing: the drawer does not open by itself). The editor pane, its toolbar and the editor together, takes `EDITOR_SHARE` = 0.4 of the room under the exercise prompt (the plan's "40% of the main height"), never below `EDITOR_MIN_HEIGHT` = 120 px of editor or `RESULTS_MIN_HEIGHT` = 96 px of result under its summary line (`resultsNeed()`; a Check's verdict is left out of it and pushes the rows down instead, so the prompt is not cut for it); the room is measured on `.main` minus the prompt (`measure()`), never by adding up the panes, so a window that shrinks in one step is measured right. The exercise prompt is capped at `PROMPT_SHARE` = 0.4 of the window and less on a short one, so that the toolbar, the smallest editor and the smallest result (with its summary line) keep their room (at least `PROMPT_MIN_HEIGHT` = 72 px; it scrolls inside): `--prompt-max` on `.main`, read by `css/exercises.css`. Both splitters are focusable `role="separator"`s (`aria-valuenow`, `aria-valuetext`): drag them, or the arrow keys (16 px, `SPLITTER_STEP`), Home and End; on the sidebar's, Enter or a double click hides and shows it, and a drag narrower than `SIDEBAR_HIDE_BELOW` = 160 px hides it. Their pressable area is wider than the 1 px rule, on the main area's side (16 px more on a touch screen). `--sidebar-width` is set on `.app`; `--editor-height` (px) on `.main`, recomputed by a `ResizeObserver` on `.main`, the prompt and the toolbar. The sizes are saved under `sqllab.layout` (section 12) after each key, at the end of a drag and on a double click; a stored value that makes no sense is ignored, and storage that throws leaves the sizes to this page.
- **Tabs (`mountTabs`).** The WAI-ARIA tabs pattern with automatic activation: a click, or ArrowLeft, ArrowRight, Home, End on a tab, selects it and shows its panel (`hidden` on the others); only the selected tab is a Tab stop. `onShow(tabId)` runs after each show; `index.js` redraws the History tab from it.
- **Menus.** The database switcher, Export, Theme and the exercise set picker are `mountMenu` menu buttons (`js/lab/menu.js`; Shift+Tab in an open menu goes back to its button, Tab moves on past it). Help is a non-modal `popover` dialog (`mountHelp`): its button is the invoker, the popover is placed under it (`placePopover`), `aria-expanded` follows `beforetoggle`, the focus moves into it on open, it closes when the focus moves on out of it, and the browser returns the focus on close; it lists the privacy line, the shortcuts, the link to About and the SQLite function reference, and the button is hidden in a browser without the popover attribute. `placePopover` aligns a popover with one edge of its button and sets `--popover-room`, the room to the far edge of the window, which the menus' and Help's width stay within (a popover's own width is not known before it shows); after showing, `keepInside` is a last resort. A menu entry's description is one line (cut with an ellipsis, its `title` whole); the More menu's, short sentences with no title, wrap, and so do the lines of the two actions of the database menu (`#open-file`, `#open-url`), which say what the entry takes: they are written to stay on one line from 360 px wide, and where the menu is narrower than the line it wraps and is not cut, in lines of about the same length where the browser knows `text-wrap: balance` (`tests/ui6-responsive.spec.js`, `tests/aw0-states.spec.js`). A `.button`'s children (icons, shortcut hints) take no pointer events, so a press on them is a press on the button (`js/controllers/actions.js` takes the pressed element, or its parent, as the button).
- **Database switcher.** `#database-button` shows the open database (`aria-label` "Database: <name>") and is disabled until the manifest has answered or a database is open. `#database-menu` holds a `menuitemradio` per course database (name, its description, its size; the open one `aria-checked`), a separator, then `#open-file` ("Open a file…", Ctrl/Cmd+O: clicks `#open-file-input`) and `#open-url` ("Open from URL…", Ctrl/Cmd+U: the dialog below), each showing its shortcut at its end. Choosing the course database last asked for (the open one, or one still loading) does nothing (it would discard the student's work); choosing the open one while another is still loading asks for it again, so the last choice wins (`sync()` resets this after a switch that failed). The sizes come from one `HEAD` request per database for its relative file the first time the menu opens; a size that cannot be had is left out. After an upload or open-from-URL the button names the file and no entry is ticked. The entries are rebuilt when the manifest arrives.
- **Export menu.** `#export-menu`: "Database (.db)…" (`#export-db`), "Results (.csv)" (`#export-csv`; `aria-disabled` with its line changed to "Run a query that returns rows first" unless `results.canDownload()`, checked when the menu opens) and "Evidence (.txt)…" (`#export-evidence`, section 9.3), each with one line on what it is for; with the flag `export` on (the default: `?preview=-export` leaves it out) there is one entry more, "Tables as CSV…" (`#export-tables`, section 9.7), right after "Database (.db)…". "Database (.db)…" opens `#export-dialog` (a native `<dialog>`, `showModal`): a file-name input (`#export-name`, filled with the database's name and selected), Export and Cancel. The name is also the database's name: submitting a changed name goes through `changeName` (the SQL saved for the old name moves to the new one without writing `database.query`, the page title, the switcher, the status bar and the CSV file's name follow; the run log's key was fixed at open and does not move), then `actions.exportDatabase()` saves it. A blank name keeps the current one. Either way the dialog closes and the focus goes to `#export-button`. With no database open, or an empty one (`exportProblem`, `js/lab/export.js`), the dialog does not open and a toast says why (`NO_DATABASE_MESSAGE`, `EXPORT_EMPTY_MESSAGE`); a saved file is confirmed by a toast that names it ("Exported northlight_retail.db"). A database that loads while the dialog is open closes it, so its name never lands on the new database.
- **More menu** (`more-menu.js`, a phone's). `#more-button` (an ellipsis, named "More actions") opens `#more-menu` on its first entry (`opensOn: "first"`): Copy link (`#more-copy-link`), the group "Export" (`#more-export-db`, with the flag `export` on (the default) `#more-export-tables`, `#more-export-csv`, `aria-disabled` without rows to save as in the Export menu, `#more-export-evidence`), the group "Theme" (the `menuitemradio`s `#more-theme-light`, `-dark` and `-device`, the chosen one ticked), and Help (`#more-help`). Each entry does what its own control does: Copy link's popover and Help open under the More button (`altAnchor`), and a dialog opened from it gives the focus back to the More button (the `returnFocus` that `index.js` hands the export and evidence dialogs picks whichever of Export and More is shown).
- **Text size** (`text-size.js`). A− (`#text-smaller`) and A+ (`#text-larger`) step `--code-size` on `<html>` through `TEXT_SIZES`, 12 to 24 px (14 to begin with, `--text-sm`); the editor, the result table, the error band's SQL, the long-cell popover's value and Expected and Yours are drawn at it, the interface is not. Each press stores the size (`sqllab.textSize`, section 12) and says it ("Text size 16 pixels") in `#text-size-said`; at either end the button keeps its place and the focus but carries `aria-disabled` and a title that says why. Their names start with what they show ("A−, smaller text", WCAG 2.5.3). Under 700 px they are not shown and the default size applies (a phone zooms), though the stored size stays for a wider window.
- **Open from URL.** `#open-url-dialog` (`open-url-dialog.js`, a native `<dialog>`, `showModal`, `.app-dialog`) replaced `window.prompt`: a title, one line on what it opens, the labelled field `#open-url-input` (empty every time, so no third-party address is ever suggested; `type="text"`, `inputmode="url"`, no spell check or autocomplete), a hint, `#open-url-error` (`role="alert"`), Open and Cancel. An empty field and an address `DatabasePath` calls `unsupported` are refused inline (the field `aria-invalid` and described by the error, the focus kept there, nothing requested); anything else closes the dialog and is handed to `startFromUrl` as typed, trimmed. Asked again while it is open (Ctrl+U in the field) it keeps the address being typed. Escape and Cancel give the focus back to what had it (the database button from the menu, the editor for Ctrl+U); open menus are closed first.
- **Status bar.** `#status-database` (a course database's manifest name, else the file name, also its `title`; `rename` updates it only for a non-course database), `#status-run` (`No query run yet` after a load, then `N rows in ` and the time in a `span.status-time`, for a run with no result set the summary line's own words (`resultDoneText`: `Done in `, or `Done. 3 rows changed in `) and the time, or `Last run failed`, from `lab:run-finished`), `#status-version` (`SQLite <version>`, read once from `capi.sqlite3_libversion()`) and the credit link, static markup, to About ("SQL Lab · Made by Abdou Sadou, based on SQLime by Anton Zhiyanov", the app name through `data-app-name`; its pressable area is 32 px high). The result's summary line puts its time in a `span.status-time` too (`withTime` in `results.js`): the screenshot baselines give those spans, and the History tab's times, one value before each shot.
- **Caps Lock.** Ctrl/Cmd+O and U arrive as "O" and "U" with Caps Lock on; upstream's `ShortcutController` matches the key's case, so a listener in `index.js` takes those two as the same shortcuts when Shift is not held (the browser would otherwise open its view-source).
- **Platform keys (`keys.js`).** `IS_APPLE` and `MOD_KEY` (`Ctrl`, or `⌘` on a Mac, iPhone or iPad); `showPlatformKeys()` writes `MOD_KEY` into every `[data-mod]` element (Run's and Check answer's hints, the Help list); the markup holds `Ctrl`.

### 4.5 The editor (UI plan, Phase 3)

Code: `js/components/sqlime-editor.js` (the element), `js/lab/code-editor.js` (CodeMirror's setup), `js/lab/editor-schema.js`, `js/lab/sqlite-words.js`, `css/editor.css`; the library is `vendor/editor.js`.

- **Two editors, one element.** `<sqlime-editor id="editor">` renders the plain contenteditable box (upstream's editor) and at once imports `vendor/editor.js` and `js/lab/code-editor.js` (the latter is already inside `bundle/app.js`; `vendor/editor.js` is the one import the bundle leaves a file of its own). When both arrive, `mountCode` sets CodeMirror up first (if that throws, the box is untouched), then empties the element, takes off its `contenteditable`, `role`, `aria-*`, `tabindex` and `spellcheck`, and puts CodeMirror's DOM inside, with the box's text, its caret (as text offsets) and its focus. `data-editor` says what is shown: `loading`, `code` or `plain`. `ready` is a promise that settles when CodeMirror is drawn, when it failed, or after `EDITOR_UPGRADE_WAIT_MS` (3 s; the box is shown, `plain`, and a late CodeMirror still takes over); `start()` in `index.js` waits for it alongside the database (`Promise.all`, before the ticket check), so the first text and the focus go to the editor that stays. Blocking `vendor/editor.js` leaves the box, and everything works as before Phase 3 (`tests/ui3-editor.spec.js`). The caret and a selection are carried as offsets into the box's `innerText` (`caretOffsets` counts line breaks as `innerText` does: a `<br>`, and a line block's edges between text). Text typed into the box while `start()` is still waiting is replaced by the restored query (or emptied), as before Phase 3 during a slow database load (the wait is the longer of the two).
- **The surface `index.js` and the lab modules use**, the same in both: `value` (CodeMirror: the document; setting it starts a new state, so undo never reaches back past text the app put there, and no `input` fires; an open list of names is closed first, as a Run closes it, because the new state would keep a measure the list asked for and read the list's place in the new text, which logs "RangeError: Invalid position"), `query` (the selection if the page's selection is in the editor, else all of it: CodeMirror's main selection counts only while CodeMirror has the focus or the page selection is inside its content), `focus()` (caret at the end), `clear()`, `setSchema(objects)`, `view` (the `EditorView`, or null), `code` (the object `createCodeEditor` returns: `value`, `selectedText()`, `caretToEnd()`, `insert(text, {atCaret, spaceBefore, spaceAfter})`, `setSchema`), and the events `input` (CodeMirror: fired on the element from a microtask after each document change, once; the content's own `input` events are stopped there, because they come before CodeMirror has read the change), `start` (the first `input`; nothing listens), `execute` (detail: `query`) and `replace` (the app set the text through `value`; `insert.js` then forgets the caret, which the student did not choose).
- **The DOM.** The textbox is CodeMirror's `.cm-content` (contenteditable, `role="textbox"`, `aria-multiline`, `aria-label` "SQL query", `aria-describedby="editor-hint"`, `aria-placeholder`, `spellcheck="false"`, `autocorrect`/`autocapitalize` off, and `tabindex="0"`, which changes nothing for the keyboard but tells accessibility checkers that the scrolling `.cm-scroller` holds a Tab stop): it has the focus and is the editor's one Tab stop. `#editor` also holds the line numbers (`.cm-gutters`, with a hidden spacer number) and the placeholder, so its `innerText` is not the SQL: read `value`.
- **Keys.** In a `Prec.highest` keydown handler: `leavesEditor` (Escape arms, the next Tab or Shift+Tab is left to the browser; CodeMirror's own two-second Escape mode is reset on blur, and both are cancelled by a press in the editor, so an Escape never outlives the visit or outlasts the student going on editing) and Ctrl or Cmd with Enter, any other modifier included (as the box), fires `execute` and closes the completion list. Tab inserts two spaces at an empty selection, else indents the selected lines (`indentMore`); Shift+Tab outdents; neither takes a completion. Then `defaultKeymap` without its Escape binding (`simplifySelection` would collapse the selection, and Escape is the first key of the way out to Run, which runs the selection; Ctrl/Cmd+/ toggles `-- ` comments; its Ctrl/Cmd+Enter `insertBlankLine` never runs) and `historyKeymap` (Ctrl/Cmd+Z, Ctrl+Y, Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+Z) without its Mod-U (`undoSelection`, "undo the last change or selection") and Alt-U pair: Ctrl/Cmd+U is the page's "Open from URL…", and with the binding the key also undid the student's last edit (found in Phase 4; `tests/ui4-review.spec.js`). Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+Enter with a set open is taken before CodeMirror by `exercise-panel.js` (capture phase on the document), and Ctrl/Cmd+O and U still reach `ShortcutController`.
- **Completion.** `autocompletion({override: [lang-sql's schemaCompletionSource, the columns, the words], icons: false, selectOnOpen: false, filterStrict: true})`. The schema source completes tables and views ("table" / "view") and, after `name.` or an alias and a dot, that object's columns with their types; the columns source offers every column for a word typed on its own (detail: the tables that have it, boost -1); the words are SQLite's keywords and types (capitals, boost -1) and functions ("function"); none of them inside a string, a quoted name or a comment (`ifNotIn`). A name that needs quoting is inserted as `identifierForSql` writes it, as the Schema tab does. Nothing is picked when the list opens, so Enter is a new line until the student picks a name with the arrow keys (a line ending in an alias, `FROM orders o`, would otherwise get the first name); a name already typed in full is not offered (`withoutTyped`, which filters again at once through the result's `update` as the word grows: a list waiting for a fresh answer ignores the arrow keys). lang-sql reads a name only from an ASCII letter or digit on, so its answers are dropped where it did not read what was typed (`readSoundly`): the end of a longer word (the `pr` of `__pr`, the `b` of `üb`: it would splice a name onto the rest), the columns after a name it cannot read and a dot (`café.`, `_a.`, `"a""b".`: it offers the tables), and a quote typed right after a closing quote (a quote doubled inside a name, which it takes for a new name). The editor's own sources answer instead: names that start with `_` or a letter outside ASCII are offered with the columns, and after such a name and a dot the qualified source offers that table's columns (an alias of such a table is not followed). Words are matched whole, letters of any script with their marks; no keyword or function is offered after a dot. After an opening quote lang-sql quotes the name itself without doubling a quote inside it, so `quotedRight` doubles it. The namespace has no prototype, so a table named `__proto__` is a name like any other. When the list opens CodeMirror's live region says how many names it holds and how to pick one (`completionsOpenedText`), since nothing is picked to announce; once per opening (a list that waits for a fresh answer while it is open has not opened again). `editor-schema.js` hands the names over on `lab:database-loaded` and after a run that moved `PRAGMA schema_version`; the plain box keeps them for a late CodeMirror.
- **Look.** The dialect is lang-sql's SQLite with SQLite's own keyword list and its functions as the built-ins (lang-sql's SQLite built-ins are the sqlite3 shell's dot commands, which would draw a column named `mode` as a function); case-insensitive names, double quotes first. `tagHighlighter` gives classes, not inline styles: `sql-keyword` (keywords, types, `NULL`, `TRUE`; 600), `sql-function`, `sql-string`, `sql-number`, `sql-comment` (italic), and `sql-quoted-name` for a `"quoted"` name, which stays ink (a name, not text), coloured by the `--syntax-*` tokens in `css/editor.css`, where every CodeMirror part is styled under `#editor` (CodeMirror adds its base theme to the page later, with selectors of up to five classes): 48 px of line numbers in graphite, 12 px from the SQL; 14 px code face at 1.6; the caret's line and its number on `--sunken` only while the editor has the focus (`:focus-within`, which changes with the focus itself; CodeMirror's `cm-focused` class follows a moment later), and the line not while text is selected (`sql-selecting` on `.cm-editor`, from `editorAttributes`: CodeMirror draws the selection under the text, so an opaque tint would hide it); selection and the matched bracket on `--accent-subtle` (in forced colours both the selection and the picked name are outlined in `Highlight`); the caret does not blink under `prefers-reduced-motion`; the focus ring drawn by `.cm-editor:focus-within::after` over the editor (an outline would be under the line numbers, which stay put while the SQL scrolls sideways); the completion list styled as a menu (4 px inside the list, 32 px rounded rows, 44 on a touch screen). Lines do not wrap (the mockup draws the editor with `white-space: pre` and `overflow-x: auto`): a long line scrolls sideways inside the editor, never the page, and the plain box still wraps as it always did.
- **Paste and drop.** CodeMirror pastes `text/plain` only and drops text at the drop point; a drop carrying files is cancelled and inserts nothing (`code-editor.js`), as in the box. A drag that starts in the editor is CodeMirror's move until its `dragend`; as in the box (`plain-text.js`), a pointer or key event that comes first means the drag ended without one, and `code-editor.js` then sends CodeMirror a `dragend`, so the next drop from elsewhere is not taken for that move (which would delete the dragged text).
- **The bundle.** `tools/editor-entry.js` re-exports the CodeMirror pieces used; `tools/build-editor.mjs` bundles it with esbuild (ESM, minified, the README's browser floor as target, a banner naming every package and version), checks that the packages bundled are exactly the fourteen it lists, writes `vendor/codemirror.LICENSE.txt`, and fails over 500,000 bytes. `--check` builds in memory and fails if the committed files differ (`tests/ui3-editor.spec.js` runs it). Versions are exact in `package.json`, and `@lezer/common` and `@lezer/lr` are pinned under `overrides` (each was chosen at least two weeks old).

## 5. Running SQL: `js/lab/runner.js`

- `splitStatements(capi, sql)`: cuts at each `;` where `sqlite3_complete` says the text since the last cut is whole, so a semicolon inside a string, comment or trigger body does not split and a long script is never rescanned. A `;` inside a string, a quoted or bracketed name or a comment is not even asked about: `nextSemicolon` (private) steps over those with `indexOf`, by `sqlite3_complete`'s own rules (a quote runs to the next one of its kind, `[` to `]`, `--` to the next LF only, and `*/` is searched from right after the `/*`, so `/*/` stays open; anything unclosed means no more semicolons), because asking costs a pass over the statement so far and a text value with 100,000 semicolons (a `.sql` upload goes through `runSql`) took seconds or threw. `sqlite3_complete` remains the only judge of whether a `;` ends a statement, which is what keeps trigger bodies whole (`m4-runner.spec.js` counts its calls, and a one-off differential run of 150,000 random strings against the previous splitter showed no difference). Statements are trimmed; a lone `;` is dropped, and so is text that is only whitespace, comments and semicolons (a `-- note` after the last statement is not a statement; a comment in front of a statement stays part of it, and `stripLeadingNoise` removes it where the statement is quoted back to the student). An unterminated block comment needs at least one character after its `/*` to count as a comment, and whitespace counts, as in SQLite: `/*` followed by any character is a comment, a bare `/*` as the very last two characters is a slash and a star. So `select 1; /*` keeps `/*` as a statement and the engine's `near "/": syntax error` shows, while `select 1;\n/*\n` and `select 1 /* ` run (`addStatement` trims, but keeps the one blank character after a final `/*`, both for the nothing-to-run decision and for the text executed: `select 1 /* ` is executed as written). `/* a` is a comment in both. Checked against `python3`'s `sqlite3` module (SQLite 3.54, `executescript`) on 244,268 strings built from `select 1`, `;`, blanks, `/*`, `*/`, `*`, `/`, `--`, `x`, tab, `'a'` (up to five pieces) and 177,155 more with CR, a double quote and a trigger: no difference in what runs and what errors (the first set had 1,155 differences before the change). The `sqlite3` shell is no reference: it reads line by line and drops the newline after a final `/*`. One known difference remains, kept on purpose (the lenient side, so text pasted from a page or a document runs as it looks): JS `trim()` also removes Unicode blanks such as U+00A0 at the edges of a statement, which SQLite reports as an unrecognised token. A lone UTF-16 surrogate is replaced by U+FFFD in `runSql` before the text is split (`toWellFormed`): the engine's own conversion swallows the character after it, where `sqlite3_complete` and `nextSemicolon` read U+FFFD, so the statements were cut one way and run another and a `VACUUM INTO` could hide behind a quote the engine did not see (only the authorizer refused it; `m4-runner.spec.js` pins the agreement). Text with a NUL character (U+0000) is refused whole by `runSql` with `SQL_NUL_MESSAGE` before anything runs, because the engine stops reading at a NUL and the statements after it would silently not run. It splits because the engine's `exec` collects rows for the first column-returning statement only.
- `runSql(database, sql)` -> `{columns, values}` of the last statement with result columns, or null. One `database.db.exec({sql, rowMode: "array", columnNames, resultRows})` per statement. Sets `database.query = sql`. Values are numbers, BigInt (beyond the safe range), strings, `Uint8Array` (BLOB) and null. A failing statement throws the engine's exception after `statementText`, `statementPosition` (1-based) and `statementCount` are attached to that same object (class and message unchanged); results.js names the statement from them. They hold student SQL, or the model query when the checker runs it, so the checker drops that exception.
- `engineMessage(exc)`: the engine's text without the `SQLite3Error: SQLITE_...: sqlite3 result code N:` prefix; used for the error band (as it is) and its announcement, the run log, the checker's feedback and `lab:run-finished`.
- **The guard.** The engine's heap limits and its memory filesystem are shared by every connection on the page, and a check opens fresh connections, so student SQL must not leave anything there. `runSql` installs an authorizer (`sqlite3_set_authorizer`, once per connection) that refuses `PRAGMA hard_heap_limit` / `soft_heap_limit` (a low limit survives closing the connection and made every later Check fail) and `ATTACH` of anything but `:memory:` or `''` (a file, or a `file:...?vfs=memdb` URI, would stay in memory for the page's life and be visible to later checks); the student sees "not authorized". `VACUUM INTO` a file reaches the authorizer as an `ATTACH` and would be denied there ("authorization denied"), but `isVacuumInto(statement)` (starts, after whitespace and comments, with VACUUM and says INTO outside comments and quotes, so `VACUUM /* into */` still runs) refuses it first with `VACUUM_INTO_MESSAGE`: the student is told why, and the refusal does not rest on the authorizer alone. The guard applies to every run, Run and Check alike, and to a `.sql` script loaded as a database (`manager.js` `fromSql` calls `runSql`; it used to call `database.execute`, which bypassed the guard), because a file made by a Run would reach a later Check. Temp tables and `ATTACH ':memory:'` die with the connection and stay allowed. Any new way of running student SQL must go through `runSql` or it loses the guard.
- `SQLite.execute` (`db.js`) has no caller left (the tables view that used it is gone) and keeps the old object-row, first-result-set behaviour; do not use it for user SQL.

## 6. Addresses and loading

### 6.1 `resolveRoute(location)` (`router.js`)

Never throws; returns `{database, dataset, sql, exercise, damaged, notice, problem, manifest}`. Priority, first match wins:

1. A share fragment (anything `isShareFragment` accepts; never taken for a database path). If it names an exercise set that loads and whose dataset is in the manifest, that set's dataset opens whatever dataset id the link carries. Otherwise a known dataset id opens it; an unknown or absent id opens the default with a `notice`; the SQL is restored either way (blank SQL counts as none). A damaged link opens the default with `damaged`.
2. A plain fragment (`#datasets/x.db`, `#https://...`): upstream's open-from-URL, `database` set, no dataset.
3. `?set=<id>` with optional `&ex=<id>`: opens the set and its dataset. A set that cannot load, or whose dataset is not in the manifest, adds a notice and routing goes on as if no set was named. An exercise id not in the set adds a notice and selects none. `&ex=` without a set is ignored.
4. `?db=<id>`: a known id opens it, an unknown one opens the default with a notice. An id that does not match `SHARE_ID_PATTERN` is not echoed.
5. The default dataset. If the manifest is unusable and nothing else was asked for, `dataset` is null and `problem` holds the message (empty database).

`exercise` is `{set, exerciseId}` (`exerciseId` null when none was named) or null. Notices are joined with a space. The fragment path is read raw from `location.hash.slice(1)`; there is no `hashchange` listener, `popstate` covers hash edits.

`DatabasePath.inferType` rules: empty -> `empty`; `ArrayBuffer` -> `binary`; `http(s)://` (any case) -> `remote` (a plain `fetch`, so the host must send CORS headers); the URL parser resolves it to this origin -> `local` (`fetch(value)` resolved against the page, so subpath-safe); anything else -> `unsupported` (refused by `start`, no request). The URL parser decides on purpose: a check for `:` or `//` would miss `\\host\x` and `HTTPS://`. Both `local` and `remote` take the `.sql` branch if the whole string ends in `.sql`. `extractName` is the last `/` segment (a query string stays in it); binary, sql, unsupported and empty give `""`, which `SQLite` turns into `new.db`.

Open question for the owner, not changed: a link can carry `#https://some.host/x.db`, which makes the page fetch that host. That is upstream's open-from-URL, kept by the spec, but it lets a crafted link cause one third-party request.

### 6.2 Share links (F3)

Format `#s=<payload>`, `payload = LZString.compressToEncodedURIComponent(JSON.stringify({v:1, d, x, e, q}))` with absent fields omitted (`d` dataset id, `x` exercise set id, `e` exercise id, `q` SQL). The link is the page URL with query and fragment replaced, so it is valid under any subpath and the SQL never reaches a server log. A blank editor omits `q`; an incoming blank `q` counts as none.

Reading is lenient about the start (`SHARE_START`: `#S=`, `#s%3D`, `#?s=`, `# s=`); a fragment that looks like one but does not decode is `damaged`, never a database path, so its payload is neither requested nor printed. `decodeShare` treats the fragment as hostile and returns `{ok:false, reason}` for: `payload` (empty, a character outside the alphabet, a bad `%` escape, over `SHARE_MAX_PAYLOAD_CHARS` = 100,000, or a decompressed JSON over `SHARE_MAX_JSON_CHARS` = 400,000, checked before parsing because lz-string expands a few KB into hundreds of MB), `decompress`, `json`, `shape`, `version` (`v` not 1), `id` (not `SHARE_ID_PATTERN`), `sql` (over `SHARE_MAX_SQL_CHARS` = 200,000). Unknown keys are ignored. Copy link refuses to build a link that would exceed the limit. For an upload, open-from-URL or the empty database the link carries no dataset and the status says so (`NOT_IN_LINK_MESSAGE`).

### 6.3 The dataset manifest (F1)

`datasets/manifest.json`, fetched by the relative URL `MANIFEST_URL` with `cache: "no-cache"`. `validateManifest` accepts it only if: the top level is an object with a non-empty `datasets` list; every entry is an object with a unique `id` matching `SHARE_ID_PATTERN`, a non-blank `name`, and a `file` that is a relative same-origin path (no leading or trailing space, control character, backslash or leading `/`, no scheme, no `..` segment even percent-encoded, and the URL parser resolves it to this origin); `description`, if present, text; `default`, if present, a boolean; and exactly one entry has `default: true`. Otherwise `{ok:false, message}` with the specific reason, shown in the error band; the app starts on an empty database. Unknown keys are ignored. `samples` (the UI plan's sample queries, optional) is a list of `{label, sql}`: an entry that is not two non-blank pieces of text is left out with a `console.warn` for the instructor, and a `samples` that is not a list gives no samples (the first table's rows are offered instead): a teaching aid does not make the manifest invalid. The label is trimmed, the SQL kept as written (line breaks included); the results pane shows the first three (section 8). The shipped manifest lists Northlight Retail (`northlight`), Home Store (`home-store`, the default since 2026-11-12) and Shopping (`shopping`), in that order. Northlight's samples are the first-visit mockup's: "Every product", "Orders per store" and "Revenue share by category" (the mockup's CTE). The `.db` is fetched by its relative `file` and must start with the 16-byte SQLite header, so a server answering a missing file with an HTML page is an ordinary failure. A dataset's database name is the basename of its file, which is also its `sqlime.query.<name>` slot.

## 7. Exercise mode

### 7.1 Sets (`exercises.js`)

- `validateSet(data, expectedId)` -> `{ok, set}` / `{ok:false, message}`. Rules: `id` matches `SHARE_ID_PATTERN` and equals the file name; `title` (the set's too) non-blank; `dataset` an id; `exercises` a non-empty list with unique ids; per exercise `title`, `prompt`, `model_query` non-blank text, `hints` optional list of text (default `[]`), `order_matters` and `check_column_names` optional booleans (default false); unknown keys dropped. Messages are worded for the instructor and echo only ids that matched the pattern.
- `loadSet(id)`: id checked first (no request otherwise), then `fetch("exercises/<id>.json", {cache: "no-cache"})`. Never throws. A success is remembered for the page; a failure is retried.
- **Deploy-time listing (decision).** GitHub Pages cannot list a folder, and adding a set must stay "commit one JSON file", so the app discovers sets by reading the folder page `exercises/`: `listSets()` fetches it (`cache: "no-cache"`) and reads the links with `DOMParser`. `python3 -m http.server` writes such a page itself; the Pages workflow step "List exercise sets" (after staging, before the upload) writes `.site-stage/exercises/index.html`: a minimal page with one `<a href="x.json">x.json</a>` per `*.json` file (names HTML-escaped, and `%` written `%25` in the href because the reader percent-decodes every href, as a real server's listing expects; `nullglob` so an empty folder gives no links, `LC_ALL=C` for a stable order). `tests/static-server.mjs` answers `exercises/` with the same text (`exercisesIndexHtml`) and `m4-listing.spec.js` runs the step's script and compares. Not a manifest: a second list would have to be edited by hand. Only an href whose entire value, percent-decoded, is `<id>.json` is an id (`idsInListing`), and the fetched URL is rebuilt from the id, so a listing cannot send a request off the site. The other plain `.json` file names of the folder (no `/` or `\` once decoded, not matching the id pattern: `Week 3.json`, `week1.v2.json`) come from `badNamesInListing`, cut to 80 characters, and `listSets` makes them `{id: <name>, ok: false, problem}` entries (`SET_BAD_FILE_NAME_MESSAGE`) with no request; that `id` is only a name to show. No listing (404, error, junk) means `[]`; `?set=<id>` and share links do not need one. Entries (ids and unusable names together) are sorted by id, `{id, ok:true, set}` or `{id, ok:false, problem}`, at most `MAX_LISTED_SETS` (the sets first; the unusable names only fill the room the sets leave, so badly named files cannot push working sets out of the menu). The app asks for the folder at every page start without awaiting it.
- **`model_query` is in the loaded set** (the checker needs it, and a student can read it in the fetched JSON; spec section 5 accepts that for formative practice, F9 is the stricter variant and is not built). It must never reach the DOM, an attribute, a message, a URL, the run log or the evidence file by any route: render `title`, `prompt` and `hints` with `textContent`, and never copy exercise objects wholesale into `dataset` attributes or a share link. `m4-panel.spec.js` searches the DOM after loading, failing, passing and every hint for all ten shipped exercises; `m5-evidence-dialog.spec.js` checks the log, the evidence file, the page and storage after a Check.

### 7.2 The checker (`checker.js`)

- Pure parts: `normalizeValue` (INTEGER and TEXT exact, REAL rounded to 6 dp, NULL equals only NULL, BLOB by hex, BigInt exact; an integer-valued number is an integer, so INTEGER 3 equals REAL 3.0; tagged JSON forms `{"$int"}`, `{"$blob"}`, `{"$real": "Infinity" | "-Infinity" | "NaN"}` keep types apart), `normalizeRow`, `serializeRow`, `displayValue`, `compareResults(expected, got, {orderMatters, checkColumnNames})`. `order_matters` false: each normalised row serialised, strings sorted by code unit, arrays compared; true: sequences. Column count is always compared; names (case-insensitive, in order) only with `check_column_names`.
- `createChecker()` -> `{check(set, exercise, sql), clearCache()}`, one per page (made in `mountExercises`). `check` never throws and resolves to:
  - `{status:"pass", rows}`: `rows` is how many rows matched, for the banner's "Correct. 12 rows match." (the UI plan's Phase 5 added it; nothing about what passes changed)
  - `{status:"fail", kind, message, ...}`; kind and spec rule: `sql` (1; `message` = `engineMessage`), `columns` (2; "expected N columns, got M"; a statement list with no result columns is M = 0), `column-name` (3; plus `position`, `expected`, `got`), `rows` (4; "expected N rows, got M"), `row` (5; plus `rowIndex` 1-based, `sorted` true when the index counts rows after sorting, `columns`, `gotColumns`, `expected` and `got`: at most `DIFF_MAX_ROWS` = 3 display-string rows from `rowIndex`; `message` is multi-line plain text and can hold student text).
  - `{status:"unavailable", reason, message}`: `model` (the model query fails or returns no columns: an authoring bug; the generic `CHECK_BROKEN_MESSAGE`, nothing about the query. For the instructor the console gets one warning, `exercise "<set>/<id>" cannot be checked: its model query fails: <engineMessage>` or `... returns no result set`; only the engine's own message with every quoted piece in it replaced by `"…"` (`withoutQuotedText`: SQLite echoes a double-quoted word it cannot find as a column, and a model query can be one), never the query text or the exception's `statementText`; the engine's own step-time warning is cut down to its result code by `engine-guard.js`), `dataset` (the open dataset is not the set's, or no built-in dataset is open: `CHECK_NO_DATASET_MESSAGE`) or `internal` (a connection could not be opened, or anything unexpected; `CHECK_INTERNAL_MESSAGE` says to reload).
- **How it runs.** The pristine bytes are copied (`getPristineBytes()`, two copies taken before any await) and each query gets its own connection through `manager.init(name, new DatabasePath(bytes, "binary"))` (names `model.db`, `check.db`), both closed in `finally`. SQL goes through `runSql` (the guard applies). The model result is cached per `set id/exercise id` (the entry also holds the query and dataset, so an edited file is not served from it); a failure is not cached. The model runs first, so a broken exercise reports `unavailable` even when the student's SQL has an error. Tests count connections by wrapping `manager.init` (it is called as a property).
- **Known limits (decisions).** INTEGER 3 and REAL 3.0 compare equal (friendlier: `AVG` returning 450.0 passes against 450). Two TEXT values that are different invalid UTF-8 sequences compare equal. A result in the right order passes even without an `ORDER BY` the prompt asks for, when the engine happens to emit it so (for example `GROUP BY` on a text key, e08; its hint says ORDER BY is what guarantees it). No timeout exists for a runaway query, in Run or Check.

### 7.3 Progress and saved exercise SQL (`progress.js`)

One localStorage key per set (section 12), holding passes and **per-exercise saved SQL** ("drafts"). Decision: the SQL a student last wrote for each exercise lives in the set's progress record, not in the dataset's free-query slot (`sqlime.query.<name>`), so moving between exercises or reloading never loses work and one exercise's SQL never appears in another. It is saved on every `input` event of the editor, by every Run and Check (the full editor text), and never for text that arrived in a link or was set by the app (those fire no `input`). Opening an exercise shows its saved SQL or nothing; exercise mode never reads the free-query slot (a Run in exercise mode still writes it, because `execute` is shared, so leaving exercise mode can show that SQL as "your last query"). The Reset progress button (`resetSet`) clears passes and saved SQL of that set, not the run log.

`isPassed`, `markPassed`, `passedCount(setId, exerciseIds?)` (pass the set's ids so an exercise removed from the file is not counted), `getDraft`, `saveDraft` (blank removes), `resetSet`. Passing is sticky. Corrupt or foreign stored JSON reads as empty; a storage exception never escapes: that set's progress then lives in memory, and `reportStorage("progress", false)` makes `storage-notice.js` say so. Every write (and `resetSet`) tries `localStorage` first, also for a set that failed before, so when there is room again the set goes back to storage and the notice goes (it stays while any other set is still only in memory). **That first successful write merges what is stored into the set held in memory**, because another tab may have stored passes meanwhile and passing is sticky: the union of passes, and a stored draft only for an exercise this page holds no draft for and did not empty meanwhile (`emptiedDrafts`: a draft the student deleted while the set was in memory is not brought back from the older stored copy; a pass held in memory does come back even if another tab reset the set, because passing is sticky) (without it, memory `{e1}` + `markPassed(e4)` overwrote a stored `{e2, e3}` with `{e1, e4}`). A stored value that could not be read (`getItem` threw) is not written over, also for a set that was not in memory before: `read` marks the empty state it answers with `unread`, and `write` then merges what is stored first, as it does for a set held in memory. If storage still cannot be read the throw puts the set in memory (later writes merge what is stored once it can be read). A set that was read normally is not merged (reads go to storage every time). A `resetSet` whose `removeItem` throws keeps an empty state in memory and reports `reportStorage("progress", false)` like `write`, and notes the set in `pendingResets`: the copy still stored is the one being reset, so the next successful write skips the merge and overwrites it, which completes the reset (a successful `setItem` or `removeItem` clears the mark). Ids that do not match the id pattern are ignored.

### 7.4 The panel (`exercise-panel.js`, `css/exercises.css`)

- **Wiring.** `mountExercises({pickerSlot, ui, execute, isLoading, openSet, closeSet, showTab, showIdle, modeChanged, say})` -> `{editorSql, open, close, syncPicker, isOpen}`. It follows `lab:database-loaded` (`detail.exercise` opens the panel and calls `showTab()`, which brings the Exercises tab forward; null closes it, so choosing a database, an upload, open-from-URL, back/forward and a failed-set route all close it) and `lab:run-finished` (saves the draft). Every change of set or exercise calls `setShareContext({set, exercise})`; closing clears both.
- **Two places.** The **Exercises tab** (`#panel-exercises > .exercise-set`) holds the set picker, a progress block (`#exercise-progress`, `7 of 10 complete`, and a bar), `#exercise-list`, `#exercise-reset` and, while no set is open, the `.exercise-empty` text. The **prompt panel** `#exercise-panel` sits in `main#main` above `#editor-toolbar` (it scrolls past 40vh) and holds `#exercise-prompt`, `.exercise-controls` (`#exercise-hint-button` at the left, the group `#exercise-nav` "Move between exercises" with `#exercise-previous` and `#exercise-next` at the right), `#exercise-hints` (under the buttons, so Show hint stays where it is pressed) and `#exercise-announce` (docs/design/mockup/exercise-correct.html).
- **Set picker.** A menu button, `#exercise-set-picker` (`aria-label` "Exercise set: <title or None>"), and its menu `#exercise-set-menu` (`role="menu"`, `mountMenu`), both created into `.exercise-set-picker-slot`. Entries are `menuitemradio`s with `data-set-id`: "None", then `listSets()` in id order (code unit; the labels are the titles); a set that failed validation, or a file whose name cannot be an id, is an `aria-disabled` entry `<id>.json (not valid)` with the problem on a second line and as its title (text and `title` only; the select handler looks a set up among the `ok` entries, so such an entry opens nothing); a set opened by address but not listed is added. The menu is not rebuilt while it is open; the button is disabled while "None" is the only entry. Choosing the open entry does nothing; another calls `openSet(set)`, "None" `closeSet()`.
- **List and prompt.** `#exercise-list` is an `<ol>` of `button.exercise-item` (a ticked or an empty circle, the number from a CSS counter, the title) with `aria-label` "Exercise 3 of 10: <title>, passed|not passed" and `aria-current` on the active one; roving tabindex (one Tab stop; arrows, Home, End). `#exercise-prompt` shows the title (`h2#exercise-prompt-title`), "Exercise N of M" and the prompt (`white-space: pre-line`). `#exercise-hint-button` reveals one hint at a time into `#exercise-hints` (revealed counts kept per page) and counts them: "Show hint (1 of 3)" is the hint a press shows, of how many; it goes once all are shown (the focus moves to the last hint if it was on the button), and a new hint is scrolled into the capped panel's view. The hints' numbers are a CSS counter on the panel's left edge, each hint's lines hanging after its number (`role="list"` in the markup keeps the list a list without markers). `#exercise-previous` and `#exercise-next` (`refreshNav`) open the exercise before and after in the set's order through `select`, as the list does (the focus stays on the button); each one's `title` names its target ("Exercise 4 of 10: <title>"); at an end of the set it stays in place with `aria-disabled="true"` and a title saying it is the first or the last (not `disabled`: a focused disabled button drops the focus). `#exercise-reset` asks `confirm()`; `#exercise-announce` is a polite live region that speaks the prompt when the exercise changes.
- **Check.** `#exercise-check` ("Check answer", `button--primary`, with the platform's shortcut hint) is added right after `#execute` in `#editor-toolbar` when a set opens and removed when it closes; while it is there the toolbar has `editor-toolbar--exercise`, which draws Run as the plain outlined button (one filled primary per context). It has a plain click handler, not `ActionController`, which disables the button and loses focus. `runCheck`: empty editor -> "write a query first"; loading -> `execute()` refuses with its own message; else `execute(editor.query)` (the visible run, logged once; shown in the results pane but not said, the verdict is) then `checker.check(...)` whether the run failed or not (a run that `execute` dropped, because a database began loading in its frame, checks nothing); a result is dropped if `generation` changed (another exercise, set or database); a pass calls `markPassed`. Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+Enter is caught by a capture-phase `keydown` listener on `document` while a set is open and stopped, so the editor's Ctrl+Enter handler does not also run.
- **The feedback banner** (`showVerdict`; the UI plan's Phase 5, docs/design/mockup/exercise-correct.html and exercise-wrong.html). `#exercise-feedback` is first in `#results-pane`, above the state the pane shows, and is not a live region: `showVerdict` hands the banner's words to `say` (the results pane's `#results-announce`), so a verdict is said once, without its tables. It holds `div.verdict.verdict--<kind>`: `.verdict-band`, a band across the pane (no border, `--success-subtle`, `--danger-subtle`, `--warning-subtle` or `--sunken`) with the icon (`.verdict-mark`), `.verdict-text` (`p.verdict-head` with `strong.verdict-headline`, then on the same line `p.verdict-message`, then lines of their own: `.verdict-complete`, `.verdict-note`) and, after a pass, `button.verdict-next` at its end. Kinds: `pass` "Correct." and `checkPassMessage(rows)` ("5 rows match.", "1 row matches.", "No rows, as expected."), the tick drawn inline from the sprite's `circle-check` shapes (`drawnTick`: `pathLength="1"`, the circle stroked in over 200 ms, then the tick over 150 ms, 300 ms in all; reduced motion draws it at once), and Next exercise (the first unpassed exercise after the current one, wrapping; its title names it; it moves the focus to the editor) or "All N exercises complete. Well done."; `fail` "Not quite." with a `circle-x`: the checker's sentence (`.verdict-message--checker`, drawn with a capital by `::first-letter` and, when it has no full stop of its own, one by `::after`: the text stays the checker's) or, for kind `sql`, the engine's message as it is (`span.verdict-engine`, mono); when the visible run failed with that same message, its error band under the banner already shows it with the statement, so the banner says "This query has an error, shown below." (`CHECK_SQL_ERROR_BELOW`) and the message is what is said (`runCheck` keeps what `execute` rejected with). For kind `sql` after a visible run that worked, the note that the query ran on the working copy while Check uses the original (`CHECK_COPY_NOTE`); `warning` "Not checked." with `circle-alert` (the checker's `unavailable` message); `info` "Nothing to check." with `info` (an empty editor). The banner fades in over 120 ms (`menu-fade`; not with reduced motion). Colour is never the only signal: each kind has its icon and its words. A new banner where the student had scrolled the results pane past the old one sets the pane back to its top (a phone's pane does not scroll). When the focus was in the banner (Next exercise, a table's box) and a Check from the keyboard redraws it, the new banner goes in before the old one goes and the focus moves across: to its Next exercise, else to the banner (`tabindex="-1"`, its ring drawn inside); moved from the page itself, the focus would not show its ring.
- **Expected and Yours** (`compareView`, `side`): for kind `row`, under the band, `.verdict-diff` holds two `.verdict-side` columns side by side (stacked when narrow): the checker's rows (at most 3, from the first that differs, numbered in a graphite `th.verdict-row-number` column headed "Row") in `table.verdict-table`, ruled like the result table (2 px ink rules above the header and under the last row, 1 px under the header, hairlines between rows, numbers right-aligned: a column whose shown values all read as numbers, NULL aside), captioned "Expected" and "Yours", each in a `role="region"` scroll box named after it (`.verdict-scroll`, a Tab stop only while it overflows, positioned so the hidden words below stay in it). The cells of Yours whose text differs from Expected's get `.verdict-cell--differs` (a 2 px `--danger` outline) and a visually hidden " (differs)"; when the first row shown reads the same (the difference is past the 60-character cut or in a value's type: text, a number or NULL), that whole row is outlined and a second note says why (`verdictHiddenDiffNote`). The two boxes scroll sideways together (`linkScroll`, which does not pass a box's own echo back), and a first outlined cell out of its box's window is scrolled into it when the view is drawn (`revealDifference`). "Outlined cells differ from the expected result." sits under Yours; "These tables are wider than the screen..." under both while one overflows. The student's own result stays under the banner as after any run.

## 8. The results pane (F5 and the UI plan's Phase 4: `results.js`, `result-table.js`, `toast.js`)

`mountResults({ui, pane, summary, getDatabase, getSamples, isExercise})` -> `view`; `index.js`'s `showResult` / `showError` delegate to it, the load flow calls `showLoading` / `showEmpty` / `showProblem`, and `execute` takes the `error` of `lab:run-finished` from `errorText(exc)`. Styles: `css/result.css` (the mockups `main-light.html`, `first-visit.html`, `error.html` and `phone.html`). Everything shown is built with `createElement` and `textContent`.

- **One state at a time.** `#results-pane[data-state]` is `loading`, `empty` (no result yet), `result`, `done` (a run with no result set), `error` (the band of a failed run, or a load problem with no database open) or `report` (an import's report, section 9.6: `showReport`). `#results-summary` (the summary line: `#status`, a plain `<p>`, and Download CSV) shows only for `result`, `done` and `report`. A live region the module adds, `#results-announce` (`role="status"`, polite, visually hidden), says each outcome once, ~60 ms after it (emptied first, so the same words after the next run are said again; a newer outcome within those 60 ms replaces the older one): "5 rows returned", "906 rows returned. The first 500 are shown.", "Done." / "Done. 3 rows changed.", "This query has an error: <message>", a problem's text, or, for an empty Run, the "no result yet" heading. A Check's run says nothing (`quiet`): the verdict is what is said, through `say(text)` (`exercise-panel.js` calls it with the banner's words). Every change of state empties it, so it never says "1 row returned" beside a pane that shows something else. `#status` is not a live region any more.
- **A load that fails keeps the view.** `showLoading` keeps what the pane showed (its nodes, state, summary line and the CSV on offer, never the "Running…" of a run the load overtook; the first load of a series keeps it, a load that takes over from another keeps the same). When the load fails while a database stays open (a pick whose fetch fails, a file or address the engine refuses, back/forward to a dataset that cannot be fetched) or a set cannot be opened, `loadFailed(message)` puts that view back and shows the band above it, at the top of the pane (`.results-problem`, a pane child before the summary line), says it, and lets the next state remove it; a load given up (a set closed while its database loads) `loadCancelled()`, the view back with no band. With no database open there is nothing to keep: the band is drawn in `#result` (`showProblem`). A set that cannot be opened is not a load of its own (`loadFailed(message, {ownLoad: false})`): a load on its way keeps its loading state, with the band above it. Before, a failed switch replaced the student's result with "no result yet"; the result is still true of the database that stays open.
- **Summary line.** `resultRowsText` "5 rows in ", `resultCappedText` "Showing 500 of 12,400 rows in " (the time stays, as the build spec's F5 asks), `resultDoneText` "Done in " / "Done. 3 rows changed in ", each followed by the time in a `span.status-time` (`withTime`). Counts have thousands separators (`countText`).
- **Row cap.** `ROW_LIMIT` = 500. The table shows the first 500 rows; exactly 500 is not capped. Every count (summary, announcement, `lab:run-finished.rows`) is the full count. The checker never sees the table: it compares full results. The cap bounds the DOM, not memory or time.
- **The ruled table** (`buildResultTable`, the plan's section 3): `table.result-table`, `thead th[scope=col]` with the names exactly as returned (duplicates kept, `white-space: pre`), `tbody`. A 2 px ink rule above the header and under the last row, a 1 px ink rule under the header (the mockup's; the plan says "a 1 px rule"), hairline `--rule` between rows, no vertical lines, no stripes; mono 14 px at 1.6 with tabular figures; cell padding 6 px with a 40 px gutter on one side (24 px under 700 px, as the phone mockup), the first column 4 px from the rules. A column whose shown values are all numbers or BigInts, NULLs aside, is a number column (`.num`): its heading and cells are right-aligned; one text value makes it a text column. NULL reads `NULL` in graphite italics (`span.cell-null`; the CSV still writes an empty field, and an empty string is still an empty cell). Cells keep their line breaks and runs of spaces and never wrap (`white-space: pre`): a wide table scrolls sideways in `#result` instead of squeezing its columns. The header is sticky (`top` minus the box's 4 px padding, so no row shows above it); the borders are separate, not collapsed, so the rules travel with it. A zero-row result draws the header with the closing rule (`.result-table--empty`); Download CSV stays hidden for it. A value longer than `CELL_MAX_CHARS` = 60 characters shows its first 60 and `…` in a `button.cell-more` (`popovertarget="cell-popover"`, `aria-expanded`, described by `#cell-popover-hint` "Shows the whole value."). Characters are grapheme clusters (`Intl.Segmenter`, code points where it is missing): a flag, an emoji with a skin tone or a family, a letter with a combining accent are one character and are never cut apart; only a value of more than 60 UTF-16 units is segmented, and only as far as the cut, which also stops at 960 units (`MAX_SHOWN_UNITS`, never between the halves of a surrogate pair): one cluster of thousands of combining marks or ZWJs is still cut. The whole cell is the button's target (its `::after` covers the cell, which is `position: relative`): 34 px high, and on a touch screen the row of a long value grows to 44 px. `mountCellPopover` fills `#cell-popover` (a non-modal popover dialog named "<column>, row N" and described by its length line, the length in characters as above, counted when it opens; its content is the whole value, selectable, scrolling past 24rem, and is emptied when it closes, so a value of megabytes is not kept) before the browser opens it, places it under the cell (`placePopover`), moves the focus into it, closes on Escape, a press outside or the focus leaving, and gives the focus back to the cell; a press on another long cell while it is open shows that one. It follows its cell (placed again, once a frame at most, on any scroll, a resize, or a change of `#result`'s size: a placement made from a layout that was still settling would stay where the cell no longer is). Any new state closes it. In the result state `#result` has `scroll-padding-top` the height of the sticky header (`--result-head`, measured by a `ResizeObserver` on the table's head: a column name with line breaks, which an unaliased multi-line expression has, makes it taller than a line), so a control the focus moves to (Shift+Tab back up through long cells) is scrolled clear of the header, not under it.
- **Several statements.** As before: the last statement with result columns is shown (zero rows included), one summary line per run, the time for the whole run (4.2).
- **Done.** A run whose statements returned no result set: the summary line alone, "Done in 2 ms", or "Done. 3 rows changed in 2 ms" (`changes`, 4.2); no table. The status bar says the same.
- **The error band** (`errorBand`): `.results-band` on `--danger-subtle` with a `circle-alert` icon (20 px, `--danger`, `aria-hidden`), no coloured edge (the plan: the same band pattern as exercise feedback); `h2.results-band-title` `ERROR_HEADING` "This query has an error", `p.results-band-message` SQLite's message as it is (`engineMessage`, mono, wrapped), and for a statement of the run `p.results-band-where` (`errorStatementText`: "In statement 1" for a one-statement run, the mockup's wording, "In statement 2 of 3" otherwise) and `pre.results-band-quote` on `--paper`: the statement as written, its line breaks kept and long lines wrapped (so the box never scrolls), without the comments in front of it (`stripLeadingNoise`: they would make it look commented out), cut at `STATEMENT_QUOTE_CHARS` = 2,000 characters (code points) with `…`. The table is cleared. Messages of the app's own (VACUUM INTO, a NUL character) come through the same band.
- **A problem that is not a query's** (`showProblem(message)`, and `showEmpty({problem})`): a load that failed (`loadFailedMessage`), a damaged link, the manifest, a dataset that could not be fetched, a set that could not be opened, a Run with no database open. `.results-band--problem` with the message alone (`p.results-band-text`, the interface face), at the top of `#result`, and under it "no result yet" of the database that is open, if one is (state `empty`; `error` with none open).
- **No result yet** (`showEmpty({variant, notice, problem})`; `refreshEmpty()` draws it again when exercise mode begins or ends): `.results-empty` (max 760 px, 32 px by 24 px of padding): `h2` `EMPTY_TITLE` "Run a query to see its result here.", then lines: a router notice (`.results-empty-notice`, in ink), `RESTORED_MESSAGE` or `SHARED_MESSAGE` for those variants, in exercise mode `EMPTY_EXERCISE_TEXT` (and no samples: they would replace the exercise's draft), then `EMPTY_SAMPLES_TEXT` and `ul.results-samples` (`aria-label` "Sample queries") of `button.results-sample` (a 200 px label in 600 and the SQL on one line, cut with an ellipsis; 44 px rows; the button's name is what it shows, WCAG 2.5.3, so its SQL text is cut at `SAMPLE_SQL_CHARS` = 120 characters with `…`, more than the line ever shows, and a long sample is not read out whole; its label is cut at `SAMPLE_LABEL_CHARS` = 60). `getSamples()` is `samplesFor(openDataset, database)`: the manifest's `samples` (6.3), at most `MAX_SAMPLES` = 3; for a database without them, its first table's first rows (`firstRowsSampleLabel`, `SELECT * FROM <name> LIMIT 10;`, the name quoted only when it needs it, read again each time); none for a database with no table, which shows `EMPTY_DATABASE_TEXT` and links to About and to SQLite's function reference instead (the old welcome's). Choosing a sample calls `ui.editor.fill(sql)`: the editor's whole text replaced as one edit the student can undo (CodeMirror: a transaction with `userEvent: "input"`; the plain box: `selectAll` + `insertText`), the focus and the caret at its end, nothing run. An empty Run shows "no result yet" again.
- **Loading** (`showLoading({name, size})` -> `{name(n), progress(loaded, total)}`): `.results-loading` with `h2#results-loading-title` (`loadingTitle`: "Loading Northlight Retail", the file name for an upload or a URL, "Loading the database" until the route is known; a long name with no space breaks rather than widening the pane), `.results-loading-size` ("43 KB of 86 KB") and `progress.results-progress` labelled by the title. The bar fills from `fetchDataset`'s `onProgress`; with no size (a URL the engine fetches, a compressed body, more bytes than announced) it waits empty and still (nothing animates but menus, toasts and the feedback banner). In forced colours it draws an edge in `CanvasText` and its fill in `Highlight` (`forced-color-adjust: none`), since backgrounds are dropped there. A load that is no longer the latest stops updating it.
- **Running…** (`startRun()` -> `end()`): `.results-running` (`aria-hidden`; `RUNNING_TEXT`) over the summary line, or with no summary line over the whole of `#result` with the words where the summary line would be; `opacity` animates from 0 after a 300 ms delay (120 ms fade, 1 ms with reduced motion), `will-change: opacity`, so the compositor draws it while the run blocks the page (4.2). A screen reader is not told: the page cannot speak during the run, and the outcome is announced after.
- **Download CSV.** `#download-csv` ("Download CSV", with a download icon, `button--quiet`) at the right of the summary line, `hidden` unless the table of a result with at least one row is on screen. The Export menu's "Results (.csv)" calls the same `download()` and is enabled by `canDownload()`. `showResult` keeps the full result and the printed table; a `MutationObserver` on `#result` retires the button when that table leaves, whichever code path removed it, `lab:database-loaded` retires it, and `download()` checks again before saving. The file is `csvBytes(full result)` via `saveFile`, named `csvFileName(database.name)` (sanitised like the database export, `.db`/`.sqlite`/`.sqlite3` dropped, plus `-result.csv`); a toast says "Exported <file>", and a failure `CSV_FAILED_MESSAGE` (a problem toast).
- **`toCsv`** is RFC 4180: comma, CRLF after every record including the last, a header row, a field quoted when it holds a comma, quote, CR or LF, quotes doubled. NULL and the empty string are both empty fields; numbers are the JS value's shortest text; a BigInt is its digits; a BLOB is `x'<hex>'` (`cellText` in `cell-text.js`, which the result table on screen uses too). Duplicate column names are kept.
- **Toasts** (`toast(message, {tone})`, `css/toast.css`): one card at a time at the bottom centre, over the status bar (16 px from the bottom under 700 px, where the page scrolls), on `--paper` with a rule, the float radius and the float shadow, with an icon for its tone: a tick (`info`, the default: something was done), an `info` "i" (`note`: nothing was done, and why: a Run refused while a database loads, nothing to export yet, an empty database not exported, a query too long for a link) or a `circle-alert` in `--danger` (`problem`: something failed: a CSV, database or evidence file that could not be saved, no checksum available), fading in over 120 ms (not with reduced motion). It shows ~60 ms after the call in `#toast-region` (a polite live region in `index.html` from the start, outside `.app`, `pointer-events: none`), emptied first so the same words twice are said twice, and goes after `TOAST_MS` = 4 s, a problem after 8 s, a long message after 300 ms a word. Used for the answer of an action (the plan names link copied, file exported and database loaded): "Link copied", "Exported <file>" (the database, the CSV, the evidence with its query count), "Opened <name>" after a load the student asked for (not the page's first), and the refusals and failures of those actions (a link too long, an empty or failed export, no checksum, nothing to export yet), a Run refused while a database loads, and a query put back from the History tab (the result on screen keeps its summary line). A load that failed is a band (its message can carry an engine's detail worth reading), never a toast.
- **Decisions.** No formula escaping in the CSV: a cell starting with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` is written unchanged, because this is a data export for analysis and altering cells would corrupt the data (the data is the student's own query of a teaching dataset or their own upload). `csvBytes` prefixes a UTF-8 byte order mark only when the text has a non-ASCII character (Excel misreads non-ASCII UTF-8 without one; plain ASCII stays byte-clean). The first visit runs nothing and offers samples (4.1). A long value opens in a popover rather than growing its row: the table keeps its shape, and a value of thousands of characters stays readable.

## 9. Run log and evidence export (F6, F11)

Files: `run-log.js`, `evidence.js`, `evidence-dialog.js`, `verify.html` + `verify-page.js`, `css/run-log.css`, `css/evidence.css`, `css/verify.css`. Texts and limits are in `config.js` (`RUN_LOG_*`, `HISTORY_*`, `STUDENT_ID_PATTERN`, `EVIDENCE_*`, `VERIFY_*`). Spec section 5a is the format. Events and the version 2 file (the assessment plan's Phase 0) add `events.js`, with its settings in `config-workflow.js`: section 9.5.

### 9.1 The run log (`run-log.js`)

- **What is logged.** One entry per Run: `lab:run-finished` with `preview` false. A Check performs its visible run through the same `execute`, so it is logged once; the checker's own runs go through `runSql` and announce nothing, so the model query cannot reach the log. Entry: `{t, sql, rows}` or `{t, sql, error}`; `t` is `utcTimestamp()` (`new Date()`, second precision, so a test controls it with `page.clock.setFixedTime`), `sql` as `execute` ran it (trimmed, never cut), `rows` the full count, `error` the engine's message on one line.
- **The log key.** `logKeyFor(dataset, databaseName)`: the dataset id for a built-in dataset, else `file:<database name>`. It is fixed when the database opens (a `WeakMap` database -> key filled on `lab:database-loaded`), so renaming a database in the export dialog does not split its log. Two uploads with the same file name share a log. `isDatasetKey(key)` (matches `SHARE_ID_PATTERN`, which a `file:` key never does) says whether a share link can exist.
- **Storage.** Key `sqllab.log.<log key>`, value `{v: 1, entries}`, oldest first, at most `RUN_LOG_MAX_ENTRIES` = 200. An append is synchronous: `appendLog` rereads the stored log, adds the entry and calls `setItem` before it returns, and `execute` announces `lab:run-finished` in the same task that shows the result, so a Run that is on screen is already in `localStorage`. A page left at once (a link, a reload, a closed tab) loses nothing; `readLog` reads storage (or the in-memory copy, below) and there is nothing to wait for in a test. **What is promised:** every Run is kept, and two tabs that run at human spacing (tests use tabs 25 ms apart) lose nothing, because each write rereads what the other tab stored. **What is not:** reading and writing one key is not atomic across tabs, so two tabs that append within the same few milliseconds can overwrite each other's entry (a test that ran 90 appends in each of two tabs at once stored 117 of 180 in Chromium); a person cannot run two queries that close. **No Web Lock (decision).** An earlier version queued the entry in a module `Map` and wrote it inside `navigator.locks.request` to close that race.
