Web Locks are asynchronous, so the entry existed only in memory until the lock was granted and was lost when the page left first: a script that ran a query, waited for the status line and went to `about.html` lost the entry in 180 of 200 runs in Chromium (158 of 200 when it reloaded; Firefox lost none in 200, but the grant is not ours to time). Losing a Run the student can see is worse than the same-millisecond race, so there is no lock, no queue and no asynchronous step in front of the write; tests `a Run followed at once by leaving the page keeps its entry` and the two-tab test in `m5-audit-fixes.spec.js` pin this. Stored text is untrusted: JSON that is not `{v:1, entries:[...]}` is an empty log; an entry that is not `{t: timestamp, sql: non-empty string, exactly one of rows (integer >= 0) or error (non-empty string)}` is skipped. When `setItem` throws the oldest entries are dropped from the *stored* copy one at a time until the new one fits, and the page keeps the whole capped log in a module-private `Map` (also when even the newest entry alone does not fit, or storage is unusable). **The copy in memory is merged, never preferred:** `readLog` and every write combine it with a fresh read of the stored log (`combine` in `run-log.js`): the stored copy is normally a tail of the memory copy and changes nothing, but entries in it that the memory copy lacks were stored by another tab and are added in time order (stable, so one second keeps its order; entries are compared by time, SQL and outcome, as a multiset, so the same SQL twice in a second stays two: mine `[X, X]` with stored `[X, X, X]` is three entries, which the splice in `combine` makes so, test `equal entries are counted one to one when merging`. **Known blind spot, no ids on purpose:** an entry from another tab that equals one of this page's own (same second, same SQL, same outcome) cannot be told from it, so two tabs that ran identical SQL with an identical outcome in the same second count as one entry in this merge path, which is only taken while a log is held in memory after a storage failure). Without this, a tab that had gone to memory mode wrote its stale copy over what another tab stored once there was room again (measured: 6 of 14 entries lost with two tabs). The History tab and the export stay complete while the page is open, and only a reload sees the short stored tail. **A log that cannot be read is not written to:** `stored()` returns `null` when `getItem` throws (an empty array means "nothing stored"); `commit` then keeps the entries in memory and reports failure even if `setItem` would work, because writing the newest entry alone would replace the log with one entry that the page could not read back (measured: 30 Runs on screen, none in the History tab or the export, no notice). **A second limit:** besides 200 entries a log is capped at `RUN_LOG_MAX_BYTES` = 5,000,000 **UTF-8 bytes** of SQL and error text (counted with `TextEncoder`, not `.length`: 200 entries of 24,000 CJK characters are under 5,000,000 characters but 14.4 MB, and with their links 33 MB, against the 20 MiB `verify.html` accepts), oldest entries first (the newest is always kept, whole). Storage has a quota; memory has none, and 200 Runs of 110 KB each made a genuine 21 MB export that `verify.html` refuses (over `EVIDENCE_MAX_BYTES`); links add at most 100,000 characters per entry, so a capped log stays under it. A single entry larger than the limit is still kept whole (and a file with a SQL text over 20 MB would not verify; the editor cannot reasonably hold one). Each commit reports `reportStorage("log", ok)`, where `ok` is true only if the write kept everything **and no other log is still only in memory** (`memory.size === 0`): otherwise a small log that fits would hide the notice while another database's log is still lost on close. `progress.js` reports `"progress"` the same way (no set left in memory), and `storage-notice.js` shows `STORAGE_NOTICE_LOG` / `STORAGE_NOTICE_PROGRESS` in `#storage-notice` (a warning band on `--warning-subtle`, just above the summary line, at the top of the results pane) until every subject has saved properly once more. A page without a document (the Node tests) just skips the event. SQL is never truncated. Nothing throws. Nothing removes a log; the cap drops the oldest.
- **The History tab.** `#tab-history` opens `#panel-history`, which holds `#run-history-list` (`role="group"`, labelled `HISTORY_LIST_LABEL`). It is drawn from a fresh `readLog` by `render()` with `createElement` and `textContent`: when the tab is shown (`mountTabs`' `onShow` calls `runLog.refresh()`), and after every run and every database load while it is the selected tab (`isShown()`); an empty log shows one `<p class="run-history-empty">`. One real `button.run-history-entry` per entry, newest first: the first non-blank line of the SQL in the code typeface (cut at `HISTORY_SQL_PREVIEW_CHARS` = 80, with an ellipsis also when more lines follow), then the UTC date and time with the word "UTC" after it (`HISTORY_TIME_ZONE`) and the outcome (`12 rows`, or an icon and the word `Error`). The SQL is never put in an attribute. The list is one Tab stop (roving tabindex); ArrowDown, ArrowUp, Home and End move through it; Enter or a click puts the SQL in the editor (`editor.value = sql`; nothing runs, nothing is saved as the last query), says `HISTORY_RESTORED_MESSAGE` in a toast (the result on screen stays, with its summary line) and focuses the editor. It is a tab panel, not a popup, so nothing closes it. Its colours are the tokens of `css/tokens.css` (`--danger` for an error), all checked at 4.5:1 by `tests/ui1-tokens.spec.js`.

### 9.2 The file and its verification (`evidence.js`)

```
<APP_NAME> evidence export v1
dataset: <log key, control characters as "_">
exported: 2026-11-09T14:32:08Z
<blank>
[1] 2026-11-09T13:58:12Z · 12 rows            ("1 row", "0 rows", or "error: <message>"; the dot is U+00B7)
<the SQL, newlines normalised to LF; a line that has the shape of an entry header gets a backslash in front>
link: <F3 link>                                (only for a successful entry on a built-in dataset)
<blank>
[2] ...
<blank>
student: <student number>
sha256: <64 lower-case hex digits>
```

LF only; entries oldest first, numbered from 1; one LF after the `sha256:` line. `sha256` is `crypto.subtle.digest("SHA-256")` over the UTF-8 bytes (`TextEncoder`) of everything up to and including the LF of the `student:` line. `formatEvidence({entries, dataset, exported, student})` builds the signed part (throws `RangeError` for no entries or a student number that would not stay on one line); `buildEvidence` adds the checksum line. This section is the version 1 file, which is every file a log with no event gives; section 9.5 has what an event adds (version 2). CRLF and lone CR in SQL become LF before writing, and `escapeSql` puts `EVIDENCE_SQL_ESCAPE` (a backslash) in front of every SQL line that matches the header shape (`[n] <timestamp> · <k> row(s) | error: ...`), so in a file this exporter wrote the header-shaped lines are exactly the real entries (the entry's `link:` holds the exact SQL).

`verifyEvidence(text)` is async, never rejects, and returns `{status:"valid", student, queries, exported, dataset}` (for a version 2 file, with `events` after `queries`), `{status:"edited", reason}`, or `{status:"unavailable", reason}` when `crypto.subtle` is missing (checked first: neither word can then be said). Rules, in order: not a string -> edited; over `EVIDENCE_MAX_BYTES` (20 MiB) characters -> edited; blank -> edited; CRLF becomes LF and nothing else is normalised (a lone CR stays, so an LF changed to CR is caught); two LFs at the end -> edited (when the last line before those line breaks is not a checksum line the reason is "There is extra text after the checksum line."; otherwise the file is checked with one LF at the end, and `VERIFY_EXTRA_BLANK_LINES_REASON`, "Extra blank lines were added after the checksum line.", is the reason only if it would then be VALID, so a file whose contents were changed too gets the reason it really fails with; the line breaks at the end are walked back over in a loop, because the expression `/\n+$/` rescans a long run of them from each of its positions when the run is not the end of the text: about 25 seconds for a run of 300,000, and more than two minutes for a million), one is stripped; fewer than 9 lines -> edited; the last line must match `^sha256: [0-9a-f]{64}$` (upper case refused); the line before it must be `student: ` plus a non-empty value (`STUDENT_ID_PATTERN` is deliberately not applied, so changing it does not invalidate older files); the recomputed checksum must match; then the first line must end in ` evidence export v1` (any app name, so a rename does not invalidate older files) or, for a file with events, ` evidence export v2` (section 9.5), line 2 `dataset: <non-empty>`, line 3 `exported: <a real UTC timestamp>`, line 4 blank. The query count is the number of lines that follow a blank line, match `[n] <timestamp> · (<k> row(s)|error: ...)` and are numbered 1, 2, 3 ... in sequence; at least one is required in a version 1 file (a version 2 file needs an event instead, and numbers its events in the same sequence). The order is on purpose: a damaged footer is named as such, any other edit reports "the checksum does not match". Reason texts never contain the words VALID or EDITED. Because the exporter escapes header-shaped SQL lines, the count of a genuine file is exact, whichever entry the SQL is in. Known limit: a file that was not written by this exporter (or was written and re-signed by hand) can hold unescaped header-shaped lines, and a blank line followed by a next-in-sequence header inside an entry is then counted; the checksum only says the file is as it was written, and anyone who wants to forge a count can write the whole file (deterrence-grade, spec 5a).

### 9.3 The export dialog (`evidence-dialog.js`)

"Evidence (.txt)…" (`#export-evidence`) in the Export menu (`export-menu.js`) calls `evidence.open()`; `mountEvidence({runLog, returnFocus})` -> `{open()}` takes `#export-button` as `returnFocus` and builds no button of its own. Opening: an empty log (no query and, section 9.5, no event) -> a toast says `EVIDENCE_EMPTY_MESSAGE`, no dialog; no `crypto.subtle` (an insecure origin) -> a problem toast says so, no dialog; otherwise `#evidence-dialog` (a real `<dialog>`, `showModal`, drawn as `.app-dialog` like the database export) opens: the title "Export evidence", one sentence (`EVIDENCE_DIALOG_TEXT`, "Creates a text record of the queries you ran, the files you imported and the tables you exported, to attach to your assessment.", also the dialog's `aria-describedby`; until the import and the export were switched on it was the UI plan's sentence, which named the queries alone), a labelled input (`autocomplete="off"`, no `name`, no `maxlength`, so a too-long number can be typed and refused), a hint (which says the number goes in the file and nowhere else), `#evidence-error` (`role="alert"`), and `#evidence-file`, the file it will save ("Saved as evidence-northlight.txt", named before Export is pressed), then Export (`button--primary`) and Cancel. The number is checked exactly as typed (no trimming) against `STUDENT_ID_PATTERN`; a bad one sets `aria-invalid` and `aria-describedby="evidence-error"`, keeps the dialog open and the focus in the input. The hint and invalid-number wording (`EVIDENCE_HINT`, `EVIDENCE_INVALID_MESSAGE`) are separate constants: change them with the pattern. A good one: the log is read again, each entry gets `link: <url>` if `linkFor(key, entry)` says so, `buildEvidence` runs, `saveFile` saves `evidence-<safeBaseName(log key)>.txt` (`evidence-northlight.txt`, `evidence-file_hw.db.txt`), the dialog closes, focus returns to `#export-button` and a toast says what was saved ("Exported evidence-northlight.txt with 3 queries", or with events "... with 3 queries and 1 event"; a failure is a problem toast, with no detail). **A cancelled export stays cancelled.** The checksum is made asynchronously, so `exportNow` takes `exportToken` when it starts, and every way of closing (`closeDialog`, the dialog's `cancel` event for Escape, and `close` when the dialog is not open again) changes it; after the `await` the file is saved only if the token is unchanged. `dialog.open` alone was not enough: cancel, then reopen before the checksum finished, and the old export completed into the new dialog (tests `an export cancelled with Cancel|Escape while its checksum is made ...`).

**The student number goes nowhere but the file**: not storage, an address, the file name, the title or the console (the catch blocks log nothing); the input is emptied on `cancel`, by `closeDialog()` and on `close`. An export that is cancelled while it makes its checksum is stale (the token changed) and no longer counts as busy (`busyToken`), so a new submit right after is not ignored; the stale one saves nothing. A database that loads while the dialog is open closes it (`lab:database-loaded`, as for the export dialogs): the file it named was the other database's, and Back and Forward reach the page under a dialog. A test dumps storage, URL, title, HTML, text, cookies, console and requests for a 12-character number and finds it only in the file.

`linkFor(key, entry)` is null for an error entry, for a `file:` key (an upload or URL database cannot be reopened from a link), and when the F3 fragment is null (payload too long) or `decodeShare` would refuse it; otherwise it is the page URL with its query removed and the fragment for that dataset and SQL alone (no exercise set).

### 9.4 `verify.html` (`verify-page.js`)

Brand, credit footer, a link back to `./`, a labelled `<textarea id="evidence-text">`, `#verify-button`, a real `<input type="file" id="evidence-file">`, a drop zone (a drop is taken anywhere on the page and always `preventDefault`ed), and `#verify-result` (`role="status"`, always in the layout). It checks on paste, drop, file choice and Verify. Verify checks again what was given last. A file that is chosen, dropped or pasted as a file (or text dropped on the page) is not put in the box: it is kept as `given` (its text, or the refusal of a file that was too large or could not be read), and Verify checks that until a paste into the box or a change typed there makes the box what Verify checks (`given` is null again). An input event that leaves the box's value as it was is no change (`boxValue`): Chromium fires one for an undo that finds nothing to undo. Until Nov 21, 2026 Verify always checked the box, so a press after a file had been chosen put EDITED, "The file is empty.", in the place of the file's verdict. The result is built with `createElement` and `textContent`: VALID (a definition list of student number, queries, events for a version 2 file only, exported, dataset, and a note that it does not prove who ran the queries), EDITED with the reason, or, without `crypto.subtle`, a message that says neither. A file over 20 MB is refused by its `size` before it is read. Only the newest check may show its result (a ticket counter). The page lists no entries and no links (spec 5a asks for the verdict only, and a list would put text from the file on the page for no gain: the marker uses the `link:` lines in the `.txt`). After each check it calls `scrollIntoView({block: "nearest"})` on `#verify-result`, because the verdict sits below the box and was off screen in short windows. `about.html` (section "For markers") points markers here; the app's own pages do not otherwise link to it.

**The paste problem.** `textarea.value` turns a lone CR into LF, which would hide an LF changed to CR. The paste handler therefore reads `clipboardData.getData("text/plain")`, verifies that raw text, puts it in the box itself (`preventDefault` + `setRangeText`) and remembers `shadow = {raw, value, lossy}`; Verify uses the raw text for as long as the box shows exactly what the paste put there (`shadow.value`). Once the box shows anything else, the page knows the box's value only. That is the whole truth unless a text with a lone CR (`LONE_CR`) has been pasted on this visit (`hidden`, which nothing clears but a reload): text from that paste may still be in the box, shown with an LF for its CR, wherever the edits left it, and an undo or a redo brings back text that was deleted. Both browsers bring it back after the box was emptied; Chromium also after a later paste, at the end of the box, while Firefox's undo does nothing after a paste by the page. With the original of a file pasted after a doctored copy, Chromium's undo and one deletion by hand can leave the doctored copy alone in a box that shows exactly the last paste, so an input event whose `inputType` is `historyUndo` or `historyRedo` and that changes the value sets `undone`, which the next paste clears. `boxText()` then answers `{unknown: true}` unless the box is empty (`{text: ""}`: it holds nothing) or shows exactly what the last paste put there with no undo or redo since (`shadow.raw`, unless that paste was made into a box whose text was not known, `lossy`), and the page gives no verdict of the box: it says `HIDDEN_BREAK_MESSAGE` (status `unavailable`, neither VALID nor EDITED). A visit on which no text with a lone CR was pasted is checked as before: there the box's value is the whole truth, undo or not. (Until Nov 21, 2026 the page checked the box's value as soon as an `input` event came, and read VALID for a file with one LF changed to CR in four ways: after an undo that changed nothing, for which Chromium fires an `input` event; after a character typed and deleted again; after a second paste that was taken out again; and after the box was emptied and the paste brought back by an undo.) The message is a constant of `verify-page.js` and not of `config.js`: a browser can hold an older copy of `config.js` for ten minutes after a release, and a name that the older copy does not export would keep the page from loading. A paste that replaces part of the box is spliced into the raw text through `rawIndex`. In tests a paste is a synthetic `ClipboardEvent` whose `DataTransfer` is filled after the event is made (Firefox ignores one filled beforehand). A leading BOM added to an evidence file is not detected when the file is chosen or dropped, because `File.text()` drops it and the text is the same; only the pasted route can see one.

### 9.5 Events and the version 2 file (assessment plan, Phase 0: `events.js`, `evidence.js`)

The plan (`docs/`, its section 6) has the evidence file say where a student's data came from and went: a file imported, tables exported, a database reset or reopened. Phase 0 was the groundwork. **Only the import and the table export record events** (section 9.6: kind `import`, one event for each file, a workbook's sheets being the lines of its event, 9.8; section 9.7: kind `export`, one for each file that was saved), and each is behind its flag, which has been on since Nov 20, 2026. A page with neither switched on (`?preview=-import,-export`, or both switches set to `false`) never writes the storage key below, but it still reads it: a log that already holds notes of an import or an export gives a version 2 evidence file there too, since the evidence dialog does not look at the flags (`tests/aw0-switched-off.spec.js`). The places that spoke only of queries were brought up to date on the day the flags went on (the plan's section 8, "Switched on for everyone"): the README's "Marking evidence files" (the check page shows the number of events in a row "Events" for a version 2 file), About's "Keep evidence of your work" and "For markers", the evidence dialog's line (`EVIDENCE_DIALOG_TEXT`) and the Export menu's line for the evidence (`EXPORT_EVIDENCE_DESCRIPTION`). These texts do not follow the flags: with a feature switched off again they still say that imports and exports are part of the evidence.

- **Storage (`events.js`).** Key `sqllab.events.<log key>` (`EVENTS_PREFIX`; the log key is the run log's), value `{v: 1, events: [{t, kind, after, lines}]}`, oldest first: `t` to the second, `kind` one of `EVENT_KINDS` (`import`, `export`, `reset`, `reopen`), `lines` the event's lines of detail (one line each, at least one, at most `EVENT_MAX_LINES` = 200 of `EVENT_MAX_LINE_CHARS` = 500 characters as JavaScript counts them (UTF-16 units: an emoji is two); the file writes them as they are, with the backslash in front of one that has a header's shape), and `after`, the number of queries of the event's own second that were in the run log when it happened. Apart from the run log on purpose: `run-log.js` drops every stored entry that is not a query when it writes (so a tab on an older release would erase events), and its cap of 200 would push an import out first. The newest `EVENTS_MAX` = 1,000 are kept, and `EVENTS_MAX_BYTES` = 1 MB of line text.
- **`recordEvent(key, {kind, lines}, now?)`** stores before it returns and gives the stored event, or null when it refuses (no log key, an unknown kind, no line of detail, a time that is not a date); it never throws, and `readEvents` gives no events for no key. A line is made one line (`sanitizeLine`) and cut to `EVENT_MAX_LINE_CHARS`, and past `EVENT_MAX_LINES` the last line says how many are not listed. It follows the run log's rules: the stored events are read again first (another tab's are kept); when the browser refuses a write, the most of the newest events that it will take are stored (found by halving: about ten tries for a thousand events, not one for each) while the page keeps them all in memory (`readEvents` answers from there); stored events that cannot be read (`getItem` throws, or the value has a **later version number**, any number above `EVENTS_VERSION`, written by a newer release) are never written over, and the new ones live in memory until they can be. Stored events that this page's memory copy lacks (older ones it has let go at the cap, or another tab's) are merged in by time, and within a second before all of this page's (`combine`): for one page that is the true order in every case, and at the caps it is never the page's own newest that go. **What is not promised** (limits kept on purpose; the first two are the run log's too). Events carry no ids, as queries do not: two that are equal in every field count as one when one is only in a page's memory and the other is stored (two tabs while one could not store, or one tab whose storage could not be read between the two). Only the page whose own write was refused keeps everything in memory: a page whose writes all succeeded holds nothing of its own, so when another tab, out of room, stores a shorter list, the first page's list is that shorter one too. And when two tabs record in the same second while one of them could not store, the events of that second can come out in another order than they were recorded in, the other tab's own two included, and are then stored so; nothing is lost or listed twice by it, and across seconds the order is always the true one. A change to what an event may hold, a new kind included, therefore needs a new `EVENTS_VERSION`. Each write is reported with `reportStorage("events", ok)`, fine only while no log's events are held in memory alone, and `storage-notice.js` shows the sentence of `storageNoticeEventsText` in the same band as the run log's notice.
- **What an export's event holds** (9.7). `file: <file name>, <n> bytes`, then one `table:` line for each table in the dialog's order: `table: <table>, <n> rows`, and for a zip `, <entry name>` after the rows. Up to 199 tables are listed in full (the file's line and a line for each: 200); from 200 tables on, the event holds the file's line, the first 198 tables and `(N more lines are not listed)`, where N is the number of lines left out (the 199th table on). A line is cut at `EVENT_MAX_LINE_CHARS` (500) and ends in "…". The line of a single CSV's own file is not, since the name of a table's file is cut to 100 characters before `.csv` (`EXPORT_NAME_MAX_CHARS`). A table's line holds the table's own name, as stored, made one line (a line break or another control character in it becomes `_`): a name of 486 characters or more in a single CSV loses the end of its line (measured with letters, for a table of one digit of rows; each digit more takes a character off, so 480 for 1,234,567 rows), and in a zip, whose line holds the entry's name as well (104 characters for a long name), one of 380 or more (374 for 1,234,567 rows).
- **`timeline(queries, events)`** (pure) gives the order the file lists them in. The events are taken in time order (those of one second in stored order); an event goes after the first `after` queries of its own second, or after all of them when fewer are left; with no query in its second, before the first later query. The queries keep their order. `after` exists because a query's time has no finer grain than a second and queries carry no ids.
- **The file.** `formatEvidence` writes version 2 only when an entry is an event (`{t, kind, lines}`: an entry with a kind or lines and no SQL; with SQL it is a query whatever else it carries); otherwise the text is byte for byte what it always was. Version 2: the first line ends in `EVIDENCE_TITLE_SUFFIX_V2` ("evidence export v2"); an event is an entry of its own shape, numbered in one sequence with the queries: `[n] <time> · event: <kind>`, its lines of detail, a blank line. In a version 2 file a line of SQL or of detail shaped like either header gets the backslash, the event header's shape whatever kind it names (`[n] <time> · event: ` and anything after it to the end of the line, a line separator included), so a file this app wrote never shows the checker a kind it does not know; a version 1 file is escaped as before (query headers only). An event with no proper time, a kind that is not one of `EVENT_KINDS` or no line of detail throws `RangeError`: it is never written as a query.
- **The check.** `verifyEvidence` reads the version from the first line. Version 1 is checked exactly as before, and its result still has exactly `{status, student, queries, exported, dataset}`. Version 2 counts both header shapes in one sequence and returns `{status: "valid", student, queries, events, exported, dataset}`; it must list at least one event (a first line ending in v2 over a body with none is EDITED, "The file is marked as version 2 but lists no events.") and may list no query. An event header of a kind that is not in `EVENT_KINDS` makes the file EDITED, whatever its number, with a reason that says the page may be out of date: skipped, it would leave every entry after it out of the count under the word VALID. A release that adds a kind must therefore get its verify page out first. The checker that reads version 2 ships before anything writes it; a marker's cached copy of the old `verify.html` scripts would call a version 2 file EDITED, so the first phase that records events must not go out ahead of this one.
- **The dialog and the verify page.** `evidence-dialog.js` reads `readEvents(key)` beside the run log: with no event the call to `buildEvidence` is what it was; with events, the queries get their links first and `timeline` then puts the events among them (an event never has a link), a log of events and no queries can be exported, and the toast is `evidenceSavedWithEventsMessage`. `verify-page.js` adds an "Events" row after "Queries" for a version 2 result only.
- **The saved files.** `tests/fixtures/evidence/` holds three version 1 files written by the build before this change (two exported through the dialog, one built from an awkward input), each with the input it was built from. `aw0-evidence` requires `buildEvidence(input)` to give each byte for byte and `verifyEvidence` to give the saved verdict. Do not regenerate them.

### 9.6 Import of CSV files (assessment plan, Phase 2: flag `import`)

Files: `import.js` (the controller), `import-dialog.js`, `import-report.js`, `import-db.js`, `import-csv.js`, `import-text.js`, `import-rules.js`, `import-pace.js`, `css/import.css`; texts and limits in `config-workflow.js`; the library is `vendor/papaparse.min.js` (Papa Parse 5.7.1), fetched by `loadVendor("papaparse.min.js", "Papa")` the first time a text file is previewed. The plan's sections 3 and 7 are the specification, and `tests/fixtures/` holds the owner's files with the result each must give (its README). **Excel workbooks are section 9.8** (the flag `excel-import`, which does nothing without `import`): this section is the import as it is with `import` on and `excel-import` off (a visit gets that with `-excel-import` in its address), and says where a clause is true only then.

- **Behind the flag.** `mountImport` (called once from `index.js`) reads `flagOn("import")`. The flag is on by default since Nov 20, 2026, so "off" here is a visit whose address says `-import`, or a build whose switch is `false`. Off: it returns `{enabled: false, take: () => false, busy: () => false}`, builds no dialog, and nothing in the page differs: the file input takes one file, the menu entry says what it said, no drop listener is added, no library is ever asked for. On: `#open-file-input` gets `multiple` (and no `accept`: a database can have any name), the "Open a file…" entry says it takes CSV files too (CSV and Excel files with `excel-import`, 9.8: the dialog's sentence and the toast for a mixed choice then differ as well), `index.js` adds a `drop` listener on `document`, and the object also has `run`, which the dialog calls and the tests call with a job made by hand.
- **A choice or a drop.** The input's `change` handler copies the files first (`Array.from`: Chromium empties the input's own list when its value is cleared) and offers them to `importer.take(files)`; with the flag on a drop anywhere on the page does the same, unless a dialog is open (`dialog[open]`) or an import is running, when it is ignored. The drop itself is still cancelled as ever (`guardFileDrops`, `plain-text.js`): no text reaches the editor and the browser does not open the file. `take`: a file is the import's when its name ends in `.csv`, `.tsv`, `.txt`, `.xlsx` or `.xls` (`IMPORT_NAME`) or in `.xlsm`, `.xlsb` or `.ods` (`OTHER_SPREADSHEET_NAME`, in `import-text.js`; the dialog refuses these three by name, see "What a file is"), in any letter case, the ending being the end of the name (`notes.ods.csv` is a CSV file); one file that is not the import's is opened as ever (`openFile`); files that are open the dialog, a file with one of the three endings being one more tab beside others and a refused tab alone (a drop is a choice: `index.js` keeps no list of names and hands the files to `take`); a mix, or more than one other file, is refused in a toast (`IMPORT_MIXED_MESSAGE`, `IMPORT_SEVERAL_DATABASES_MESSAGE`) before any dialog opens, and so are import files that come while a database is loading (`IMPORT_LOADING_MESSAGE`; see "An import and a load never overlap", below).
- **What a file is** (`import-text.js`). Decided from its bytes before its name, in the plan's order (`kindByStart`), except for the three endings of the next paragraph: no bytes; the four zip bytes; the eight OLE2 bytes; a `<` first under a workbook's name (what comes before it can be white space, which is looked past up to 16 MB: `headOf` gives `kindByStart` the first 4,096 bytes, and for a file under an .xls or .xlsx name that is white space to there, reads on, in pieces that grow by sixteen, to the first character that is not; while only the first 4,096 bytes were looked at, such a file after more white space than that was imported as one column of text, also with `import` alone, where it is now refused as a workbook is); anything else is text. The first is refused as empty; the next three are workbooks, refused with `IMPORT_WORKBOOK_REASON` whatever the file is called unless `excel-import` is on too, when they are read (9.8). A text file over `IMPORT_TEXT_MAX_BYTES` (250 MB) is refused; one over `IMPORT_TEXT_WARN_BYTES` (50 MB) asks first, in its tab, before it is read (`showWarning`, the plan's "step of its own, with Continue and Cancel"): *Read this file*, or *Leave this file out*. Left out, the file is a refused tab like any other, by the student's own choice (`refuse(tab, reason, {own: true})`): the reason is `IMPORT_LEFT_OUT_REASON`, the tab's words for a screen reader are `IMPORT_LEFT_OUT_TAB_TEXT` in place of "cannot be imported", and its sign is a quiet one, in the tab's own colour and not the danger colour (`.import-tab-refused--own`: nothing went wrong); it is never read, and the other files can be imported without it. After either answer the focus is on the file's tab, also in a browser where a press does not give a button the focus. Until one of the two is pressed Import is off, and the line beside it names the file. Then one pass over the whole file (`inspectText`): a zero byte with no UTF-16 byte-order mark means it is not text; the encoding is the byte-order mark's, else UTF-8 when every byte fits, else Windows-1252. Decoding is strict everywhere (`TextDecoder` with `fatal`). Windows-1252 takes every byte: the five it leaves undefined come out as the control characters of the same number, as Windows reads them (decided in this phase; the plan's section 9 has it). **A name ending in `.xlsm`, `.xlsb` or `.ods`** (a macro workbook, a binary workbook, an OpenDocument spreadsheet; any letter case, tested at the end of the name) is looked at first, by `look` in `import-dialog.js`, before `headOf`: the tab is refused with `importOtherSpreadsheetReason(ending, excel)` ("A file ending in .xlsm cannot be imported. Save it as an Excel workbook (.xlsx) or as CSV, and import that."; with `import` alone, where a workbook is refused too, "... Save it as CSV, and import that."; `ending` is the ending made lower case, with its dot, and `excel` is whether the dialog has the workbook reader). No library is asked for and none of the file's bytes is read, so a real workbook under such a name is refused all the same: the name is what the student sees, and the advice is about the name. Everything else about the tab is a refused tab's (the sign, "(cannot be imported)", Close alone when nothing else can be imported, the others imported without it, and its name in the line of what was not imported, below). With `import` off nothing changes: such a file is tried as a database as ever. **Accepted, and known:** with `import` on, a SQLite database that someone has called `x.ods` can no longer be opened by choosing it, since the name decides before the bytes are looked at.
- **The dialog** (`import-dialog.js`). A wide `.app-dialog`, built once. `open(files)` starts a session, a counter that every late answer is checked against (a file still being read for a dialog that has since closed is dropped); the dialog's `close` event arrives after the fact and is ignored when the dialog is open again by then. One tab for each file (and, with `excel-import`, a strip of tabs for the sheets of a workbook inside its panel, 9.8; `mountTabs` on a tab list built for the session; a long name is cut short in its tab, with the whole of it in `title`, and a tab is never wider than the strip, `max-width: min(16rem, 100%)`: at 320 px the ends of the tab that had the focus were cut off, its ring with them); the files are looked at one after the other (`look`, `read`, `readPreview`). The first `PREVIEW_BYTES` (1 MB) are decoded and read by Papa as one text (`readStart`): the delimiter is its guess among four, comma when it cannot tell; when the text is not the whole file its last row may be cut short and is left out, and a file with not one complete row in that megabyte is refused (`IMPORT_NO_ROW_REASON`: nothing could be shown, and no line ending found). **The line ending is not Papa's guess** but `lineEnding` (`import-csv.js`): Papa's own rule (a line feed when one comes before the first carriage return; else the pair when a line feed follows more than half of the carriage returns; else the carriage return), applied to what is outside the quoted fields, with the quotes read as the parser reads them (a quote opens a field only at the start of a field, and closes it only where the field ends: white space of any kind may stand between, all that `String.trim` takes off, as in Papa). Papa pairs every quote with the next one instead, so one lone quote in a heading (`Height (in")`) puts the pairs out of step, a Windows file is read with `\n`, and every row keeps a carriage return: wrong values and wrong types, silently. It is found with the four candidate delimiters, then again with the delimiter in use, and the text is read again if the two differ. A carriage return at the very end of a text that is not whole is not counted: it may be the first half of a pair that the megabyte cut. A text that starts with U+FEFF (a second byte-order mark) is given to Papa with one more in front: Papa takes one off a whole text and none off the pieces of the import, and the preview must show what the import stores. The preview table shows the rows as read, numbered: up to three rows above the row of column names, tagged "left out", that row tagged "column names", then `PREVIEW_ROWS` (20) rows of data. An empty field reads NULL; a field with white space at an end is tinted and underlined with dots, so the space shows (the tint alone is too faint, and is not drawn in forced colours); a field of more than 80 characters is cut short, with the whole of it in `title`, as the report does. Changing the delimiter reads the start again. Changing the encoding first checks the whole file against it (`fitsEncoding`); one the bytes do not fit says so and turns Import off. What the student has set by hand (a name, the delimiter, the names row) is kept when the file is read again, and what they have not is guessed again. Readings of a tab are numbered (`tab.turn`): a newer one takes over from an older one still on its way, and until the newest has ended the tab is `unsettled` and Import is off, so the settings on show are the settings used. A file that cannot be read as it is, is refused; a reading that the student's own choice made impossible (a row of more than 2,000 fields, no row at all) is `tab.problem`: said in the tab in place of the preview (`.import-problem`, an alert), Import off, and the controls stay, so the choice can be changed back. Each preview's text is marked (`markOf`, `import-text.js`: its length and a 32-bit number) for the import to know it again. Table names are suggested in one pass (`suggestNames`: the destination's names first, then the tabs in order) and checked on Import (`checkNames`: empty, `sqlite_`, twice here, taken there: `takenNames` in `import-db.js` gives the tables, views and indexes of the destination and the connection's TEMP tables and views, one of which would hide a new table of its name; it is a Map that says which names only a TEMP table or view has, and such a name is refused in words of its own, `IMPORT_NAME_TEMPORARY_MESSAGE`, because the student would look for that table in the database in vain). The destination is a new database, named after the first file that is not refused (with `excel-import`, the first that can give a table: a workbook none of whose sheets can be ticked is passed over too, 9.8), or the open one when that is not a course database. With nothing left to import the dialog offers only Close. A refusal moves the focus to the tab when it took a focused control away, and gives the names again (`suggestNames`). When Import is off because of a file that is not the tab on show, a line above the buttons says which (`#import-hold`, which the Import button is described by; "still reading" only after 600 ms). A file that waits for an answer is named there whichever tab is on show, its own too: in a low window (683x384) its two answers lie below what the body shows when the dialog opens, the wait does not end by itself, and nothing else in view would say why Import cannot be pressed. A problem in the tab on show is not repeated there: it is said where the student has just made it, and a line that came and went with each key would move the body under their hands. For the same reason the line names the first file, in the tabs' order, that it can name (one that waits; one still being read, or with a problem, on another tab), and what the tab on show says of itself never decides it: when it did, a problem typed in the tab on show took away the line of a file that waits on another tab, and its mending brought the line back. When the line comes, goes or changes, the foot is higher or lower by it and the body shrinks or grows from below, so a control of the body that has the focus, and could be seen whole before, is scrolled back when that has cut it (`focusedWhole`, looked at before the foot changes, and `keepWhole`: at 683x384 the tab of a large file was left cut in half just after the dialog had opened). Nothing else is moved then: a control that is still whole stays where it is, and so does a body that the student had scrolled away from the control (the first form scrolled to the focused control whenever the line changed). And nothing is moved while the main button of a pointer is down on the dialog (`pointerDown`, from the `mousedown` to the release, wherever that comes; a menu that opens over the press, or the window losing the focus, ends it too, since the release may then never be told): what is owed to the control is done at the release, when it is still the control that has the focus and the body has not been scrolled since (the first form threw the body back, at the release, to a tab the focus had left). **The dialog is a column of two parts** (`css/import.css`): `.import-body`, the only thing that scrolls (the title, the destination, the tabs and their panels, the progress), and under it `.import-foot` (`#import-hold`, `#import-error`, Import and Cancel), which stays in reach whatever is scrolled and is beside the body, never over it. No control can lie under the buttons, and a line of the foot is in view whenever it is shown. The dialog element itself does not scroll, except as a last resort when the foot alone is higher than the room there is. (The first build had a sticky bar over the scrolling content. Six faults came of that one choice: a focused field hidden under the bar; Chromium scrolling the dialog to its end when a button of the bar took the focus, once a scroll padding kept controls clear of it; Firefox leaving the preview half under it; a press that moved a field from under the pointer; the import's error shown behind the bar; Firefox jumping by half the dialog. Do not go back to it.) A control that takes the focus is scrolled wholly into view by a `focusin` listener (`showWhole`: `scrollIntoView`, nearest; for a field that shows a message, the whole field, since Phase 5, 9.8; the body's `scroll-padding-block` leaves room for the focus ring), because Firefox scrolls a focused control only when less than a line of it can be seen, and so left the preview, which is tall, cut off (the small controls it brings in by itself, to the middle; Chromium brings in all of them). Two cases are left alone: a control that a pointer is pressing (`pressing`, set by a capturing `mousedown` for the length of that turn, in which the focus of the press arrives: a double click in a field must not land beside it), and one that does not show its focus (`:focus-visible`: a tick box whose label was pressed), which the dialog does not move, whatever the browser then does by itself ("Limits", below). **A database that opens while the dialog is open and idle closes it** (`lab:database-loaded`; the export's dialog does the same): Back and Forward reach the page under a modal dialog, and the destination and the names were worked out for the database that was open.
- **A name that the dialog made unique, and the note under it.** With the open database as the destination, `suggestNames` makes a suggested name unique against the names the database holds (`getDestination().taken()`, keys by `sameNameKey`: a table, a view, an index or a TEMP table) and against the earlier files and sheets of the same choice. When the natural name (`tableNameFor` of a text file, `sheetTableName` of a sheet) is among those names the unit gets `unit.note`: `importNameMadeUniqueNote(database, wanted, given)` ("sample.db already has a table, view or index called Staff, so this table is called Staff_2.") when the database holds it, and `importNameMadeUniqueTemporaryNote(wanted, given)` ("A temporary table or view is already called Staff, so this table is called Staff_2.") when only a TEMP table or view has it (`taken()` gives `true` for such a name: it is in no database, so the note does not name one; a name that both hold is the database's). A name that is made unique only by an earlier file or sheet of the same choice has none, since both tabs are in view. The note is worked out in the same pass for every unit that is named (a text file's tab and each sheet that can be ticked), kept on the unit (the panel of a sheet may not be built yet: `buildUnitControls` shows it when it is built), and shown by the `field()` helper's `showNote` / `clearNote` under the name field, after the hint and before the place of the error: a `<p class="app-dialog-hint import-name-note" id="<field id>-note">`, hidden while there is no note, named in the field's `aria-describedby` while it shows (the error first when there is one, then the hint, then the note). It is a note and not a fault: no `aria-invalid`, no `role="alert"`, Import is not held, and nothing scrolls for it (`toShow` and `keepMessageWhole` are for errors). It goes at once when the student types in the field (`nameEdited`: it does not come back when the same text is typed back), when the destination becomes a new database (the names are suggested again, and the natural name is back), when the file is refused or left out; it comes back when the open database is chosen again and the name is still the dialog's. A note and an error are never on show together: `showError` hides the note, `clearError` shows it again. The line wraps inside the settings' column (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`), also in the two columns of a sheet's panel and on a phone; its colour is the hint's.
- **The import** (`import.js`, `run`). Nothing starts while a database is loading (`IMPORT_LOADING_DIALOG_MESSAGE`, which says both ends: when that database has opened the dialog has closed and the files are chosen again; when it could not be opened the dialog is still there), nor into the open database when that is a course database after all (`IMPORT_DESTINATION_GONE_MESSAGE`: the dialog does not offer it, and this is the second lock). The busy state goes on; the database is `manager.init(name, new DatabasePath(""))` for a new one (built apart: the open one is not touched) or the open one; `beginImport` opens a savepoint (`import-db.js`). For each text file (a sheet of a workbook is run by `importSheet`, 9.8; and the reader, Papa, is loaded only when the job has a text file): `textPieces` reads it in slices of `IMPORT_SLICE_BYTES` (64 KB) through one streaming decoder and never ends a piece on a carriage return; `sameStart` lets the pieces through while the text still starts with the text the preview was read from, and throws when it does not (the file was saved again meanwhile: Chromium refuses to read such a file, Firefox reads the new bytes, and the settings on show were found in the old ones; the file is refused with `IMPORT_CHANGED_REASON`); `readPieces` hands the pieces to Papa through its stream input, with the delimiter and the line ending the preview settled, and Papa calls back once for each piece (a quote error counts for the unpaired-quote warning only when its row ended in that piece: Papa reads a row that a piece cut short again with the next piece, and says again what still holds. Papa gives the error the place after the quote that opens the field, so "ended" is "at the cursor or before it": at it, when that quote is the last character of the file); `newTableReader` drops the rows above the names, makes an empty field `null` and tallies every column; `stage.add` puts the rows in a TEMP table of text columns `c1` to `cN`, widened when a wider row comes (in the main database the staging table would leave its pages behind and double the exported file). At the end of the file `finish()` gives the columns (names, a type from all rows, blanks, the value that blocked a number), and `stage.copyInto` creates the table with those declared types and copies the rows by rowid, `COPY_CELLS` at a time: the column's own type does the conversion (`'100'` in a REAL column is stored as 100.0). After each slice and each copy step `pace.rest()` gives the page a turn (`import-pace.js`: `scheduler.yield()` or a `MessageChannel`, and a drawn frame every `PAINT_EVERY_MS` for the progress bar), and the import looks whether it was cancelled. Then `endImport`. A new database goes to `openBuilt` (section 4.1); an import into the open one is announced as `lab:database-changed`, which the Schema tab and the editor's names follow (it is not a run: nothing goes into the run log, and the status bar keeps the last query's line). One event is recorded for each file (`importEvents` in `import-events.js`: consecutive tables of one `File` are one event, and a workbook's `table:` lines end with the sheet, 9.8), under `runLog.current().key` (section 9.5), the report goes to `results.showReport`, and a toast says what the summary line says. **The files that gave no table.** The dialog adds `left: [{name, why}]` to the job: one entry for each file tab that gives no table in this import, in the tabs' order, `why` being the very words that tab says to a screen reader (`filesLeft`: a refused tab's `tab.said`, which `refuse` also writes into the tab, so "cannot be imported" for a refused file and "left out" for one the student left out; and `bookWords` for a workbook that gives no table, "nothing ticked" when it has sheets to tick and none is ticked, "cannot be imported" when none can be; the same function draws the workbook's tab). A workbook that gives at least one table is not listed: its unticked sheets are the student's choice. `run` takes `job.left` (it may be missing, as in a job made by hand: the tests do that) and, when it has entries, the sentence of the summary line, the toast and the announcement (one string) is `importedWithLeftMessage(tables, database, left)`: `importedMessage` then " 1 file was not imported." or " 3 files were not imported."; with none it is `importedMessage` alone. The event of the evidence log is not changed: it lists what was imported. An import that fails, is cancelled or is refused says what it says without this.
- **Nothing is left behind.** A refusal found while reading (`ImportRefused`: a row of more than 2,000 fields, the names row past the end of the file, a file that stopped being readable), a failure and a cancel all end in `undoImport` and, for a database built apart, in closing it. `undoImport` is `ROLLBACK TO`, then `RELEASE`, inside a transaction the student had open; where the savepoint began the transaction it is a plain `ROLLBACK`, so that the database is left as it was to the byte (a `RELEASE` would end the transaction as a commit of nothing, and the engine counts one more change in the file's header for it). When the engine runs out of memory, or a database that was given a page limit is full, it has rolled everything back by itself, the student's own open transaction included, and the message says so whichever failure it was (`IMPORT_LOST_TRANSACTION_MESSAGE`). An import into the open database sits inside a transaction the student left open, if there is one: it is theirs to commit.
- **The busy state.** `importer.busy()` is true while `run` runs. Since the table export (9.7) the page's state of the long jobs is `index.js`'s own (`runningJob`, `jobMessage`, `addressAtJob`, written by both jobs' `onBusy`), and the names this section uses were replaced: `refusedWhileImporting()` is `refusedWhileBusy()` (it toasts the running job's own message), `importInTheWay()` is `jobInTheWay()` (true also while a dialog is open, whichever it is) and `addressAtImport` is `addressAtJob`. `index.js` then refuses, with the toast `IMPORT_BUSY_MESSAGE`: Run (`execute`; a Run that was waiting its frame is dropped), Check (the `isLoading` the exercise panel is given includes it), the database export (`getProblem`, and again in `onExport`), a pick in the switcher (which is then put back, `picker.sync()`), "Open a file…" and Ctrl+O, Open from URL and Ctrl+U, opening or closing an exercise set (`lab.syncPicker()`), and a chosen file. Back and Forward cannot be refused, because the address has changed already: `popstate` opens nothing and puts the address back (`pushState` of `addressAtJob`). Ctrl+O and Ctrl+U are ignored while the dialog is merely open too (`jobInTheWay`): they reach the page under a modal dialog, and they are ignored under every dialog of the page (but for Ctrl+U in the Open from URL dialog itself, which goes to its field). The page's own `loading` flag will not do for any of this: closing an exercise set clears it.
- **An import and a load never overlap.** No load starts while an import runs (the busy state, above), and no import starts while a load is on its way: `mountImport` is given `isLoading` (the page's `loading` flag), `take` refuses import files meanwhile and `run` refuses to begin. A load that ended in the middle of an import into the open database would close the database being written to; and a dialog opened during a load would offer a destination that is about to go. **An import and a table export never overlap either**: `mountImport` is given `otherJobMessage` (the export's busy message while one runs, else null), and `take()` and `run()` refuse with it, as `open()` and `run()` of the export refuse with the import's (9.7).
- **Closing the dialog while it imports is Cancel.** Escape cannot be held back in a `<dialog>` (the third press closes it whatever the page does), so Cancel, Escape and any other close all set `cancelled`, the import stops at its next rest, and everything is undone. Escape is told to the import at once: the dialog's own `cancel` event (the browser sends it before it closes the dialog, and the `close` event only later) ends the session, and so sets the same flag, so a step that was already waiting for its turn in between does not run on as if nothing had happened. A key held down repeats, and a repeated Enter (`event.repeat`) does nothing in this dialog: Enter held on Import would press Cancel, which has the focus once the import runs. When the dialog has closed under the held key, the repeats go on to the page: `endSession` calls `swallowHeldEnter()` (`held-enter.js`, 3.2), once for each closing, the first time the dialog hears of it (for Escape, at the `cancel` event, before the dialog has closed), so that they type no empty lines into the editor and press nothing that got the focus back. (The table export's dialog does the same, 9.7.)
- **The report** (`import-report.js`). For each table: its name, its rows and columns and the file it came from; a warning when a quote in the file could not be paired; one paragraph (`.import-report-quoted`, text `importReportQuotedText`) when the table's name or some columns' names must be written in double quotes (a reserved word such as `Order`, or a word such as `null` or `current_date` that SQL reads as something else), after the warning and before the columns; and for each column its name, the name it had in the file where that changed ("blank" for a blank one), its type, its blanks (the NULLs that pad a short row included) and, for a text column where at least half of the values are numbers, the first value that is not (a sheet's table has two notes more, 9.8). A name or a value of more than 80 characters is cut short with its whole text in `title` (one long value would push the other columns out of sight), and each table of columns is `aria-labelledby` its heading. It points at what to clean and cleans nothing. When some files gave no table (the job's `left`, above), `buildImportReport(tables, {left})` puts one paragraph before the first table, `.import-report-left`, text `importReportLeftText(left)`: "Not imported: a.xls (cannot be imported), b.xlsx (left out)." (the names joined with a comma and a space, a full stop at the end; a name is text and nothing else, set in a `<bdi>` element of its own so that a character in a file name that changes the direction of the text, such as U+202E, cannot reorder the words and the names after it; the sentence comes from `importReportLeftParts(left)`, pairs of text and "is a name", which `importReportLeftText` joins; a name of more than 80 characters is cut as a value is, with the whole sentence in the paragraph's `title`). With nothing left out the report is what it was. **The names to quote** are found by the engine, in `import.js` right after a table is made (`namesNeedingQuotes` in `import-db.js`, on the database the import writes to; it reads no table, writes nothing and never throws; the result is `quoted` in the table's entry: the table's name first, then the columns in the table's order, a word once whatever its letter case, since SQL reads `Group` and `group` as one name: a file `Group.csv` with a column `group` names `Group` alone). A name that is not ASCII letters, digits and underscores, or starts with a digit, counts as needing quotes and is never put into SQL bare (the import makes no such name). Any other name is tried by running a query of one row that reads no table of the database, with the name unquoted and inside parentheses: a column as `select (<name>) is ?1 from (select ?1 as "<name>")`, a table as `with "<name>"(x) as (select ?1) select (<name>.x) is ?1 from <name>`. Each must answer 1, which it does only when the bare name reads the text that was bound. A word the parser refuses, such as `select`, fails. A word that parses and means something else gives another value: `null`, `current_date`, and `true` and `false`. Those two are a case of their own: SQLite 3.54 reads a column called `true` straight from its table, bare or quoted, and through a subquery, a WITH clause or a view it reads the bare word as the constant (1 for `true`, 0 for `false`) and does not find the quoted name at all ("no such column"; the inner query has to give the column another name with AS). So the report lists such a column, although a query on the table alone reads it bare: quoted, a wrong value at least becomes an error. The parentheses are there because a word can pass without them and fail with them: `with` begins a WITH clause after an opening one, so `WHERE (with = 1)` is refused where `WHERE with = 1` is not. A table's name is written bare in the two places a query writes it, after FROM and in front of a column: `cast`, `raise`, `current_date`, `current_time` and `current_timestamp` pass after FROM and are refused in front of a dot, so a table called `cast` is listed. A probe that throws means the name needs quotes. Nothing is put in the event line. The probes were compared with the engine for 963 names (the 275 words of `js/lab/sqlite-words.js` and 48 other names, in up to three letter cases), each written bare and quoted in up to 38 places of a query as a column and 26 as a table, in Chromium and in Firefox: the list was the engine's for every name but one. That one is a table called `if`, which every query tried reads and `DROP TABLE if` refuses (the parser expects IF EXISTS); it is not listed. What a name does as an alias written without AS (`SELECT qty left FROM t` is refused) is not the import's to say: the alias is the student's word, not a name of the table.
- **Limits, and what is not decided.** 2,000 columns (`MAX_COLUMNS`, in `config-workflow.js`: refused in the preview when any row of the megabyte read for it is wider, shown or not, else as soon as such a row is met). The sizes above (a workbook's are in 9.8). SQLite's own text-to-number conversion: a decimal of twenty or more significant digits can differ in its last place from the nearest number. A decimal that no number can hold (some 300 digits before the point: it would be stored as infinity; as many zeros after it: as zero) is not a plain number, and its column stays text (`isDecimal`). `-0.0` is a decimal by rule 3 as written and is stored as 0.0 (the plan lists it for the owner). Left as the library reads them, because the plan decides none of them: a line holding only `""` (skipped), text after a closing quote (read on, with the quote warning), and a file that mixes line endings (read with the one ending `lineEnding` finds in its first megabyte, so the other kind stays inside fields). Two corners are known and left: in a file whose quotes are already out of order (it gets the quote warning), a quoted field that begins with a line break of the other kind can still mislead `lineEnding`; and a file over 1 MB whose first megabyte ends exactly with its only row is refused as holding no complete row (`readStart` leaves the last row of a text that is not whole out, and cannot tell that this one is whole). **In the dialog, what the browsers do by themselves is left to them** (each was measured with the dialog's listener silenced, and none hides a control for good). A control that Tab reaches while part of it is out of view is brought in by the browser: Chromium to the nearer edge, with the air of the focus ring (12 px, the body's scroll padding), or to the middle when most of it was hidden, and a tick box always to the middle; Firefox to the middle, so the body can move by half its height there, as any scrolling box does in Firefox. A control that was wholly in view stays where it is in both. Beyond that: Chromium moves a `<select>` that a mouse presses near the body's edge, and moves the body by the air of the focus ring (12 px) when a click on a label gives its tick box the focus there; both bring a control that was part out of view to the middle when its label is pressed; Firefox scrolls while a drag selects text near the edge, selects nothing on a double click in a field of which a quarter can be seen, and can take a control that lay within that air of the edge to the end of the scroll (28 px, one stop in some 3,700 tried); in Firefox a Tab followed by Shift+Tab within about 30 ms, which no hand does, can leave the preview cut off; and Firefox can bring a text field that has the focus back into view when the body changes its height, from wherever the student had scrolled it (in about half of the runs, with the dialog's own scrolling taken out: so the tests of `keepWhole` use a radio button and a tab). So "a control that was in view is moved by the air at most" is what the tests hold at three window sizes, not a law. What counts as a held press is what the page is told of the mouse, and three things follow from that, found by reading the code (none was tried in a browser; each is a matter of one line's height, once). A finger resting on the dialog is not a held pointer: a touch sends its `mousedown` only when the finger has gone up, so a line that comes during the rest scrolls a cut control back at once. Any release ends the hold, whichever button, pointer or key it came from (another button let go, a second finger, the keyboard's menu key). And should a browser never tell the page of a release (a pick dragged through the list of a `<select>`, perhaps), `pointerDown` stays set until the next release anywhere, and a focused control that the line cuts meanwhile stays cut until then. When the foot alone is higher than the room (both of its lines at 320x256; one at 320x180), the body shrinks to its padding and the dialog itself scrolls, the last resort the stylesheet names: everything is still reached, a part at a time. `#import-error` stays after a failed import until Import is pressed again or the dialog is opened again: what it says is still why nothing was imported. In `css/import.css` the body's `min-height: 0` and the foot's `flex: none`, and in the dialog the capture flag of the `mousedown` listener, change nothing in Chromium and Firefox today (a part that scrolls may shrink to nothing anyway, a part that does not keeps its height, and nothing under the dialog stops a `mousedown`): they are written out for the browsers this was not run in, Safari among them, and for a later child that would stop the event.
- **Holding an import in a test.** The code has no hook for tests. To keep an import running, a test holds its library: after the preview has loaded it, `delete window.Papa` and answer `vendor/papaparse.min.js` through a gate; `run` then waits in `loadReader()` with the busy state on. To cancel in the middle of a file, a test slows `scheduler.yield` down before choosing the file. **A second import, mounted by hand**: what the page cannot reach (the second lock of `run`) is tested on a second import that the test mounts itself, with stand-ins for the page, from the page's own module (`(await import("./bundle/app.js")).import.mountImport`; a copy of the source module is refused, section 11). The flag is read once, when the page loads, so the page is opened with it, and the page's own `#import-dialog` is taken out first, because the second has the same id. A file over 50 MB is made in the page (`new File([new Uint8Array(...)])`, dropped there), so no bytes travel; `tests/ui-states.js` has the dialog (`import-dialog`), the large-file question (`import-warning`), a running import (`import-progress`) and the report (`import-report`) as states, for axe and for the screenshot baselines. The library of workbooks is held the same way, by a route (`holdVendor(page, "xlsx.full.min.js")`), and 9.8 says how.

### 9.7 Export of tables as CSV (assessment plan, Phase 3: flag `export`)

Files: `export-tables.js` (the controller), `export-tables-dialog.js`, `export-csv.js` (what the files hold, pure), `export-read.js` (the engine side), `css/export-tables.css`; texts and limits in the last section of `config-workflow.js`, "Export of tables as CSV"; the library is `vendor/fflate.min.js` (fflate 0.8.3, MIT, Arjun Barrett: the publisher's own `umd/index.js`, unchanged, 33,044 bytes, its licence in `vendor/fflate.LICENSE.txt`; it defines the global `fflate`), fetched by `loadVendor("fflate.min.js", "fflate")` when Export is pressed with two or more tables ticked and never before. The plan's sections 5 and 7 are the specification, and `tests/fixtures/export/` holds the owner's database of awkward tables (`export_edge_cases.db`) and the exact CSV each must give (the fixtures' README).

- **What a student can do.** With the flag on (the default), the Export menu and the Export group of the More menu have one entry more, "Tables as CSV…" (`#export-tables`, `#more-export-tables`; the line under it is "Tables and views, one file each"), right after "Database (.db)…". It opens a dialog that lists every table and view of the open database with its row count and a tick box; under the list, one line says what Export will save ("Saves basic.csv." or "Saves sales-tables.zip, with 3 CSV files."). One table is saved as one `.csv`; two or more are saved together as one `.zip` with a `.csv` for each. Every row is exported, however many the results pane would show. While it runs the dialog shows which table is being written and how many rows of it, with Cancel. A saved file is told in a toast ("Exported basic.csv with 5 rows", "Exported sales-tables.zip with 14 tables"); when a table held a binary value (a BLOB) the dialog stays after the file is saved, with one note and a Close button, because a BLOB cannot be a CSV value and comes out as hex text. The evidence file lists each export that saved a file (9.5). With the flag off nothing is added to the page.
- **Behind the flag.** `mountTableExport` (called once from `index.js`, before the two menus) reads `flagOn("export")`. The flag is on by default since Nov 20, 2026, so "off" here is a visit whose address says `-export`, or a build whose switch is `false`. Off: it returns `{enabled: false, open() {}}` and touches nothing: no dialog element (`#export-tables-dialog` is not in the page), no listener, no request, and `index.js` hands the menus no `tables` entry, so both are exactly what they were. On: the dialog is built once and appended to `document.body`, and the entry's texts (`EXPORT_TABLES_MENU_TEXT`, `EXPORT_TABLES_MENU_DESCRIPTION`) go to the two menus through `index.js`, so that the plan's Phase 8 renames the item in one place. Nothing is fetched: the zip library comes with the first export of two tables. (`aw0-flags` holds what the flag alone adds: the entry in each menu and the dialog's element, no request, no storage key, no toast.)
- **The modules and what each promises.**
  - `export-csv.js` is pure (no DOM, no engine; the specs load it in Node; it imports `safeBaseName` from `export.js`, which loads in Node too, and `VALUE_KINDS` from `export-read.js`, which it hands on). It holds the rules of the file: `fieldText` (a field is in double quotes, each quote doubled, when it holds a comma, a quote, a CR or an LF, or starts or ends with the space character; a tab does not count); `realText` (a REAL: the fewest digits that read back the same, which is what JavaScript's `String` gives, an exponent written out in full, always a decimal point, `-0.0` for negative zero, `Infinity` and `-Infinity`); `valueText(kind, value)` by the engine's own number for the storage class (`VALUE_KINDS`: INTEGER 1, FLOAT 2, TEXT 3, BLOB 4, NULL 5), an INTEGER by `String(value)` whether it is a Number or a BigInt, a BLOB as `x'<lower-case hex>'` (`x''` when it has no bytes; written by a table in this module, not by `cellText`, so that the export's rule cannot change by accident: a spec compares the two on 200 random BLOBs), a NULL as nothing; `rowText(kinds, values)` -> `{text, blobs}` (a record ended by CRLF, and how many of its values were BLOBs; in a table of one column a NULL and an empty text are therefore a line with nothing on it, which most programs skip when they read a file, the import here too, so those rows do not come back: the owner's `rules.py` writes it so, plan section 5); `headerText(names)`; `CSV_BOM`; `csvFileNames(tableNames)` (the name of each table's file: `sanitiseName` and the cut to 100 characters, `.csv` added, and the names made unique by the rule of `uniqueName`: the name itself, else `_2`, `_3`, the first that is free; the count for a name goes on where it stopped and does not start at 2 for every table, so that a long list of names that come out alike, names with no letter of A to Z all being `_`, is not walked again for each of them and the work grows with the number of tables and not with its square); `tablesZipName(databaseName)`; `planOutput({format, databaseName, tables})` and `zipProblem(entryCount, bytes, limits?)` (`EXPORT_ZIP_MAX_ENTRIES` and `EXPORT_ZIP_MAX_BYTES` unless others are given: a test gives small ones). `planOutput` gives `{format: "csv", kind: "file" | "zip", fileName, type, library, entries: [{table, name, renamed}]}` (`null` for no table; a format other than `"csv"` throws): one table is a file with `library: null`, several are a zip with `library: {file: "fflate.min.js", global: "fflate"}`; `renamed` says that an entry's name is not simply the table's name and `.csv`, so the dialog compares no strings of its own. `format` and `entries` are there so that Phase 8 can add a workbook, whose entries are sheets, to the same dialog. A text that is not valid UTF-8 never reaches it: the reader has already read each bad sequence as U+FFFD.
  - `export-read.js` hands over values, not text, and nothing in it knows CSV. `listObjects(database)`: `select type, name from main.sqlite_schema where type in ('table', 'view') and name not like 'sqlite\_%' escape '\' order by name`, so SQL does the sorting. `countRows(database, name)`: `select count(*) from main."<name>"`. `openRows(database, name)` prepares `select * from main."<name>"` (names through `quoteIdent`, `js/lab/sql.js`) and returns `{columns, next({chars, ms}), close()}`. `next` gives `{rows, done}`: rows are `{kinds, values}`; `kinds[i]` is `capi.sqlite3_column_type` asked before the value is read (a REAL 1.0 and an INTEGER 1 both come back as the Number 1 and only the kind tells them apart); an INTEGER is `stmt.get(i)` (a BigInt beyond 2^53 - 1 in absolute value), a REAL `stmt.getFloat(i)`, a TEXT a string, a BLOB a `Uint8Array` of its own, a NULL `null`. It takes rows until `chars` characters of values (a text its length, a BLOB twice its bytes, a number 20, a NULL 1) or `ms` milliseconds are used, always at least one row unless the table has ended. The reader finalizes its statement by itself when the table ends, and `close()` finalizes it at any time and any number of times: whoever opens a reader closes it, on every path, or the open statement keeps the database from being closed. A `step()` that fails throws and leaves the reader open for `close()`; the engine writes its own warning to the console then (`sqlite3_step() rc= …`), without the SQL (`engine-guard.js`). **A text is read as its bytes** (the plan: "text exactly as stored"), not through the engine's own JavaScript reader (`stmt.get`), which drops a U+FEFF from the start of every text it hands over: a value, a column's name or a table's name that starts with one would come out without it, and a table so named could not be found again by its listed name. For a TEXT value, for each column's name and for the names `listObjects` gives, the reader asks the engine for the address and the byte count of the text (`sqlite3_column_text` first, which settles the bytes, then `sqlite3_column_bytes`; for a name `sqlite3_column_name` and the length of the C string) and decodes those bytes from the engine's memory with a `TextDecoder` that keeps a leading U+FEFF (`ignoreBOM: true`; a copy of the bytes first when the memory is shared). The memory is `database.wasm`, which `manager.js` puts on every database it makes; a database made by hand without it makes the reader throw a `TypeError`. Where the engine gives no address (it had no memory left to make the text), the reader throws an engine error with the out-of-memory code, which `isEngineError` and `isOutOfMemory` of `import-db.js` both know; nothing can make that happen in a test without a hook. **The header** is the names the engine gives for `select *`, which are the names the results pane shows for the same statement, with one difference (a name that starts with U+FEFF keeps it here, as every text does, and the pane's own reader drops it): a view that names two columns alike has `a`, `a:1` (the engine adds the numbers), and the two old pragmas `full_column_names` and `short_column_names` change the names here as they change the pane (with the first on and the second off, the header of `t(a)` is `t.a`).
  - `export-tables.js` is the controller: `mountTableExport(page)` -> `{enabled, open()}` (it has no `busy()`: nothing asked for one, since the page is told by `onBusy`). `page` is `{returnFocus, getDatabase(), isLoading(), otherJobMessage(), runLog, onBusy(busy)}`. `run(job, {progress, cancelled})` is what the dialog's Export calls (below), and `job` is `{database, tables: [{name, count}], plan}` (no format and no kind of table: what is saved is in the plan); `open()` is what the menu entry calls. The module also exports the sinks (step 7: `fileSink`, `zipSink` and `sinkFor`) and loads in Node: nothing in it touches the page before it is mounted.
  - `export-tables-dialog.js`: `mountTableExportDialog({returnFocus, getDatabase, list, count, plan, onExport})` -> `{open() -> boolean}`. The dialog gets the engine side as functions (`list`, `count`, `plan`), so it holds no SQL and no CSV rule; its pace and its frame are `newPace` and `nextFrame` of `import-pace.js`, which the controller uses too.
  - The menus: `export-menu.js` and `more-menu.js` take an optional `tables: {text, description, onSelect}` and insert one entry after the database's; without it they are what they were.
- **One export, from the menu item to the saved file.**
  1. *The menu item.* `open()` of the controller refuses with a note toast (`tone: "note"`) when no database is open (`NO_DATABASE_MESSAGE`, as the database export says it), when an export's own earlier run has not ended (`EXPORT_TABLES_BUSY_MESSAGE`: the dialog closes at once on Cancel, the run notices at its next look), when an import runs (`otherJobMessage()`: the import's own busy message), and when a database is loading (`EXPORT_TABLES_LOADING_MESSAGE`). Then `dialog.open()`: it lists the objects; one that throws gives a problem toast (`EXPORT_TABLES_LIST_FAILED_MESSAGE`), none gives `false` and the note toast `EXPORT_TABLES_NONE_MESSAGE` (objects named `sqlite_…` do not count), else the dialog opens.
  2. *The dialog.* `open()` captures `database = getDatabase()` once; everything after it (the counts, the plan, the job) is for that database. It makes a row for each object, shows the dialog (`showModal`), puts the focus on the first tick box (the "All tables and views" box when there are two rows or more, else the row's own) and starts counting. It first clears what an earlier opening left: the alert line, the status line and the count of tables announced.
  3. *The counts.* One frame is awaited (`nextFrame`) so that the dialog is on screen, then each object is counted, one at a time, with `pace.rest()` between two; each row says "Counting rows…" until its count is known ("2,000 rows", "1 row", "0 rows"). A count that succeeds paints its own row only (`paintRow`: the tick, its `aria-disabled` and its line): the whole list (`refresh()`, step 4) is gone through again only when a count fails, when a tick changes and when counting ends, because a pass over every row after every count made the counting of a long list quadratic. Ticks can be changed meanwhile, and Export stays off until every count has ended. A count that throws keeps its row in the list with the reason (`exportTablesUncountedText`: "Its rows could not be counted, so it cannot be exported. The database said: …", the engine's words on one line by `engineLine`), takes its tick away and cannot be ticked; whether that was only the database being closed under the dialog is told by state and not by the engine's words (`gone`: the dialog's session has changed, or `getDatabase()` is no longer the database the dialog opened with, or `database.db.isOpen()` is false): then nothing is shown, the dialog is closing. A session counter, as the import dialog has, drops counts that come back for a dialog that has closed or opened again. Nothing in the counting loop is expected to throw; if it does, `countingFailed` gives every row that has no count the same kind of reason and ends the counting, so that the dialog never says "Counting rows…" for ever.
  4. *The plan.* `refresh()` runs when a tick changes, when a count fails and when counting ends: it asks `plan(database, ticked names)` (that is `planOutput`) and keeps the result in `current`, writes each ticked row's "Saved as …" when its entry is `renamed`, sets the "All tables and views" box (checked when every row that can be ticked is, `indeterminate` when some are, `aria-disabled` and not `disabled` when no row can be ticked: a press does nothing, and the box keeps the focus it had when the last count took the last tickable row away) and sets the line `#export-tables-hold` and Export's `aria-disabled`. The job that Export hands to `run` carries that very `current`: `run` never works the plan out again, so what was shown is what is saved.
