A sheet's tab says its state in words and never by colour alone (`IMPORT_SHEET_TICKED_TEXT` and the others; a sheet whose name is only spaces is "(no name, number 3)"); a workbook none of whose sheets is ticked has on its tab the quiet sign and "nothing ticked", or "cannot be imported" when none can be ticked at all. A panel is named by the sheet's name alone (a workbook's by the file's), never by the tab's words, which change as boxes are ticked.
  - *A sheet's panel* is built when it is first shown, and kept (`buildUnit`: a workbook can have hundreds of sheets and each preview is a table); what a sheet is set to (ticked, its name, the names row) lives in its unit, built or not, and a name problem found by Import in a sheet that was never shown builds it first. Top to bottom: "Sheet <its whole name>" (a web page's table has its label alone), the word "hidden", and under it, for a sheet that is ready or too wide, `importSheetSizeText` ("Its values span 51 rows and 4 columns.": the rectangle, the row of names included; a value typed by accident a million rows down shows here before anything is imported); the tick box "Import this sheet as a table"; the hidden note, or the reason a sheet cannot be ticked; then, as a text file's tab has them, the table name, the box "The sheet has a row of column names" (ticked: nothing is guessed for a sheet) with "Column names are on row", and the preview: the row of names and 20 rows, up to three rows above it when the names are lower down, a cell of more than 80 characters cut short with the whole in its title; a cell's preview is its value by the rules above, NULL as the preview shows an empty field. When the table does not start at A1 a line says "This table starts at C5 of the sheet. Rows are counted from there."
  - *Names.* `suggestNames` runs over every unit of `nameUnits()` in one pass (so ticking never changes a name: the destination's names first, then the tabs in order, each taking the first of x, x_2, x_3 that nothing before it has; one the student typed is kept; a name made unique because the destination has the natural one gets the note of 9.6, kept on the sheet and shown when its panel is built), `checkNames` over `tickedUnits()` on Import: a name that is empty, `sqlite_`, taken here, taken in the destination, a TEMP table's. A name that two units have is said in the sheet's panel (`IMPORT_NAME_TWICE_SHEET_MESSAGE` when either is a sheet); Import pressed with one shows the workbook's tab and the sheet's tab and puts the focus in the name field. **The new database's name** comes from the first file that can give a table (a workbook's file name without its extension; not a refused file, and not a workbook none of whose sheets can be ticked), from the first file when none can, is given again when a workbook's sheets arrive, and is never replaced once the student has typed one.
  - *Why Import is off, and where that is said.* A workbook tab holds nothing itself once it has been read; a **ticked** sheet holds Import up when its row of names is no row of it (and an unticked sheet never does: a row typed beyond a hidden sheet nobody wants must not block), a workbook still being read or asking its question holds it as a file does, and when nothing at all is ticked though something could be, Import is disabled with "No sheet is ticked. Tick at least one sheet to import." (`IMPORT_HOLD_NO_SHEET_MESSAGE`). The Phase 2 rule stays: **the tab on show says its own problem where the student made it** (the message under the field, and a sheet's own words, "The sheet has only 5 rows."), and the line beside Import (`#import-hold`) names the first file or sheet, in the tabs' order, that it can name and that is not on show ("The sheet orders of shopping_db.xlsx cannot be imported as it is set. Its tab says why."); what is on show never decides it. A sheet is on show only when the outer strip's current tab is its workbook's and the inner strip's current tab is its own (or it is the only sheet). With no tab able to give a table the dialog has only Close.
  - *A strip of tabs is no stop of the Tab key* (both strips: `sendFocusToSelectedTab`). In Firefox a strip that scrolls sideways was a stop of its own, and the arrow keys then scrolled it and moved to no tab, so a strip has `tabindex="-1"`; a click on its empty part, which would leave the focus on the strip with no key doing anything, sends the focus on to the selected tab. The selected tab is the strip's one stop; a tab that the dialog selects (the first sheet that is ticked, the sheet whose name is wrong) is brought into the strip (`bringIntoStrip`: only the strip scrolls, never the body or the dialog; nothing happens while the strip is not laid out, and showing that file's tab does it then). Inner panels are `import-sheet-panel`, never `import-panel`, and `app.importTabs` in the tests' kit is narrowed to the outer strip.
  - *What is brought into view, and when* (this applies to a text file's tab as well). `toShow(control)` is the field's whole box (`.import-field`: label, control, hint and message) when the control has `aria-invalid="true"` and the box is no higher than the body's `clientHeight`, else the control alone; `showWhole` (the `focusin` listener), `isWhole` and `keepWhole` all go through it, so the rule of 9.6 for the foot's line sees the box too. The reason: the tab on show is the only place that says why Import is off for what is wrong in it, and a message cut by the foot says nothing (the row of names, side by side at 1366x768, ends 9 px above the foot: a red field, half a line, and Import off with no reason in view). So (1) a message that comes under the field the student is typing in brings the field into view whole by the least scroll (`keepMessageWhole`: the field with the focus, or the one whose own `input` event is being answered, `whileEdited`, since Firefox gives a number field no focus when one of its arrows is pressed with the pointer; a key typed while the message is there brings it in again); (2) when the dialog itself sends the student to a field (Import pressed with a wrong table name or no name for the new database) the field gets `focus({preventScroll: true})` and is brought in the same way, also when it already had the focus (no `focusin` comes then); (3) a field that shows a message and takes the focus by the keyboard is brought in whole. While a pointer is down on the dialog nothing moves (`pointerDown`, 9.6): it is done at the release if that field still has the focus and the body has not been scrolled since. A field with no message is brought in as before, its control alone (a note, 9.6, is no message: it is not `aria-invalid`, and nothing is brought in for it); nothing else scrolls: not when a message goes, and never a tab that is not on show.
  - *The layout of a sheet's panel in a wide window that is lower than 1080 px* (`@media (min-width: 900px) and (max-height: 1079.98px)` in `css/import.css`): the preview is beside the sheet's settings, not under them, because stacked, the strip, the count line and the sheet's own lines push the preview under the fold (at 1366x768 and 1536x730 no row of it is in view, where a text file's tab shows four) and the preview is how a student sees that a title row is being taken for the column names. The panel is a head row (`div.import-sheet-head`: the sheet's line and its size, `div.import-sheet-what`, and at the right end the tick box) and under it, across the width, the hidden note or the reason, then on the left (14 to 17 rem) the settings (`.import-settings`: name, box of the names, row) and on the right `div.import-sheet-rows` (the preview's heading, its notes, the preview). The order of the elements in the document, so the Tab order and what a screen reader meets, is the same in both layouts; in any other window the two wrappers are plain blocks and the panel is as it always was; a text file's tab is never changed. **Why 1080**: stacked, the seven rows of the preview's box are above the foot only from 1080 px of height (two rows at 900, three at 950, five at 1000, six at 1040), side by side from 920, and a browser's window on a 1080p screen is about 950 px tall, so a bound at 900 left the commonest desktop window with three rows where one pixel less gave six; at 1080 and more the rows are the same and the preview is half as wide again. Two things to know: from 1080 to 1096 px of height the stacked body still scrolls, by 17 px at most (the last edge of the preview's box and the body's padding are under the foot; the whole fits from 1097 px; the bound stays, since no number removes every such band and no row is lost on either side of it: moving it to 1097 is the owner's to ask for); and the width bound of 900 px is a step of up to four rows, since two columns need about that width.
  - *Closing.* `endSession` lets every workbook go and stops a reading that is under way (`current()`); Cancel while an import runs closes the dialog as in 9.6, and the workbook then has to be chosen and read again.
- **The run** (`import.js`, `import-db.js`). A job's entries are in the tabs' order, a workbook's sheets together: a text entry as 9.6 has it, a sheet's `{id, file, sheet, hasNames, namesRow, tableName, listed}` (`sheet` is the object `readSheet` made, the workbook travels inside it; `listed` how many sheets its workbook lists, which tells a web page of one table from one whose other tables were unticked). The job has `left` as in 9.6: a workbook with no sheet ticked is in it with "nothing ticked", one none of whose sheets can be ticked with "cannot be imported". **The reader of text files (Papa) is loaded only when the job has a text entry**: a job of sheets alone must not ask for it and must not fail when it cannot be loaded. A sheet is never refused by the run: everything that can stop one (no values, too wide, a names row beyond it) is decided in the dialog before Import can be pressed, and what the run throws for a sheet that is not Cancel and not the engine's is a failure of the import (`IMPORT_FAILED_MESSAGE`), everything undone as in 9.6. `importSheet` (the sheet's name in the table's source and in the event is what the tab and the panel call it: a name of only spaces is `(no name, number 3)`, the dialog passes `label` with the sheet): the table is worked out in a first pass (`tableOf`, a `rest()` every `IMPORT_SHEET_CELLS` (30,000) cells; the progress is "read", counting cells of the rectangle); `newTypedTable(database, name, columns)` creates `main."name"` with the declared types at once and prepares one insert (**no staging table**: the values are already typed); the rows go in steps of `IMPORT_SHEET_CELLS` cells (`floor(IMPORT_SHEET_CELLS / width)` rows; some tens of milliseconds), with `rest()` and the "copy" progress between steps and Cancel honoured there. `finish()` ends the insert at the end of the rows, and `abandon()` (which drops the table) is called from a `finally` when `finish()` was not reached; neither throws, and whichever comes second does nothing, and everything is inside the job's savepoint as ever, so a later failure or Cancel takes the earlier tables with it. **Binding**: the engine's `stmt.bind` takes a JavaScript number that is whole as a 64-bit integer and wraps one of 2^63 or more (1e19 would be stored as a negative number, even in a REAL column), so a number for a REAL column is bound with `sqlite3_bind_double` whatever it is, a number for an INTEGER column with `bind` when it is a safe integer and as a double when it is not, and text and NULL with `bind`; after each row `stmt.reset(true)` clears the bindings so that a short row leaves NULLs. A text in an INTEGER or REAL column is converted by the column, as for a text file. **The progress counts tables** in any job that has a sheet ("Importing table 2 of 5: orders (shopping_db.xlsx)", "Importing orders (shopping_db.xlsx)" for one table; a text file in such a job is named by its file); a job of text files alone keeps its words byte for byte. A table's result also carries `origin` (the `File`), `file`, `bytes`, `sheet` (the sheet's label, null for a text file and for a web page of one table), `emptyFormulas` and `padded`, and `source` for the report is `importSheetSource(file, sheet)` ("shopping_db.xlsx, sheet orders"), `importPageSource` ("prices.xls, Table 2") or the file's name alone. **Workbooks are read one at a time** (`workbookTurn`, a promise chain in the dialog): the bytes of two are never held together; they go in the order they ask, which is the tabs' order except that a workbook that asks a question first goes when it is answered and does not hold up the workbooks behind it. **A workbook changed after it was chosen** is imported as the dialog showed it (the sheets are the library's objects, read once). Cancel, a failure and a refusal leave nothing, as 9.6 says.
- **The events and the report** (`import-events.js`, `import-report.js`). `importEvents(tables)` makes one event for each **file** in the job's order: consecutive tables from one `File` object (not one of the same name) are one event, so a workbook of three tables is one event of four lines, and two files of one name are two events. A workbook's event has the file's line (`importEventFileLine(file, bytes)`) and then one line for each table ending with its sheet, `table: orders, 50 rows, 4 columns, orders` (`importEventSheetTableLine`); a text file's event, and the table of a web page that has one table, is byte for byte as before. `recordEvent` (9.5) already makes each line one line and cuts it. In the report a sheet's table has up to two more things, after the line of rows, columns and source and the notes a text file can have: one paragraph (`.import-report-formulas`, `importReportEmptyFormulasText`) when formula cells came out empty ("3 formula cells of this sheet came out empty: the workbook stores no result for them, or they give empty text. In the table they are blanks."), and in the Note cell of a column whose format showed zeros that the table does not hold (`.import-report-padded`) "Excel showed 00123 for the number 123: the zeros are not in the table." (with no example, "This column's format pads numbers with zeros, which are not in the table."); a column can have both that and the blocking note. Nothing else in the report changes, and a text file's report is as it was.
- **Limits, and what is not decided.**
  - *The sizes* are in "What a workbook is" above. `MAX_COLUMNS` (2,000) now lives in `config-workflow.js`; `import-csv.js` hands it on.
  - *What a workbook near each limit costs*, measured in real browsers on the machine it was measured on (Chromium 158 and Firefox 159, headless, on one fast desktop with 30 GB of memory, each browser allowed 9 GB; the workbooks had one sheet of 20 columns of numbers, of texts or of half and half, an .xlsx just under each line for sheet data and an .xls just under each line for the file): read without a question (sheet data under 100 MB, an .xls under 20 MB) the page is frozen for 2.8 to 5.2 seconds (an .xls 0.9 to 1.3) and the tab holds 0.6 to 2.0 GB at its peak (the texts cost the memory, the numbers the time); after the plain question (under 300 MB, an .xls under 50 MB) 8.5 to 15.6 seconds (an .xls 2.2 to 3.3) and 1.6 to 3.0 GB in Chromium, 2.8 to 4.5 GB in Firefox; after the question with the sentence on memory (under 500 MB, an .xls under 100 MB) Chromium reads and imports all six workbooks (frozen 15.9 to 22.9 seconds, an .xls 3.0 to 6.8; peak 2.2 to 5.1 GB), and Firefox reads the workbook of numbers (26.2 seconds, 4.6 GB) and the three .xls (6.2 to 9.3 seconds, 2.5 to 3.8 GB) but **runs out of memory on the workbook of texts and on the mixed one**, at about 7 GB (its engine's own limit, not the machine's), and each is refused, some four seconds after the memory ran out, with the words for no memory, the page working on (a workbook of 1.4 million texts chosen straight afterwards was read every time it was tried, 30 times). The import after the reading takes 0.5 to 14 seconds and the page answers meanwhile; in Chromium, with millions of text cells, it pauses for up to 2.3 seconds at a time. Nothing is held afterwards: Chromium's own heap after a collection that was asked for is 10 to 11 MB after Cancel and after an import, as before any file was chosen (4 MB). Under the line of 300 MB of sheet data both browsers read everything that was tried. A lab PC is expected to be slower (perhaps two to three times by its processor) and may have far less memory to spare: none has been timed, and the plan asked for that before the flag went on (it went on on Nov 20, 2026, at the owner's word, before the timing was made). A stricter set of limits to weigh, which is the owner's to decide after that timing: refuse an .xlsx over 300 MB of sheet data (and say the sentence on memory from 100 MB). The limits stay as the plan has them until then.
  - *Several workbooks at once.* The limits are for each file: several large workbooks chosen together are all held while the dialog is open (only their reading is one at a time).
  - *A damaged .xls cannot be told from a sound one, and can freeze the page for good.* The format has no checksums: of 600 copies of one .xls with a bit changed the library read 137 differently with no error and threw on 49; and in another run one of 241 copies never returned. The cause: a changed bit turned a record of 16 bytes into a formula record, whose reader takes 22 bytes for granted, and what it reads past the end is not a number, and its loop (`while (target != position)`) never ends. The reader of another record kind grows a list for ever the same way, and an XML spreadsheet of a few hundred bytes with a `MergeAcross` of a billion runs the library out of memory. Nothing on the page can see these coming or stop them: the page is frozen, the student closes the tab, and what was not exported is lost. A Worker that the page can end is the only guard, and the plan settles that the reading of a workbook uses no worker (its section 10), so none is built and no test supposes one. An .xlsx is not exposed in this way: the zip check refuses a changed file before the library sees it.
  - *Small files that cost a great deal* (figures measured once, in Chromium and Firefox, with files made to do it; none is guarded, and the owner decides which of them to guard, each by a limit that would change what a student sees). The limits measure what a file claims and what its sheets unpack to, and a file can be small and cost much more. (1) The zip check reads the directory and the library reads the local headers, so a file can pass the check and every limit and be read as something else: a local header that gives a part another size (a file of 5.8 KB then made the Firefox tab pass 2 GB and a Chromium tab take 1.1 GB, with no question asked), another name (sheet data under the name of a picture is not counted as sheet data), a second end record at the end of the file that the check skips, a part marked encrypted in the directory only, which the check skips, or a Zip64 extra field the directory does not have. (2) The merged blocks are looked up by row, never by column, so a sheet with k blocks that all cover the rows of its n cells costs n times k: a file of 431 KB with 50,000 blocks and 100,000 cells froze Chromium for 19 s and Firefox for 35 s, the import then froze it again, and 16,000 blocks of one column each, which Excel allows, cost 7 s. (3) The library's reader of a web page tests every cell against every merge it has met: 160,000 rows with a colspan (6.5 MB, under the 20 MB that asks first) froze Chromium for 35 s and Firefox for 75 s. (4) The library makes a stub for every cell of a hyperlink's range: 5.6 KB of file with a range of 8 million cells took 0.6 GB in Chromium and 1.2 GB in Firefox, and the range of a whole sheet runs a Node process out of memory. (5) One very long text takes about 47 times its size: a shared string of 52 MB (a file of 53 KB, under the 100 MB of sheet data that asks first) took 2.5 GB in Chromium and passed 2 GB in Firefox. (6) The library is slow in the number of sheet parts: 10,000 sheets froze Chromium for 14.5 s, and an import makes a table for each. (7) The other way round, a zip that the library reads and the check refuses, with words that blame the file: more than 64 KB after the end record, or a deflate stream that ends with no final block (no real program was found that writes such a file, and it is a guess that one exists). The library's table of number formats is not one of these: the page's workbook that holds nothing is written with a table of its own, and the library is asked for its table again once that workbook was read, so a workbook with very large formats leaves nothing held once it is let go (in Chromium the page's heap is back at 10 MB after Cancel and after Import for a workbook with 100 MB of number formats). The number formats of an .xls or an XML spreadsheet stay in the library's table until the next workbook is read; they are no larger than the file was.
  - *The table is the rectangle of the values* (the plan). A value left by accident far below or far to the right of the data makes the table that large, in rows or columns of blanks; the sheet's panel says its size, and nothing else warns.
  - *A file called .xls or .xlsx whose bytes are not a workbook and not text either* (random bytes) is refused with the text file's words, "This file is not text, so it cannot be read as CSV.", with both flags as with `import` alone: what a file is, is decided from its bytes, and tests pin those words. A sentence of its own for such a name is the owner's to ask for.
  - *What the plan left open for a workbook, and what is built* (each is the owner's to overrule). Each workbook is a tab with its sheets as tabs inside it and a tick box in each sheet's panel, with "Tick every sheet" and "Untick every sheet"; a workbook that lists one sheet shows it with no strip and no buttons. The library reads in dense mode. The limits measure what an .xlsx's sheets unpack to, not its file's size. The zip check is made before the library. The app's own test of a date format and its own calendar stand where the plan names the library's; a time that rounds up to a whole day is `00:00:00`; a date-formatted number that no date can be stays a number. Five refusals have a sentence each (cut short, damaged, not a workbook, locked, unreadable), and so have a web page with no table, a workbook of charts only, a library that could not be loaded, a workbook the browser has no memory for and a large workbook that came back a sheet short. A sheet has the tick box "The sheet has a row of column names" as a text file has, ticked, with nothing guessed. A web page with several tables gives a table each. A sheet of more than 2,000 columns, or with a cell beyond Excel's last row or column, is listed and cannot be ticked, and the rest of its workbook can be imported; a workbook whose sheets all hold nothing is listed, not refused. Empty formulas are counted on the whole sheet below the names row, also beyond the table's columns. The progress counts tables. An OpenDocument spreadsheet is refused. A field's message is brought into view with its field. In a wide window lower than 1080 px a sheet's settings are beside its preview. The page's own script (`bundle/app.js`) grows by about 68 KB (17 KB compressed) and its stylesheet by about 5 KB (1 KB compressed) for every visitor, flags on or off, since the code that reads a workbook is part of the one file the page loads; the library itself (952 KB) is asked for only when a workbook is first read, which is why a first visit still asks for the same files.
  - *Checked by hand for this phase*: nothing yet. *Not checked by hand yet* (the plan's section 9 lists the checks by hand): the time and the memory of a workbook of 20 MB and one of 100 MB on a lab PC (above); several workbooks chosen and imported in Safari (WebKit does not start on the machine the tests run on, so no test has run there either); one real course workbook of each kind imported and its report read as a student would; the new parts of the dialog (the sheets' tick boxes, the hidden marks, the question for a large workbook, the report's additions) in light and dark, as screenshots; and the dialog read by a screen reader (it was checked with axe and by keyboard alone). The report's count of empty formula cells has no expected value in the fixtures: the page gives 10 for `excel/formulas_uncached.xlsx`, 3 for `excel/xlwt_written.xls` and 4 for `excel/formulas.xlsx`, with the names on row 1.
- **What a later phase finds.** The export of workbooks (the plan's Phase 8) writes with the same library, `vendor/xlsx.full.min.js`: `loadVendor("xlsx.full.min.js", "XLSX")` hands back the global once it is on the page (and asks for no second request), so a page that has read a workbook already has it. The rules of `import-cells.js` (a date by value, a number in full, the formats) are for reading only: the writer starts from the engine's own kinds (9.7), not from these.
- **Holding a workbook's import in a test.** The code has no hook for tests; the bundle exposes `importCells`, `importDb`, `importWorkbook` and `importZip` by name (`EXPOSE`, 10), so a page test tries the typed table against the engine, the zip check in the browser's own `DecompressionStream` and the reading with the library as the page has it; the pure modules also load in Node (`aw5-excel-rules`, which hands them the library by `require`). *How a test makes a workbook*: `workbookFile(name, sheets, bookType)` in `tests/helpers.js` (sheets `[{name, rows, hidden}]`, written by the library in Node), `made()` of the dialog spec (also cells, merges, chart sheets and an `edit` of the unzipped parts), and, for what the library's writer cannot make (a formula with no stored value, a chart sheet, a built-in format id, a cut sheet part, a part's name in another form), the zip unpacked, edited and packed again with the vendored fflate (`editZip` in the rules spec); a size is made in the page (`new File([head, new Uint8Array(n)])`, no bytes travel) or by a directory that claims it, since a zip is judged from its directory alone; the owner's workbooks are never edited. *Holding the library, the check and the inserts.* The library's file is held by a route (`holdVendor(page, "xlsx.full.min.js")`, or the `holdLibrary` option of `watchReading`: the request waits until the test lets it go; there is no `window.XLSX` to delete first, since the library is not on the page until it is asked for). The frame that the reading waits for before the library's call, and every timer of the page, are held by stopping the page's clock (`page.clock.install` then `pauseAt`; `runFor(VENDOR_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS - 1)` is the library's wait). The zip check and the inserts rest through `import-pace.js`, so the kit's `slowDown` holds them for Cancel, and `gateTurns(page)` holds the import's next turn at a place the dialog shows (`at(titleRegExp, part)`, then `held()` and `release()`; the page's clock stands still from `at()` until the release), whatever the machine's speed; `watchReading` notes the page's own steps in `window.__trace` (`bytes:<name>` when a file is read whole, `read:start`, `read:end`, `read:nothing`, `frame`, `turn-held`) and holds the next turn of the zip check. **How the specs tell the read of a chosen file from the read of the workbook that holds nothing**: three specs wrap `XLSX.read` in the library's own file as the route serves it (`countReads` in `aw5-excel-fixtures`, `watchLibrary` in `aw5-excel-limits`, `NOTE_READS` in `aw5-excel-page`), and tell the two calls apart by their options: the read of a chosen file has the plan's options (`dense: true`), the read that lets the strings go has `{type: "array"}` and nothing else; the first is counted as a read (`reads()`, `read:start`/`read:end`) and the second as `hollow()` or `read:nothing`, and every call goes on to the real library. `watchLibrary`'s `throwing` makes a read throw what a source says instead. *Which spec holds what*: **`aw5-excel-rules`** (Node, `chromium-root` only, the first project) the pure modules (limits, texts and `tallyNumber`, the events, the vendored file's size, hash, banner and version and its licence's, formats and dates and numbers in full against lists, cells, rectangles, merged blocks, tables, the report's two facts, the wiring of the library, the limits at their edges, the zip check, every owner's workbook read through the three modules against its expected file also in other time zones, copies with one bit changed, the names of parts, what the library keeps, and the watch); **`aw5-excel-fixtures`** (page) each owner's workbook chosen in the page and imported through the real dialog, the plan's "Done when" line by line, workbooks under other names, 1e19 and 6.02e23 stored as they are, and the zip check in the page; **`aw5-excel-dialog`** (page) the sheets' strip and panels, ticks, the two buttons, the count line, hidden and empty sheets, names, the line beside Import, keyboard, focus, roles and live regions, axe in both themes, 390 and 320 px, a workbook of 300 sheets, the layout in a window lower than 1080 px at both sides of its bound, and the field's message in view; **`aw5-excel-limits`** (page) the question for a large workbook and its two answers with every limit at its edge, the refusals of files made in the test, and a last group for a workbook that the browser has no memory left for; **`aw5-excel-page`** (page) the typed table against the engine, the report, the run of a sheet with a job made by hand, workbooks beside CSV files, the flags, the library asked for once and only when needed, one workbook read at a time, Cancel and Escape at each moment, the busy state, Back and Forward, drops, and the event in an evidence file that verifies. **How the last group of `aw5-excel-limits` makes a browser have no memory left** (nobody runs a browser out of memory for real in a test): the library is made to throw what an engine throws for it (`RangeError("Invalid string length")`, the bare string "out of memory", an object whose `message` itself throws), or to come back and then fail in the page's next step (the table of formats, a sheet's property, a cell that the preview looks at), through `watchLibrary`'s `throwing` and the counter `window.__lose`; an element made by the page is made to throw (`Document.prototype.createElement`) to imitate the refusal that cannot be shown; **the room that the watch asks for is counted and withheld**: `Array.from` is wrapped so that a call with a source of 100,000 or more and a map function (nothing else on the page makes one) counts in `window.__probes` and throws `out of memory` while `window.__noRoom` is true, so a test waits for two probes (two turns of the watch that asked and had none) and sees that nothing is told, then gives room and sees the refusal; and, in Chromium alone (the other browsers have no such command here, and the two tests skip), the protocol is told to end a running script (`Runtime.terminateExecution`, while a library read spins, with a `finally` of its own that must never run) and to collect what nothing holds (`HeapProfiler.collectGarbage`, with a `WeakRef` to a sheet, to show that nothing holds a lost workbook's sheets while the watch waits for room, nor once its tab is refused, and that a workbook on show is held, as the control). Every refusal there is also compared with `IMPORT_WORKBOOK_NO_MEMORY_REASON`, a CSV file chosen beside the lost workbook is imported, and the same workbook chosen again is read. The screenshot states `import-workbook`, `import-workbook-sheet`, `import-workbook-warning` and `import-report-workbook` of `tests/ui-states.js` hold the dialog and the report for the pictures and for axe (`ui-a11y` runs every state).

## 10. Build, deploy and tests

| Item | Facts |
|---|---|
| `Makefile` | `sqlite` (re-download the engine into `vendor/`) and `serve` (`python3 -m http.server $(PORT)`, default 8000). |
| `npm run build:editor` | One of the two build steps: `tools/build-editor.mjs` bundles CodeMirror into `vendor/editor.js` (committed; section 4.5). |
| `npm run build:app` | The other: `tools/build-app.mjs` joins the modules and the stylesheets of `index.html` into `bundle/app.js` and `bundle/app.css` (committed; `bundle/` in section 1). Run it after changing `js/` or `css/`; `tests/app-bundle.spec.js` fails until you do. None of the site's code is built or checked at deploy (`pages.yml` runs no npm: an AC7 test keeps `make` and `npm ci`, `install` and `run` out of it): the site gets `bundle/` as committed, so push a change to `js/` or `css/` with its rebuild. `EXPOSE` in the tool lists the twenty-one modules `app.js` exports by name for the tests (`tests/app-bundle.spec.js` holds the same list; Phase 5 added `importCells`, `importDb`, `importWorkbook` and `importZip`, so that a page test can try the typed table, the zip check and the reading of a workbook with the library and the engine as the page has them); the build prints what it wrote, each file's size and how many modules and stylesheets went in (on Nov 19, 2026: 454,195 bytes from 78 modules and 108,779 bytes from 16 stylesheets, 118,735 and 23,497 bytes at gzip level 9). |
| `.github/workflows/pages.yml` | On push to `main` and manual dispatch: `actions/checkout@v7`, `actions/configure-pages@v6`, an `rsync` staging step into `.site-stage/`, the step "List exercise sets" (section 7.1), the step "Link previews and web manifest" (`node tools/site-meta.mjs --stage .site-stage --url <the address configure-pages gives, its output base_url>`; below), `actions/upload-pages-artifact@v5` with `include-hidden-files: true` (without it the action tars with `--exclude=.[^/]*` and silently drops `.nojekyll`; it always drops `.git` and `.github`), `actions/deploy-pages@v5`. Repository setting needed once: Settings, Pages, Source = GitHub Actions. |
| What ships | The repo root minus `.git`, `.github`, `.gitignore`, `.site-stage`, `tests/`, `node_modules/`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `playwright.config.js`, `docs/` and `tools/` (the `--exclude` list in `pages.yml`; the test server's `HIDDEN` set must equal it, an AC7 test compares). Everything else at the root is public, `CODEMAP.md`, `README.md`, `Makefile` and `datasets/generate_db.py` included. |
| `.nojekyll` | Empty file at the root; without it Pages runs Jekyll, which drops underscore-prefixed paths. |
| URLs | Every fetched or linked URL is relative (the site serves from `https://<user>.github.io/<repo>/`). AC7 greps shipped HTML/CSS/JS for root-absolute URLs and origin-based path building. |
| Link previews | `tools/site-meta.mjs` (Node's own modules only; it reads `js/lab/config.js` and `css/tokens.css` as text) writes into each staged page, between `<!-- social:begin -->` and `<!-- social:end -->` in its `<head>`: `og:type`, `og:site_name`, `og:title` (as `brand.js` titles the page: the app's name, "About - <name>", "Check evidence - <name>"), `og:description` (the page's own description), `og:url`, `og:image` (`img/social.png`, the site's one absolute URL), its size and text, `twitter:card`, and the link to `site.webmanifest`, which it writes beside them (the name, the desk colour, the three icons; `start_url` and `scope` relative). The address is `SITE_URL` (`config.js`) when set, else `--url`; with neither, the tags that need one are left out. Running it again replaces its tags. The repository's own pages carry only the markers: no tag names the app or holds an address there (`m2-brand.spec.js`). |

**Adding a page to the site** (a root `<name>.html`, deployed automatically because the whole root ships). Copy `about.html` or `verify.html` and keep: `<title data-page-title="Part">`, the `<!-- social:begin -->` and `<!-- social:end -->` markers (and add the page to `PAGES` in `tools/site-meta.mjs` with the title part `brand.js` gives it), `data-app-name` on empty elements in the header and footer (the app name is never typed), `<script type="module" src="js/lab/brand.js">`, `css/article.css`, a `<meta name="description">` of more than 20 characters that does not name the app, relative `href`/`src` only, no `og:*` tags, and the footer credit "Made by Abdou Sadou, based on SQLime by Anton Zhiyanov" written as plain HTML. Scripts go in `js/lab/` and use `createElement`/`textContent`. The tests that enumerate pages: `m2-brand.spec.js` (title/header/footer rename check, "sqlime" appears only inside the credit, credit present, metadata; add the page to each list), `ac6-no-third-party.spec.js` (a session through the pages), `ac7-pages.spec.js` and `m2-no-sharing.spec.js` (scan every root `*.html` automatically).

## 11. Tests

Playwright (`@playwright/test` 1.65.0, dev only; `npm ci`, `npx playwright install chromium firefox`, `npm test`, some 16,700 tests, about thirty-five minutes with six workers on a fast desktop: run the files of what you changed first). `playwright.config.js` starts `tests/static-server.mjs <port> <mount>` twice, at `TEST_PORT_BASE` (default 4173) mounted at `/` and at `TEST_PORT_BASE+1` mounted at `/repo/`, with projects `{chromium,firefox}-{root,subpath}` (plus `webkit-*` with `PW_WEBKIT=1`); `ui-visual.spec.js` is left out of every project but `chromium-root` (`testIgnore`). The server is zero-dependency, answers 404 outside its mount (so a root-absolute URL fails on the subpath run), sends no COOP/COEP and no compression, hides what the workflow leaves out, and answers `exercises/` with the workflow's folder page. Tests navigate only with relative URLs and are named after their acceptance criterion where one applies.

`tests/helpers.js` is the shared kit; extend it rather than duplicating. `test` extends Playwright's with `net` (`NetworkLog`: every request; external and same-origin-failure assertions; body bytes), `app` (`App`: `open`, `openEmpty`, `waitReady` (the results pane left its loading state), `run` (fills the editor, runs, and waits for the run: a Run starts one frame after it is asked for), `settle` (waits on `window.app.whenIdle()`, after a Run pressed some other way), `query` (runs and reads the table), `read`, `upload`, `rename`, `copyLink` (stubs the clipboard with `stubClipboard`, which records what is written in `window.__copied`, and returns the copied link), `exportFile`, `openSet`, `check`, `exportEvidence`, `readLog`, `seedLog`, getters for the results pane (`pane`, `status`, `summary`, `result`, `table`, `errorBand`, `emptyState`, `samples`, `announcer`, `toast`, `shareField`), the panel, picker, history, evidence and CSV elements, ...) and `pageErrors`. Never wait for text a previous run could already show: wait for the run (`run`, `settle`), then assert. `App` drives the shell through the real controls: `pickDatabase(name)` and `pickDatabaseById(id)` (the switcher's menu), `databaseItems` / `databaseItem(id)` (its entries, readable while it is closed), `expectPicked(id, name)` (exactly that entry ticked, and the button's name; `null`: nothing ticked), `exportMenuButton` / `exportMenu` / `openExportMenu()`, `openEvidence()`, `tab(name)` / `openTab(name)` / `openHistory()` for the sidebar, `openSetMenu()`, `pickSet(label)` and `setMenu` for the set picker, `databaseButton` / `databaseLabel` / `databaseMenu` (`picker` is the switcher's button), `rename(name)` and `exportFile()` through the Export menu's dialog (`rename` exports too: Enter in the dialog renames and saves). The editor: `app.editor` is its typing surface (`EDITOR_INPUT`: CodeMirror's `.cm-content`, or `#editor` itself when it is the plain box) for `fill`, `press`, focus and the textbox's attributes, and `app.editorHost` is `#editor` for its methods; `editorValue(page)` reads the text (never `innerText`: CodeMirror's markup holds the line numbers and the placeholder), `editorSelect(page, anchor, head)` selects by text offsets in either editor, and `expectEditorTextOnly(page)` checks that nothing made from pasted or dropped markup is inside. Two plain functions: `tabTo(page, selector, {back, max})` presses Tab or Shift+Tab until the selector has the focus (keys only; it returns whether it got there; Tab indents in the editor, so a walk through it presses Escape there) and `expectSchemaIconsOnly(page)` (nothing in the Schema tab was made from a name: only its own icon svgs and the few tags it is built from). Exports include `openFresh` (a link in a brand-new context), `PAYLOAD` / `ATTR_PAYLOAD` (injection strings), `realManifest`, `DEFAULT_DATASET` (its entry with `"default": true`), `NORTHLIGHT_URL` (`./?db=northlight`), `routeManifest`, `withSampleDataset` (a manifest with a `sample` entry plus `datasets/sample.db` backed by `tests/fixtures/sample.db`), `realSet(id)` (one shipped set, read from `exercises/`; Northlight Retail's by default), `shippedSets()` (all of them, in the menu's order: by id), `openableSets()` (the shipped sets whose course database the site ships too, which are the ones the page can open: the specs that need "a set to work in" take it from here, and skip where there is none), `routeSet`, `routeListing`, `listingHtml`, `modelQuery(id)` and `evidenceFile(spec)` (a second implementation of the file format, used as an oracle), `failStorage` / `setStorageFailing` (Storage methods that throw on demand, per page or context, optionally only for a key prefix or a value over some length) and `fakeStorage` / `inNode` (a `localStorage` and a `document` for modules run in Node, and `tab()` for a private copy of `js/lab/` per simulated tab; a query string cannot make a second instance, because the runner's loader drops it). For the import (section 9.6): `app.chooseFiles(files)` and `app.dropFiles(files, {target})` (paths, or `{name, mimeType, buffer}`), `app.importDialog` / `importTabs` / `importPanel` / `importSubmit` / `importCancel`, `app.importFiles(files, {into, database, names})` (the whole journey, to the report), `app.rows(sql)`, and the plain functions `fixturePath(name)`, `expectedFor(name)` and `fixtureIndex()` (the owner's files and the result each must give), `largeCsv()` (writes the 200,000-row file once per machine, in the system's temporary folder, and checks it against the checksum in the fixtures), `readTable(app, name)` and `compareTable(actual, expected)`. The table export's helpers (Phase 3): `tablesItem`, `moreTablesItem`, `tablesDialog` and its parts (`tablesAll`, `tablesHold`, `tablesError`, `tablesSubmit`, `tablesCancel`, `tablesNote`, `tablesProgress`), `tablesRow(name)` and `tablesBox(name)` (by the name, exactly), `openTables()`, `waitCounted()` (waits until the line beside Export says what it says once counting has ended, which cannot be true before, with no row left saying the counting words; the words are the page's own, from `config-workflow.js`), `tickTables(names)`, `tablesRows()`, `download(trigger)` (the file the page saves, as `{name, buffer, text}`) and `exportTables(names | "all")`; and, apart from `App`, `repeatTaken(page)` (whether the page is taking repeats of Enter away at this moment: one is handed to the window itself), `watchClosing(page, selector, {freshPress})` with `closingSeen(page)` (a key handed to the page in the moment between a dialog going and the browser's `close` event for it, by a MutationObserver on the dialog's `open` attribute: a press of Enter that is not a repeat, or a repeat to whatever has the focus; and what was found there, with whether it really was before the event), `readZip(bytes)` and `crc32` (a zip read without the library that wrote it: the end record and the central directory by hand, each entry inflated with `node:zlib` and its CRC-32 checked, its local header matched to the directory (also its date and time, and its CRC-32 and sizes unless a data descriptor says them), a version needed above 2.0 refused, and so are a disk number other than 0, a name that comes twice, is empty, starts with `/`, holds a backslash or a `..` part, or is a folder's and holds data, entries that overlap, and bytes that belong to no entry; each entry comes with its `method`, `flags`, `versionNeeded` and `mtime`), and the ways of holding a long job and of looking at what it left behind, which the import's and the export's page specs share: `holdVendor(page, file)` (a library's file through a gate), `holdImport(page, app)` and `finishHeld` (an import held while it waits for its reader, and let go), `holdDataset` (a course database's file through a gate: `release()` or `fail()`), `slowDown` (every turn the page gives itself between two pieces costs 15 ms, so a long job takes seconds), `snapshot` and `expectNothingLeft` (what a cancelled job must leave as it was: the same database, name, tables, address and title, no TEMP table, no event, no report, the connection in the state it was in (a statement open or not, so that a job that leaves one fails the check; a transaction or a savepoint open or not, the databases attached, and the settings `foreign_keys`, `recursive_triggers`, `full_column_names` and `short_column_names`), and, unless `snapshot` is given `{content: false}`, the same content, by `databaseFingerprint`: the SHA-256 of the bytes the engine serialises, and `user_version`), `waitUntilIdle`, `databaseHandle` and `isOpenDatabase` (whether the open database is still that one), `schemaNames`, `eventKeys` and `eventsOf` (what the evidence log holds, in memory and in storage), `logKey(page, dataset?)` (the log's key for a file, or for a course database when it is named) and `openLogKey` (the key of whatever is open), and `csvFile(name, text)` (a CSV made in a test). What the specs of the import of workbooks share (Phase 5, section 9.8): `app.importSheetStrip(label)` (a workbook's strip of sheets, by its label) and `app.importSheetPanel` (the panel of the sheet on show; `app.importTabs` is the outer strip of files alone, so it still counts files), `workbookFile(name, sheets, bookType)` (a workbook made in Node by the library: sheets `[{name, rows, hidden}]`), `owned(name)` (an owner's file as a file chosen beside one made in the test: a path cannot be mixed with buffers), `BASENAME`, `BYTES`, `tablesOf(expected)`, `dialogReady(app)` (every file looked at and every workbook read), `expectTables(app, tables)` (each table against its expected file, and no other table), `eventLines(file, bytes, tables)`, `escaped(text)` (a text as a pattern that matches it alone), `holdLine(app)` (the line beside Import), `MB`, `OLE2_START`, `wideLastRowCsv()` (a CSV whose last row, after the first megabyte, has 2,001 fields) and `gateTurns(page)` (holds the import's next turn at a title and a part of the progress that the dialog shows, whatever the machine's speed: `at(title, part)`, `held()`, `release()`). What the export's specs share: `EXPORT_URL` (the address that opens the page with the export alone on, `./?${flagState("export")}`), `EDGE_DATABASE` and `openEdgeCases(app)` (the owner's edge-case database opened as a file, with the export alone on), `recordDownloads(page)` (the names of the files the page saves), `eventsOfOpen(page)`, `expectedBytes(file)` (an expected CSV of the fixtures) and `ZIP_TOOL` (the file name of the zip library, for `holdVendor` and for the requests). What the two page specs of the long jobs share beside the holds: `openStatements(page)` (0 when the engine has no statement open on the page's connection), `autocommit(page)` (1 when no transaction is open), `progressKey(set)` (the storage key of a set's progress) and `storedEventLines(page, storageKey)` (the lines of each event the browser keeps under a key, or `null`). What the two rules specs share: `skipUnlessFirst` (a `beforeEach` that skips on every project but the first, for a spec that checks files and not the page) and `seeded(seed)` (the same numbers on every run). What stays in `aw2-import-page.spec.js` differs from these: `slowCopy` (it slows the copy and lets the reading go) and `holdLastTurn` (the import's last turn, held until the test lets it go; the export dialog's Escape test builds a hold of its own, which it lets go from inside an event). `tests/fixtures/` holds `sample.db` and `make_fixtures.py`, and the owner's import and export files with their README (never edited by a test; `evidence/` is never made again). The page ships with the import, the export and the import of workbooks on, and an address can switch each flag on or off for one visit, so a test that is about a state of the flags names that state in full: `flagState(...on)` gives the address parameter in which every flag is named, the ones listed by their name and all the others with a minus (`flagState("import")` is `preview=import,-export,-excel-import,-excel-export` and `flagState()` has every flag off; the names come from `PREVIEW_FLAGS` of the config, and a name that is no flag throws, so that a typing fault cannot pass for a state). A test that names its state keeps testing that state whatever the shipped defaults are; a test of the page as it ships opens the plain address. `lineFit(locator)` says how a line of text sits in its box: `cut` when part of it cannot be seen (an ellipsis, or a box lower than its text; the widths are compared in fractions of a pixel, since a line one pixel too long already ends in an ellipsis) and `lines`, the number of lines it is drawn on. A visit to `./` opens the default dataset (`DEFAULT_DATASET`) and nothing runs on its own (the results pane shows "no result yet" with its samples), so a test that wants an empty start uses `app.openEmpty()`. The tests are written against Northlight, which need not be the default: a test that uses its tables, its samples or its id opens it by id (`app.open(NORTHLIGHT_URL)`), and a test of the default itself opens `./` and reads `DEFAULT_DATASET`. Only the manifest test and AC1 (`m3-datasets`) name the shipped default, on purpose. The screenshot recipes (`tests/ui-states.js`) open Northlight by id as well, and make it the default for the damaged link, so a change of default redraws no picture; the database menu's pictures show every dataset, so a change to the list redraws those.

| Spec file | Covers |
|---|---|
| `smoke`, `static-server`, `ac3-upload-roundtrip`, `ac6-no-third-party`, `ac7-pages`, `ac8-keyboard`, `ac9-size` | Boot and requests; the harness itself; AC3 (upload, export, re-upload); AC6 (zero third-party requests, including a session through check, share, history, evidence and verify); AC7 (workflow, `.nojekyll`, relative URLs, subpath); AC8 (keyboard run and check, real `<table>`); AC9 (transfer size). |
| `m2-brand`, `m2-no-sharing`, `m2-injection`, `m2-restore`, `m2-editor-keys`, `m2-table-names`, `f3-share` | App-name constant and rename; gist/token code gone; markup in every untrusted string; restored SQL never runs; Escape-then-Tab; hostile table names; F3 (AC4, AC6, damaged links, size guard, clipboard refused). |
| `m3-datasets`, `m3-audit-fixes`, `m3-export`, `m3-schema` | AC1, AC2, the `v_sales` total (£68,323.31), F1 (the database switcher, `?db=`, history, manifest validation, failed fetches, stale loads, pristine bytes, phone width); the stale-load guards; the Export menu and its dialog (the rename, the keyboard path); F4 (the Schema tab, the sidebar and its splitter). |
| `m4-runner`, `m4-checker`, `m4-exercises`, `m4-listing`, `m4-panel`, `m4-audit-fixes` | `splitStatements`/`runSql`/`engineMessage`/the guard; the checker on Northlight (AC5 engine half, fresh connections, model cache); sets, listing, progress, `?set=` routes; the deploy listing step run in bash and compared with the test server and `python3 -m http.server`; the panel (AC4, AC5, AC6, AC8 with the panel, all ten model queries of Northlight Retail's set pass with their row counts, and every model query of each other set that ships passes its own check, the sets being read from `exercises/`; the model query nowhere in the DOM, feedback rules, progress, layout at 1280/768/390 in both colour schemes); storage that throws. |
| `constraints` | The engine-guard (`sqlite3.*` parameters, plain and percent-encoded, alone and with `?db=` and a share fragment: no outside request, the app works, the others survive, a clean address untouched; a page path starting with `//` on a made-up origin served by `serveRepoAt` in `helpers.js`; a guard that cannot rewrite the address stops the page; the guard alone on a minimal page served by `page.route`: other engine warnings pass unchanged, the step warning is cut to three arguments, `history.state` survives the rewrite); plain-text only (real drag and drop and real copy/paste in both browsers: no element, no request, text at the drop point, one line in the name, a file dropped cancelled and putting nothing in a field, on the page as it ships (where it is also taken as a chosen file) and with every flag off (where it is ignored), a drag from inside left alone, and forgotten at the next pointer move, press or key when no `dragend` came); `.sql` scripts under the guard (heap limit, ATTACH, VACUUM INTO refused, a plain VACUUM that mentions INTO in a comment still loads, a normal script with a trigger, view and BEGIN/COMMIT loads, the URL route); the editor's `autocorrect` / `autocapitalize`. |
| `m5-results`, `m5-run-log`, `m5-evidence-format`, `m5-evidence-ui`, `m5-evidence-dialog`, `m5-verify-page`, `m5-audit-fixes`, `m5-durability` | F5 (cap, status, failing statement, CSV in the result summary and in the Export menu, status icons); F6 (log, cap, two tabs, quota, the History tab, layout, contrast); the pure file format (in Node; with the time the check takes when a run of 200,000 line breaks sits before the end of the file); AC10 end to end; the dialog and the student number going nowhere else; `verify.html` (paste, drop, file, Verify checking again what was given last, a paste with a lone CR checked as it was pasted while the box shows it, and after one the box not checked at all unless it is empty or shows exactly the last paste with no undo or redo since, through edits, undos after the box was emptied, undos after a later paste (the doctored copy left alone after the original), a redo, and a lone CR as the last line break; a paste spliced into a CRLF file; every kind of edit); `m5-durability`: the run log with two tabs after a full storage, an unreadable log, the size limit of a memory-only log, the notice with two logs or two sets, progress after a failed write, a bare `/*`, the editor toolbar above a long query at 640 to 1280 px, a long word in the editor (its Node tests run copies of `js/lab/` against `fakeStorage`, see `inNode` in `tests/helpers.js`); `m5-audit-fixes`: a Run followed at once by leaving the page (navigation and reload), the write being synchronous, two tabs 25 ms apart, a full storage and its notice, escaped header-shaped SQL and the count, the verdict in view, contrast of the evidence error and the command bar, UTC on history times, the hint, the editor toolbar and the top bar at 768, and the About and README statements. |
| `ui1-tokens`, `ui-a11y` (+ `ui-states.js`) | The UI plan's Phase 1: no hex colour outside `tokens.css`, the plan's token values, contrast of every pairing in both themes, every `var()` defined (script-set `--sidebar-width` and `--editor-height` allowed); the theme menu (device by default, a choice that wins, survives a reload, is on `<html>` before `<body>` exists and reaches About and verify, Match device forgets it, keys and focus, light dismiss, blocked storage); the typefaces (used, same-origin, eight files under 200 KB, licences); the sprite (exactly the icons used, 16 px, takes no room). `ui-a11y`: axe-core finds no serious or critical violation in any state of the screenshot matrix (`RECIPES.shell.states` of `tests/ui-states.js`, also used by `tools/ui-matrix.mjs --ui shell`; from Phase 3 it includes `menu-completion`, the editor's list of names open) in either theme at 1366, 1920, 1024 and 390 px (and no page scroll sideways at 360 px), and a keyboard-only session opens a database (the switcher's menu), runs a query and passes an exercise (the Exercises tab, its set menu, Check answer). |

| `aw0-flags`, `aw0-vendor-loader`, `aw0-events`, `aw0-evidence` | The assessment plan's Phase 0, groundwork (`aw` for assessment workflow, then the phase). **Flags**: the rule in Node, given its defaults as an argument (a table of flags that are on and flags that are off, so that the tests of the rule do not depend on what ships), and the shipped defaults (three on, `excel-export` off, the parameter is `preview`): names, commas, several parameters, spaces, unknown and prototype names, letter case, the needs and a chain of them, a very long value and a malformed address, and the minus (a name with a hyphen-minus switches a flag off; off wins in either order and across several parameters; a minus on a flag that is off leaves it off; a flag that needs one that was switched off is off; the result is a new object and the table is left as it was); and in the page: with no parameter the flags are the shipped ones, a minus switches one off and no other (`-import` leaves `excel-import` off), read once (a flag stays as the address set it, and the parameter leaves the address, when a dataset, a file, a set or an exercise opens; Open from URL keeps it), never in a copied link or an evidence link, with an address with a minus too. **What a flag alone changes** is shown by comparing two pages, each named in full with `flagState` (the kit, below): with the one flag that nothing reads yet (`excel-export`) on and the others off, the page shows what it shows with every flag off (menus, the one-file chooser, toasts, storage, history, requests), with the import alone on and nothing chosen yet only the chooser (several files), the words under "Open a file…", the import's dialog element and its eight listeners on the window and the document differ, and with the export alone on and nothing done yet only one entry more in each of the two export menus, the dialog's element and its one listener on the document differ (no request, no storage key, no toast, no step in the history), and with the import and `excel-import` both on and nothing done yet (the export off) the page is the page with the import alone but for two sentences, the words under "Open a file…" and the dialog's (the third, the toast for a mixed choice, is held in its own test, with the words of both flags), and a visit that chooses two text files and imports them makes the same dialog, requests (none for the library of workbooks), stored keys, tables, events and report with both flags as with `import` alone, but for the dialog's sentence. The comparison of the page with every flag off against the page with the unread flag, with the import alone, with the export alone and with both import flags (what each adds taken out of the markup first) is a deep one, written down at four moments (when the page has loaded, after a query, after the database export's dialog, and at the end, after both menus on a narrow window): the whole markup, localStorage and sessionStorage, and where the focus is; the listeners put on the window, the document, its root, its body and every element that has an id; what four key chords do; ArrowDown through the Export menu and through the More menu of a phone, and End, Home, ArrowUp, ArrowDown and Escape in each (what the page did with the key, and where the focus went); the names of the window's own properties; the timers asked for with a delay of three seconds or more; the files asked for, each as many times, up to the end of the visit; and, read once more at the very end, the length of the history and the calls of `pushState` and `replaceState`, the keys of `window.app`, the cookies, the databases of IndexedDB, the number of style sheets and of adopted ones, and the rules in each. The page as it ships is compared too, in the same way: the plain address is the page of `flagState("import", "export", "excel-import")` (the shipped defaults are that state and nothing more), `?preview=-import,-export` is the page of `flagState()`, and the unread flag named on beside the three that ship leaves the page as it ships. Only a run's time, in the two places where the status line writes it, and the run log's time stamp are made equal first. The page is written down once it has come to rest (`look`): a quarter of a second, counted by a timer of the page itself, and two frames in which nothing in the document changed. Some of what the page holds is written a moment after the event that caused it (the limits of the editor's splitter by a ResizeObserver, the words of the results pane's live region 60 ms after a run, the editor's mark of the focus), and on a busy machine two pages that were the same were read on either side of such a write. A fault put in the branch of `mountTableExport` that runs with the flag off (a node, a late request, a file asked for a second time, a storage key, a key taken, a write on a press, a listener on a menu, a property of the window, a timer set for later, a step in the history made late, a cookie, a database of IndexedDB, a rule put into a style sheet, a key of `window.app`) fails the comparison with the export alone on, which is the one that can show it: the page with every flag off and the page with the unread flag would both have it. A fault in the branch the import takes when it is off shows in the same way against the page with the import alone on, and only there. What the comparison cannot see is a change that both pages carry, one made outside every flag's branch: no test compares the page with the page as it was before the phase (that was done once by hand, in both browsers, and showed no difference). Nor can it see a write that is due after the last reading, about five seconds after the page's script starts, unless it comes from one timer asked for with three seconds or more. The tests take the exercise set (one that the page can open) and the second course database from what ships, and skip themselves where there is none. **Loader**: a first visit fetches no library; one relative `<script>`, one request however many callers; a failure (aborted, a 404 page, not the library, no answer in time, a page that cannot add the script) rejects every caller with an Error, removes the script, and the next call tries again; a name with a folder or a line break in it, and a library name that is not text, are refused before anything else, even with the library on the page; a late event of an attempt that has ended leaves the next attempt alone (the one test that runs the module in Node, with a made-up document and timers); a name the page will not let anyone read counts as a library that is not there; nothing shown or logged; no `define`, `module` or `exports` on the page. **Events** (in Node, as the run log's storage tests): what is stored and under which key, `after`, refusals, the lines (one line each, cut, capped), the caps, strict reading, a later version (any number above this one) never written over, full or unreadable storage (the most of the newest that fit, in a few writes), two tabs (whose events go at the cap, the merge second by second and its known limit within a second, events that differ in one field only, equal events matched one to one), no log key, copies; `timeline` (the rule case by case, and 300 random cases that keep every query in order and every item once); in the page, the History tab and the run log untouched. **Evidence**: the three saved version 1 files rebuilt byte for byte and judged as they were, also on the verify page; version 2 against an independent writer in the spec file; the escape rule of each version (in version 2, the event header's shape whatever kind it names); what the writer takes for an event; the checks (events only, no events, the first line with and without its space, an unknown kind alone or among known ones with its whole reason, a kind that holds a CR or a line separator, numbers out of sequence, a deletion, an insertion and six replacements at every position of a file, a CRLF copy); the dialog with events (the file, the links, the toast's wording, events and no queries); the verify page's Events row, and the scripts it loads: its own five, and none of `config-workflow.js`, the events, the flags, the loader or the bundle. |

So has each gap that was found by changing the import's modules about 850 times, one change at a time: the 95 changes that passed every test and made a difference that can be shown each fail a test now (the groups called "edges a sweep by mutation found unheld"). So are a table called `cast`, which SQL takes after FROM and refuses in front of a column; columns called `with` and `true` (refused in parentheses; the constant through a subquery); and a table called `with` with a column `With` (one word, named once). Back and Forward under the dialog; the second look of `run()` at the destination, and a new database that cannot be made the open one (a second import mounted by hand, section 9.6); while an import runs (held by gating the reader, section 9.6) Run, Check, the database export, the switcher and its menu, Ctrl+O and Ctrl+U, files that are chosen, an exercise set and a drop are refused and Back and Forward put the address back; Cancel at each moment leaves nothing (no table, no database, no event, no connection, the student's transaction still open, and the bytes of the database as they were: `snapshot` and `expectNothingLeft` of `tests/helpers.js` compare a SHA-256 of them), while the file is read and while it is copied into its table (the copy runs in steps and shows them); the dialog's keys while an import runs (Enter held on Import does not go on to press Cancel, one Enter on Cancel still cancels, and Escape between two steps, the last step held by the test, stops the import, which is undone to the byte) and once the dialog has closed under a held Enter (after Import, after Cancel, and after Escape with the key held on a tab: the repeats type nothing into the editor and open no menu, and Enter makes a line again once the key is let go); the moment between the dialog going and the browser's `close` event, which the kit's `watchClosing` makes (after Escape, a repeat of Enter that comes in it types nothing into the editor; after Escape and after Cancel, a press of Enter that is not a repeat is not followed by a new guard); a `cancel` event that comes up from inside the dialog, which ends nothing; an import, and a Cancel in the middle of one, in a browser without `scheduler.yield`; after an import into a new database (the address, the title, the switcher, the status bar, the editor, the History tab, the event's log key) and into the open one (`lab:database-changed` and no run, the Schema tab and the editor's names, the status bar as it was); a database with a page limit, and an engine out of memory, with what the message says of the student's transaction; names in the open database (taken in any letter case, a TEMP table or view of the same name, a TEMP table with the staging table's name); the report (long names and values cut, its ids, its warning of a quote that is never closed); events the browser cannot keep, the notice for them (word for word the sentence Phase 2 had, while the import is the only flag on), and axe on it; the Result view on a phone; the staging table (a TEMP table: no page left free in the database); a refusal found during the import (a row too wide after the first megabyte, a file that cannot be read any more) leaves nothing; with several files, a refusal or a Cancel in a later file undoes the tables of the files before it; where files may go (a script, a database from a URL, the empty database, an earlier import); About (what is remembered, the credit). Where the import shares code with the export of tables, the specs also hold: an import that ends by itself ends its session before the dialog closes; Escape heard the second time in a visit, in a dialog opened again; a dialog closed from script, which is still cleared; only a repeat of Enter taken away in the dialog, so that a held letter still repeats in a field; an import undone inside the student's own transaction leaving no savepoint behind (the name is the code's own, `SAVEPOINT`); the engine's allocator failing while rows are staged told as a lack of memory; and, in Node, `nextFrame` letting its caller go on in a task after the frame's callbacks and not inside them. The page tests that pick a course database while something is held start from one that is not the default, taken from what ships, and skip themselves where only one ships. **What the wording of the import holds** (the three gaps closed before students had the import): in `aw2-import-rules` the pattern of the three endings (`OTHER_SPREADSHEET_NAME`: each ending in any letter case, the ending at the end of the name, `notes.ods.csv` not one of them); in `aw2-import-page`, with `import` alone, a file ending in `.xlsm`, `.xlsb` or `.ods` (and `REPORT.XLSM`), chosen and dropped, alone gives one refused tab with the reason of `importOtherSpreadsheetReason(ending, false)`, only Close, no destination, nothing asked for from `vendor/`, no page error and nothing left, a real workbook under `.xlsm` is refused in the same words, beside a CSV file the CSV is imported, beside a database or a script the toast is the mixed choice, `notes.ods.csv` is read as CSV, and with the import off such a file is tried as a database; the sentence and the report of files that gave no table (a CSV beside a workbook and an empty file: the summary line, the toast and the announcement are `importedWithLeftMessage`, the report's first element is the paragraph with the names in the tabs' order, the events are the CSV file's alone, a name of markup is text, a name with a right-to-left override is in a `<bdi>` of its own and the parts are drawn left to right, a name of 200 characters is cut with the whole in `title`, one of exactly 80 is whole, Cancel leaves nothing, and with nothing left out the old sentence and no paragraph); and in `aw2-import-dialog` (group 20) the note under a name that the open database has: the words, the field described by the hint and the note, not invalid, no alert, gone when typed in and not back when the text is typed back, gone with a new database and back with the open one, given for a view, an index and another letter case, the sentence of its own for a name that only a temporary table or view has (`importNameMadeUniqueTemporaryNote`, which does not name the database; a name that both have gets the database's sentence, and two files beside one of each kind get one each), none for two files of one name in one choice, `x_3` with the note for a name the database has and an earlier tab took, a refused or left-out file giving its name up, the error alone beside a typed or reserved name, the layout at 1366x768, 1920x1080 and 390x844, and axe. The page as it ships (the plain address, no parameter) has tests of its own: the chooser takes several files without a list of kinds, the menu entry says what else it takes, a first visit asks for no library, a chosen CSV file opens the dialog and imports, and a dropped file is taken as a chosen one. |
