the same stand-in while the list is read gives the toast, no dialog, and no statement left open; and one that gives no name for a column while a table is opened); a reader that cannot be closed, and something that throws once the browser has the file; Export pressed in a dialog under which an import began or another database opened; the idle dialog keeping Ctrl+O, Ctrl+U and "Open a file…" shut, and a Run asked in the turn in which an export starts; the timer that looks at Cancel while the zip tool is awaited; and an export from a course database (its event under the database's own key, in an evidence file that verifies; the zip named after the file and not after the switcher's label). The page and dialog tests take the second course database and the exercise sets from what ships, and skip themselves where there is none. The page spec also holds the kit's own check of what a job leaves, `expectNothingLeft`, to its faults: a transaction or a savepoint left open, a database attached, each of the four settings changed, a statement left open, a TEMP table, `user_version` and a row each make it throw, and a read, a transaction rolled back, a database detached again and a statement finalized do not. Each fix that was made to the code after its first build has a test that was seen to fail without it; a few tests of the first build pin a fact of the engine or a rule that no change of the product can break, and were not seen to fail. `tests/ui-states.js` gains the dialog's list, running and note states (`export-dialog`, `export-progress`, `export-note`), `aw0-flags` the group of what the flag alone adds, and `app-bundle` the new stylesheet. The page as it ships has its own group in `aw3-export-page`: the entry sits right after Database in both menus with the config's words, the dialog is in the page, the chooser takes several files and no zip tool is asked for, the phone's More menu has the entry, and a minus (`preview=-export`) switches only the export off for that visit. |
| `aw5-excel-rules`, `aw5-excel-fixtures`, `aw5-excel-dialog`, `aw5-excel-limits`, `aw5-excel-page` (and `aw5-excel-reading.mjs`) | The assessment plan's Phase 5, import of Excel workbooks (section 9.8; a test of the import of workbooks opens its page with `flagState("import", "excel-import")`, and the tests of the flag being off open it with `flagState("import")` or with `flagState()`). The owner's workbooks in `tests/fixtures/` are the measure and are never edited; a workbook no fixture holds is made in the test (section 9.8 says how). A text a student reads is compared with the constant or the function of `config-workflow.js` (loaded in Node by file URL, the old words kept for `import` alone), and every new text is also typed out once, in the Node spec, so that a changed or swapped word fails a test. **`aw5-excel-rules`** (in Node, once, on the first project only, no page): the limits and the texts and `tallyNumber`; the events; the vendored library (size, SHA-256, first line, version) and its licence; the formats (what is a date, a duration, a format that pads with zeros, against lists of formats), a number written out in full, the calendar at its edges in both date systems (0 and 0.5, 1, 59, 60, 61, 1.5 under a time format, the last day and its last half day, one past it, negative), seconds at the half and at the day's end, durations; the value of a cell kind by kind; a sheet (the rectangle, its state, the name of its table), merged cells, the table of a sheet (types, names, blanks, stored values, a header cell that is a number or a date, names off with the row box set), the report's two facts; the wiring of the library (what it throws, a string, a TypeError, sheets called `constructor` and `__proto__`, a web page of two tables and of none, an XML spreadsheet, the options object that is new for each call); the limits at their edges (what counts as sheet data and what does not, a part's name in another letter case or with backslashes); the zip check (sound for every zip fixture, damaged for a changed bit in a part's data and in a stored CRC, cut at three places and the library never called, Zip64 giving no verdict, a directory longer than the tail); every workbook and impostor of the fixtures read through the three modules from its bytes against its expected file (the sheets in order with their states, the names, each column's name, type, nulls and report example, every cell, the second table of the sheets that give a `header_row`), and the same reading in child processes with other time zones (`aw5-excel-reading.mjs`: it writes down what the modules give and checks nothing); copies of a workbook with one bit changed (a fixed seed: each copy the check passes is read in a worker thread under a time limit and gives the original's cells or a refusal, and none the check refuses is handed to the library); a cell where no sheet has one; a row walked by its keys and merges that cost what they are, with the time bounded; a format the library left out of a cell; NaN and Infinity; what the library keeps of a workbook once it has read it (after an .xlsx it is given the small workbook that holds nothing, after an .xls or a web page none; the texts are not held once the sheets are let go, measured inside a function that has returned and with a workbook that has shared texts, as Excel writes; each workbook's sheets hold the number formats of their own workbook); and the memory watch (what an engine says when it has no memory, told in a reason of its own; a call begun and not ended told of at every turn until the telling returns, one that has ended never; the watch set while the library reads and taken away at every outcome; a step that fails for want of memory after the library's call told by the watch and one that fails otherwise a fault as it was; the watch telling only once the strings are let go; `watchStep`; the room it asks for, 200,000 small objects; a file whose bytes the browser cannot hold; a sheet the library leaves out, in a large workbook and in a small one). **`aw5-excel-fixtures`** (page): each workbook of the fixtures chosen in the page and imported through the real dialog with every sheet that can be ticked ticked (the sheets as the expected file lists them, the tables with names, declared types, `typeof` of the stored values and every cell, the report's facts, the event's lines); the plan's "Done when" line by line (`shopping_db.xlsx` as four tables, the first order's date `2023-08-05` also in other time zones, the leading-zero column of `excel/formats.xlsx` named with `00123`, each `header_row`, hidden rows and columns imported like any other, the three impostors that can be read, the four files that are refused leaving nothing behind); workbooks under other names; numbers of 1e19 and 6.02e23 stored as they are; the zip check in the browser's own `DecompressionStream` (a changed byte in a part's data is refused as damaged, the library is never asked to read it nor given the workbook that holds nothing, and the same file unchanged is read once and then followed by that small workbook once: the wrapper `countReads`; every .xlsx of the fixtures passes). **`aw5-excel-dialog`** (page; workbooks made in the test): a strip of sheets that is wider than the dialog at 320 px, what a tab and a panel say (the hidden words, a name of only spaces, a sheet that cannot be ticked staying unticked under click, Space and Enter), the two strips (ids, pointers, one selected tab and one stop of the Tab key in each, the arrow keys, Home, End), one sheet and a web page, the sheet on show, a panel built when first shown (a workbook of 300 sheets) with what the sheet is set to kept, the count line (written only when its words change), the ticks and the two buttons, hidden and very hidden sheets, a sheet that cannot be ticked of each kind, the size line, the names across sheets, files and the open database, the row of names, the preview, what holds Import up and the line that says so, the new database's name, keys and the focus, what a screen reader is told, markup and hostile names, axe in both themes at 1366 and 390 px, narrow windows, nothing of the sheets in the DOM until a workbook has been read, the layout of a sheet's panel in a wide window lower than 1080 px (by where things are: beside at 1366x768, 1024x768, 1920x950 and 900x900, stacked at 1920x1080 and 899 px, the two sides of the bound showing the same rows, the same Tab order in both) and a field's message in view. **`aw5-excel-limits`** (page): the question for a large workbook and its two answers with every limit read from `config-workflow.js` and tried at its edge (an .xls by its size, a zip by what its directory says it unpacks to; a picture does not count; the rounding up), the files made in the test that are refused each with its own sentence and nothing left behind (a zip cut off, a text file, a word-processor document, an OpenDocument spreadsheet, a web page with no table, a workbook of chart sheets only, a library that throws a string or a TypeError or a word about a password, an .xls that is not one, a workbook changed after it was chosen), the limits being for each file, text files keeping their own limits, and a last group for a workbook that the browser has no memory left for (section 9.8 says how it is made). **`aw5-excel-page`** (page): the typed table against the engine (numbers that the engine's own binding would wrap, 1e19, 6.02e23, a short row, a name that needs quotes, `abandon()`, the import's savepoint), the report of a sheet (the paragraph of empty formulas, the note of a padded column, both in one column, a table with neither as it was), the run of a sheet with a job made by hand (the tables, the events, the progress, Cancel between steps, a failure that undoes the tables before it, beside text files, into the open database with the student's transaction open, Papa asked for only for a job that has a text file), a workbook beside CSV files in one database, the flags (`excel-import` named without `import`, as `flagState("excel-import")` names it, does nothing; the import alone never asks for the library), the library asked for once and only when a workbook is first read, a library that cannot be loaded (a 404, a file that is not the library, no answer in time) with the CSV file beside it imported, one workbook read at a time, leaving while a workbook is read (Cancel, Escape and a second choice at the library's way, during the zip check and at the frame before the library's call; the library never called for the reading that was left), the busy state while sheets are imported, Back and Forward, drops, the page after an import, sheet names that are not plain text in the event and the report and an evidence file that verifies, a browser without `scheduler.yield`, and a phone. The screenshot states `import-workbook`, `import-workbook-sheet`, `import-workbook-warning` and `import-report-workbook` (`tests/ui-states.js`; the last holds the end of a report with the paragraph of empty formulas, and the note of a padded column is held by a page test, since the two are 950 px apart) are 32 pictures, compared by `ui-visual` and read by axe in `ui-a11y`, and the older state `import-report` waits for its toast to come and to go. **Holding a moment**: the page's own steps are held by a route for the library, the page's clock, the kit's `slowDown` and `gateTurns`, and the three wrappers of the library's read tell a chosen file's read from the read of the workbook that holds nothing (section 9.8). Where a test waits for a text not to be there, the text is read from `config-workflow.js`. The three texts of the wording gaps are typed out here too: `importOtherSpreadsheetReason` (both sentences, for the three endings), `importedWithLeftMessage` (none, one and three files), `importReportLeftText` and `importReportLeftParts` (the three words a tab can say, a name with a comma, the names marked), a name with a right-to-left override in its own element with the parts drawn from left to right, and `importNameMadeUniqueNote` with `importNameMadeUniqueTemporaryNote`. **`aw5-excel-page`** also holds the three gaps with both flags: the three endings (the reason of `importOtherSpreadsheetReason(ending, true)`, a real workbook under `.xlsm` refused without a request for the workbook library, a mixed choice with `IMPORT_MIXED_WITH_EXCEL_MESSAGE`), the files that gave no table (a CSV file and a workbook with one sheet ticked beside a file of random bytes, a large workbook left out, a workbook with every sheet unticked, one with no sheet that can be ticked and a `.ods`: five files named in the tabs' order, the events without them, and a workbook that gives a table not named for its unticked sheets; `run` called with a job that has no `left` is as it was), and **`aw5-excel-dialog`** the note under a sheet's name (the same workbook imported twice: the second sheet's panel is built only when it is first shown and has its note then; a sheet that cannot be ticked has none; a sheet whose name only a temporary table has gets the sentence for a temporary name, beside a sheet with the database's; the destination changed before a sheet was shown; the layout in the two columns of a wide and low window, stacked, and on a phone; axe). The page as it ships has two tests in `aw5-excel-page`: a first visit asks for neither library and stores nothing and is the visit with both imports and the export on, and a chosen workbook is read with the library and its sheets are imported as tables. |
| `ui-visual` | Screenshot baselines (below). |

**Screenshot baselines** (`tests/ui-visual.spec.js`, the UI plan's section 8). Each state of `RECIPES.shell.states` (`tests/ui-states.js`) is compared with a baseline PNG at each size of the matrix (1366x768, 1920x1080, 1024x768 and 390x844) in both themes, with reduced motion; what changes from run to run (`.status-time`, `.run-history-time`, and the origin of the link in Copy link's popover, whose port `TEST_PORT_BASE` sets: it becomes `http://127.0.0.1:4173`, the same length for any four-digit port) is given one value before the shot and nothing is masked; the `menu-exercise-set` recipe routes the folder listing of `exercises/` to the one set it opens, so that a set added to the site does not redraw its pictures; the tolerance is `maxDiffPixels: 40` (a restyled line of text changes hundreds). The file runs in `chromium-root` only (`playwright.config.js` gives the other three projects a `testIgnore`): one engine is enough to catch a layout that moved, and fonts render differently from one engine, and one platform, to the next. The baselines are Playwright's default snapshots, in `tests/ui-visual.spec.js-snapshots/`, named `<state>--<width>x<height>--<theme>-chromium-root-<platform>.png` (the matrix's names in `docs/ui/`, plus Playwright's suffix); they are committed, and a run on another platform than `linux` has none to compare with until it writes its own. A change to the look made on purpose updates them with

```
npx playwright test tests/ui-visual.spec.js --project=chromium-root --update-snapshots
```

and the before/after images belong in the same pull request (`docs/ui/after/` holds the plan's matrices, made with `tools/ui-matrix.mjs`).

Spec file names carry the milestone they were written in; they are not a statement of what is current.

## 12. localStorage

Complete list. `js/`, `index.html`, `about.html` and `verify.html` store nothing in `sessionStorage`, IndexedDB or a cookie (`js/lab/storage-guard.js` only reads the `window.localStorage` and `window.sessionStorage` properties and, where reading one throws, redefines it as undefined; it never reads or writes an item), and the student number of the evidence export is in none of them (section 9.3). The engine opens `:memory:`, so SQLite persists nothing (the build also registers `kvvfs`, a localStorage-backed VFS, which nothing opens).

| Key | Value | Written by | Removed by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sqlime.query.<database name>` (`storage.js`; the upstream name is kept so saved queries survive) | The last SQL run for that database name (the file basename, or `new.db`; two databases with one basename share a slot). | `execute` (every run with persist on, the trimmed text that was run); `changeName` (the rename in the export dialog) moves what is saved under the old name to the new one without writing `database.query`, so renaming never saves SQL that was not run. | `changeName` (old name); `set` with empty SQL calls `remove` and then still writes `""`. With storage unusable, writes land in memory. |
| `sqllab.progress.<set id>` (`PROGRESS_PREFIX`) | `{v: 1, passed: {<exercise id>: true}, drafts: {<exercise id>: "<sql>"}}` | `markPassed`, `saveDraft` (section 7.3) | `resetSet` |
| `sqllab.log.<log key>` (`RUN_LOG_PREFIX`) | `{v: 1, entries: [{t, sql, rows} or {t, sql, error}]}`, at most 200, oldest first; the log key is the dataset id or `file:<database name>` | `appendLog` on every Run | nothing; the cap drops the oldest |
| `sqllab.events.<log key>` (`EVENTS_PREFIX`, in `config-workflow.js`) | `{v: 1, events: [{t, kind, after, lines}]}`, at most 1,000 and 1 MB of line text, oldest first; the log key is the run log's | `recordEvent` (section 9.5), which only the import (section 9.6: one event for each file imported, a workbook's sheets being the lines of its event, 9.8) and the table export (section 9.7: one for each file saved) call, so a page with neither the import nor the export switched on (`?preview=-import,-export`) never writes the key; `about.html`'s list of what is remembered names it | nothing (the plan's Phase 1 moves it) |
| `sqllab.theme` (`THEME_STORAGE_KEY`) | `"light"` or `"dark"`; no key means match the device | `setThemeChoice` (the Theme menu); read by `theme-boot.js` before the first paint | `setThemeChoice("device")` |
| `sqllab.textSize` (`TEXT_SIZE_STORAGE_KEY`) | The size of the SQL and the results in px, one of `TEXT_SIZES` (12 to 24, even); anything else is ignored for 14 | `mountTextSize` (A− and A+); read by `theme-boot.js` before the first paint | nothing |
| `sqllab.layout` (`LAYOUT_STORAGE_KEY`) | `{v: 1, sidebar, collapsed, editor}`: the sidebar's width in px (220 to 420), whether it is hidden, and the editor pane's share (its toolbar and the editor) of the room under the exercise prompt (a fraction, 0.05 to 0.95 exclusive); a value outside those ranges, or JSON of another shape, is ignored for the default | `mountLayout` (after a key on a splitter, at the end of a drag, on a double click on the sidebar's splitter, and when a set opening brings a hidden sidebar back) | nothing |

The `sqllab.*` prefixes are storage namespaces, not branding: they deliberately do not follow `APP_NAME`, so renaming the app does not orphan saved progress or logs (`m2-brand.spec.js` lists them as the only other places the old spelling may appear). Upstream also wrote `github.username` and `github.token`; nothing reads or writes them now. New keys use the `sqllab.*` prefix. Spec constraint 4: localStorage only for history and progress, no cookies, no analytics; the UI plan adds display preferences on top (the theme, the layout and the text size), never anything a student wrote.

## 13. Engine and platform facts

- **Engine.** The official SQLite WASM build (`jswasm` `sqlite3.js` + `sqlite3.wasm`, `oo1` API), 3.54.1, not sqlean: no extensions beyond what the stock build compiles in. Raw 439,732 B + 879,430 B, about 1.3 MB of the 3 MB budget (the editor's CodeMirror bundle adds about 0.4 MB, the typefaces 0.17 MB). Loaded as a classic script on the main thread, then `sqlite3InitModule(CONFIG)` in `manager.js`; the `.wasm` is fetched relative to the script, always: `vendor/sqlite3.js` would take another place from the query parameters `sqlite3.dir` and `sqlite3.wasm`, so `js/lab/engine-guard.js` removes every `sqlite3.*` parameter from the address before the engine script runs (a crafted link cannot make the page fetch and run the engine from another host). No Web Worker.
- **No COOP/COEP dependency.** Nothing uses `SharedArrayBuffer`, `Atomics`, OPFS, `crossOriginIsolated` or `Worker`. Without special headers `crossOriginIsolated` is false, `SharedArrayBuffer` is undefined, `sqlite3.opfs` is undefined and the app works (`smoke.spec.js`). The working database lives in memory; saving is the export download.
- `sqlite3InitModule` is **single-use**: a second call throws. `manager.js` caches the result and keeps the API object private; new code must not boot a second engine. Get connections from `manager.init(name, path)`.
- `database.db.constructor` is not `sqlite3.oo1.DB`; build connections through `manager.init`, and use `database.capi` / `database.db` (not `database`'s module-private variable) from lab code.
- `crypto.subtle` needs a secure origin (https or localhost). Evidence export and `verify.html` say so instead of failing silently.

## 14. Untrusted text

Everything from a database, file, file name, URL, share link, manifest, exercise file or error is untrusted. Build DOM with `createElement` and `textContent` (or `dataset`), or pass the string through `escapeHtml` before any `innerHTML` (`sqlime-result`'s unused `print` writes innerHTML through `printer.js`, which escapes; the results pane, the toasts and the dialogs use `textContent` only). Names are put in SQL only through `js/lab/sql.js` (identifiers quoted, quotes doubled; replacement is by function so `$&` is not a pattern). `SQLite.getTableInfo` still has a raw splice; nothing calls it. File names reach the page only through `textContent` (the switcher's button, the status bar), the export dialog's input `value`, `document.title` and storage keys. A workbook brings three more (section 9.8): a sheet's name, a cell's value (a text, an error's text, a date's text) and a number format. A sheet's name reaches the page only through `textContent` (a tab, the line "Sheet <name>") and `title` (the whole name of a tab), the table's name only through `sanitiseName`, and the report's source line and the event's `table:` line through `textContent` and `recordEvent` (which makes each line one line and cuts it): a name with a line break, a tab, markup, another script or the name `constructor` or `__proto__` is held by tests. A cell's value is shown in the preview and the report by `textContent`, a long one cut at 80 characters with the whole in its `title`. A number format is read only by the app's own tests and the library's formatter, and is shown only as the text Excel showed for a number (`cell.w`, cut as a value is, in the report's note); the library's own words are never shown. Tests: `m2-injection.spec.js`, `m2-table-names.spec.js`, `m3-schema.spec.js` (hostile identifiers and manifest), `m4-panel.spec.js` (hostile set content), `m5-verify-page.spec.js`, `m5-evidence-dialog.spec.js`, and, for a workbook, `aw5-excel-dialog.spec.js` and `aw5-excel-page.spec.js`.

## 15. Extending

The app's page runs `bundle/`, so a change below to `js/` or `css/` reaches it only after `npm run build:app`; commit `bundle/` with it (section 1).

- **A feature module.** Put it in `js/lab/`, export a `mount...` function, add a one- or two-line seam in `js/index.js` that calls it, and follow the page through `lab:database-loaded` / `lab:run-finished` (and `lab:database-changed`, section 2, for something that shows the tables). Put texts and constants in `config.js` (a feature of the assessment plan: in `config-workflow.js`, see the bullet on it below). Do not edit `js/sqlite/`; the one line in `create()` of `manager.js` that gives the wrapper `database.wasm` is the exception that has been made, because the engine's memory is private to that file and the table export has no other way to read a text as its bytes (9.7). A test that needs it in the page takes it from `bundle/app.js` (add it to `EXPOSE` in `tools/build-app.mjs` and to the list of names in `tests/app-bundle.spec.js`).
- **A button in the editor toolbar.** Append a `<button class="button">` to `#editor-toolbar` (insert it after a known sibling, as Check answer goes right after `#execute`; one filled `button--primary` per context). Buttons using `data-action` get `ActionController`, which listens on `.topbar` and `#editor-toolbar` (handlers return a Promise and the button is disabled until it settles); otherwise attach a plain listener. The toolbar wraps, so check it at 360px.
- **A top bar control.** Append to `.topbar-actions` (a menu: the next item); the bar wraps at every width. A sidebar tab: a `button[role=tab]` with `aria-controls` in `.tabs` and a `div.tab-panel[role=tabpanel][hidden]` after it in `nav#sidebar`; `mountTabs` takes the tabs from the tablist, so no JavaScript changes, and the panel's stylesheet goes in `STYLES` (`tools/build-app.mjs`) and `CASCADE` (`tests/app-bundle.spec.js`), at its region's place in the cascade.
- **A panel.** A new row of the main area needs a grid area in `css/shell.css` (`.main` has prompt, toolbar, editor, splitter and results; `layout.js` observes every child of `main#main` except the editor pane, the results pane and the splitter, so a new row reflows the editor's height), with its own stylesheet in `STYLES` (`tools/build-app.mjs`) and `CASCADE` (`tests/app-bundle.spec.js`), at its region's place in the cascade, not as a `<link>` in `index.html`.
- **A shortcut.** A second `new ShortcutController({...}).listen(document)`, or a capture-phase `keydown` as the Check shortcut does. Ctrl/Cmd+O, U and Enter (and Shift+Enter while a set is open) are taken, and in the editor so are `/` (comment), Space with Ctrl (completion), Z and Y (undo, redo) and the rest of CodeMirror's default keymap; avoid `s` (browsers bind it to Save). A key for the editor alone goes in `code-editor.js` (a keymap entry), not on the document.
- **Changing the editor.** `index.js` and the lab modules touch the editor only through the surface of 4.5 (`value`, `query`, `focus()`, `setSchema`, `view`/`code` in `caret.js` and `insert.js`, the `input` / `execute` events), plus the id `#editor`. A CodeMirror extension goes in `extensions()` of `code-editor.js`; a CodeMirror piece not yet bundled is added to `tools/editor-entry.js`, then `npm run build:editor`. Keep "selection else all" for `.query` (selections outside the editor ignored), the Escape-then-Tab way out, the `aria-describedby="editor-hint"` link (`m2-editor-keys.spec.js`, `ui3-editor.spec.js`) and the plain box working on its own.
- **A dataset or exercise set.** Section 6.3 and 7; the README has the instructor's version.
- **A storage key.** Section 12; own module, own `sqllab.*` prefix, every access in try/catch, memory fallback.
- **A feature of the assessment plan.** Behind a flag: add its name to `PREVIEW_FLAGS` in `js/lab/config-workflow.js` (false), with an entry in `PREVIEW_FLAG_NEEDS` if it does nothing without another, and ask `flagOn("name")` (`flags.js`) where the feature starts; while it is built it is `false` and, with the flag off, the page must be what it was (the tests of that state open the page with `flagState()`, every flag named and off, section 11); `true` switches it on for everyone, and from then on a test of the feature's own names its state in full with `flagState("name")`, and a test of the page as it ships opens the plain address. The address's minus (`?preview=-name`) keeps the page without the feature reachable. Switching a feature on for everyone also means bringing up to date the texts that describe the site (About, the README, the evidence dialog's line and the Export menu's line for the evidence): the plan's section 8 lists the places the import and the export touched. Three tests hold lists that such a phase changes on purpose: `app-bundle` (the modules a test can reach), `m2-brand` (every line of source with the app's name or `sqllab`) and `aw0-flags` (the flags' names at its top; and its group "nothing a student can see", which compares the page with every flag that nothing reads yet, `UNREAD_FLAGS`, against the page with every flag off (`flagState()`): a phase that reads a flag takes it out of that list and adds a test of what the flag alone adds to a page on which nothing was done, as the import, the export and the import of workbooks did; the tests of storage and of the address keep all four flags). Its texts and limits go in `config-workflow.js`, never `config.js` (the verify page's size). A library: the publisher's minified file, unchanged, in `vendor/` with its licence beside it, loaded with `loadVendor("file.min.js", "Global")` (`vendor-loader.js`) where it is first needed, with the rejection caught and told to the student in plain words; never a `<script>` in `index.html` and never an import (the build refuses it), so a first visit does not fetch it (`smoke`, whose list of requests would show it, `aw0-vendor-loader`, `aw0-flags` and `aw2-import-page`; `app-bundle` holds the half about `index.html`). Something for the evidence file: `recordEvent(runLog.current().key, {kind, lines})` (`events.js`, section 9.5; `runLog` is what `mountRunLog` returned in `index.js`, handed to the module as it is to `mountEvidence`), one event per file, a line per table, what matters first in each line. The key is the run log's own for the open database, fixed when the database loaded: for an opened file or a database from a URL, `logKeyFor(dataset, database.name)` worked out again gives another key once the database has been renamed (the export dialog), and the events would then sit apart from their queries (a course dataset's key is its id, which a rename does not change). The key is `null` until the first database has loaded, and `recordEvent` then returns null and records nothing; while a database is loading it is still the key of the one before. So an import that opens a new database records its event after `lab:database-loaded` for that database.
  - **What Phase 3 shows about a second long job, a second library and a second dialog** (section 9.7 has the facts; this is what to copy and what to do better). *A second long job.* The state of the long jobs is the page's, not a job's: `index.js` declares `runningJob`, `jobMessage` and `addressAtJob` before every mount and each job's `onBusy` is `onJobBusy(kind, message)`; `otherJobMessage(kind)` gives the message of any running job that is not `kind`, so a third job takes `...longJob("name", MESSAGE)` (its `onBusy` and its `otherJobMessage`) and the first two already refuse during it (their `take()`, `open()` and `run()` check `otherJobMessage` twice, once to open and once to start), and what is refused meanwhile (Run, Check, the database export, the switcher, Ctrl+O and Ctrl+U, a set, a file, Back and Forward) is refused through `jobRunning()`, `refusedWhileBusy()` and `jobInTheWay()` with the running job's own words. The new job's dialog needs nothing to keep Ctrl+O and Ctrl+U shut, as long as it is a `<dialog>` shown with `showModal()`: `jobInTheWay()` asks the page for `dialog[open]`. A job needs its own busy message, a second lock in `run`, a `finally` that closes whatever is open (a reader, a statement) and clears the busy state even when closing fails, Cancel looked at after every piece and before anything is saved, and an event recorded only after the file has been handed to the browser. *A second library.* Vendor the publisher's file and its licence, call `loadVendor` inside `run` when the plan of the output needs it (never on mount, never on open), race it against Cancel (`loadZipTool` in `export-tables.js`: a look every 100 ms) and tell a failure in plain words with the ticks kept; no build or deploy change; `aw0-flags` (no request for either library from the flag alone) and `aw0-vendor-loader` hold the early fetch, the new spec holds that the library is asked for once and only when needed. *A second dialog.* It copied the first's shape (a body that scrolls, a foot that does not, a session counter that drops late answers, `lab:database-loaded` closing it when idle) and none of its classes: its own `css/export-tables.css` in `STYLES` and `CASCADE`, fixed ids (a test that mounts a second must take the first out), `aria-disabled` and a line that says why where the import's Export button is `disabled`. What is shared now: `nextFrame()` is `import-pace.js`'s own, used by both jobs, `swallowHeldEnter()` (`held-enter.js`) is called by both dialogs when they close, and `aw2-import-page.spec.js` takes the ways of holding a job from `tests/helpers.js`. Where the two jobs still differ, a later phase should share: the import waits for its library with no way to cancel (`loadReader()` in `import.js`) while the export races the same wait against a look at Cancel (`loadZipTool` in `export-tables.js`, which uses nothing of its closure and could move to `vendor-loader.js`), and the import of workbooks (Phase 5, 9.8) waits for its library, SheetJS, inside `readWorkbook` with no look at Cancel meanwhile either: the dialog can be closed at any moment, the reading then ends at its first look at `current()` when the library arrives (or when the loader gives up after `VENDOR_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS`) and nothing of it shows (held by `aw5-excel-page`, which makes the library arrive late and not at all).
  - **What Phase 5 shows about a step that cannot be interrupted, a library that keeps state and a flag inside a dialog** (section 9.8 has the facts). *A step that cannot be interrupted* (the library's one call): first refuse what can be refused before it (the limits by what the sheets unpack to, the file's own checksums: `import-zip.js`), paint before it (`await frame()`), and watch it from outside, since no catch is sure to finish when the browser has no memory: a timer set before the step, taken away only with its outcome in hand, never in a `finally` and never across a wait (`watchCall`, `watchStep`), and a telling that lets go what fills the memory, asks for room, and only then speaks. *A library that keeps state*: read what it holds after a call (SheetJS keeps every text of the last .xlsx and makes its table of number formats anew at each read) and measure it in a real browser before trusting a read to have let go; hand the pure modules the library as a parameter, so that Node can load them (`import-cells.js` never imports it). *A flag inside a dialog*: the dialog is handed `excel`, null with the flag off, and every new branch tests it, so the page with the flag off is the page it was (`aw0-flags` compares the two deeply); a text that differs under the flag is a new constant beside the old one, which stays for the old flag. *A test that needs a state no machine here can reach* (a browser with no memory left) makes the engine's words, and the refusal of the room that the page asks for, by hand, and counts what the page asks for (9.8, "Holding a workbook's import in a test").
- **A colour.** Only in `css/tokens.css`: the light value on `:root` and the dark one in both dark blocks; add any new text-on-surface pairing to `PAIRS` in `tests/ui1-tokens.spec.js`.
- **An icon.** `icon("name")` with the literal Lucide name, then `node tools/build-icons.mjs` (after `npm ci`) to rewrite the sprite in `index.html`.
- **A menu.** A `<button>` and, right after it, an element with an id, `role="menu"` and items (`role="menuitem"` / `"menuitemradio"`, `tabindex="-1"`), then `mountMenu(button, menu, {onSelect})` (`js/lab/menu.js`; `theme.js` is the example). Styles: `.menu`, `.menu-item`, `.menu-separator` in `css/menu.css`.
- **Branding.** The app name only through `config.js`: `data-app-name` in HTML, `APP_NAME` or `titleFor` in JS. A test fails if the name is written anywhere else in shipped HTML, CSS, JS or SVG.

## 16. Decisions, known quirks, not built

**Not built, on purpose.** Spec F9 (hidden-answer exercises storing a result fingerprint instead of the model query) and F10 (printable worksheet) are P2 and belong to no milestone; the model query is readable in the fetched JSON. (F8, CodeMirror with highlighting and autocomplete, came with the UI plan's Phase 3, section 4.5.) Out of scope per the spec: any backend or accounts, grade export or LMS integration, DML/DDL exercises, engines other than SQLite, AI query help, saving databases anywhere but the user's own file download. Not built for the import of workbooks (9.8): a Worker for the reading (the plan's section 10 settles on none, so a damaged .xls can freeze the page for good), `.xlsm`, `.xlsb` and `.ods` among the names the import reads (a file with one of those endings is refused in its tab by its name, 9.6, so a SQLite database called `x.ods` cannot be opened by choosing it while `import` is on, which it is unless the address says `-import`; under an .xlsx name an .ods is refused and the other two are read), and any change to the size limits before the owner has timed a lab PC.

**Decisions** (each is explained where it appears): restored SQL never runs, and since the UI plan's Phase 4 nothing runs on load at all (4.1); a Run waits one frame so "Running…" can show during a long one (4.2); toasts answer actions, bands hold problems that must stay (8); the model query is private to the checker; per-exercise saved SQL lives in the progress record (7.3); exercise sets are discovered through a deploy-time folder listing (7.1); student SQL runs under the guard in `runner.js` (5); the run log is per dataset and its key is fixed when the database opens (9.1); the student number goes only into the file, and the verify page ignores `STUDENT_ID_PATTERN` and the app name so older files verify (9.2); CSV cells are not escaped and a BOM is added only for non-ASCII (8); a workbook is read in one call that cannot be interrupted, with the page frozen meanwhile, after its own zip records have been checked, and a failure for want of memory is told by a watch from outside the step, never by a catch (9.8).

**Known quirks and dead code.** `DatabasePath.toHash`, `SQLite.getTableInfo`, `SQLite.execute`, `MESSAGES` (`js/sqlite/db.js`: the old status texts), `tableInfoSql` (`js/lab/sql.js`), `QUERIES.tableContent` (`js/sqlite/db.js`: the old table preview's query), `print` and `printTables` (`sqlime-result.js`, `printer.js`: the result table is `js/lab/result-table.js` since Phase 4, and `printTables`' buttons carry `data-action="showTable"`, which nothing handles), and the editor's `start` event (nothing listens) are unused. A Run in exercise mode writes the free-query slot (7.3). `execute` has no timeout, so a runaway query hangs the tab (as upstream). The reading of a damaged .xls can do the same for good (9.8).
